RDNA3-Hackintosh / docs / Agent playbook: acting on hardware-test reports

Agent playbook: acting on hardware-test reports

The agent playbook for acting on hardware-test reports: intake, the fix loop, the decision tables (symptom, likely cause, action) per step, and the findings per card run. Written for agents, not for the owner.

Contents

Audience: an AI coding agent continuing this project after the user ran the hardware tests. Follow it literally; it exists so that no planning round is needed. The user is not the reader of this file.

You cannot touch the hardware. The user runs tests on their machine and sends reports; you diagnose, fix, verify in the simulator and CI, and tell the user exactly what to run next. One round = one report batch → one fix → one retest.

0. Context you need (read once per session)

  • Project: macOS (Hackintosh) driver for AMD RX 7800 XT (Navi 32, PCI 1002:747E), ported from Linux amdgpu (MIT). Branch stage1-kext of voten641/rdna3-hackintosh. Do not open a PR unless asked.
  • Nothing had run on real hardware before these tests. Everything was verified only in the simulator tests/sim_navi32.c and in CI builds.
  • Test procedures the user follows: docs/TESTING-VM.html (Linux host, macOS VM with the card passed through, driven by tools/vm/rdna3vm) and docs/TESTING-HW.html (bare-metal hackintosh). Ladder steps: 0 1 2 3 4 5 5b 6 6b 7 8 9 10; boot-args per step are in step_args() in tools/vm/rdna3vm.
  • Kext layout: kext/src/RDNA3Accel.cpp (levels 0–3: PCI, BAR5, IFWI wait, VRAM size, discovery), RDNA3Display.cpp (VBIOS paths, AUX/EDID, DMCUB glue), RDNA3Drm.cpp (amdgpu UAPI), RDNA3Bringup.cpp (hwInit(): levels 4–8 = PSP, SMU/PM, DMCUB, GMC, IH, SDMA, GFX/KIQ/KCQ; recovery). Hardware logic is portable C in hw/ (one file per IP block, register headers generated in hw/gen/ by tools/gen_regs.py).
  • Where amdgpu lives: fetch the exact Linux file you compare against into your scratchpad, e.g. curl -sfL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/gpu/drm/amd/amdgpu/psp_v13_0.c. Register headers: drivers/gpu/drm/amd/include/asic_reg/....

Hard rules

  1. Language: talk to the user in Polish. Everything committed to the repo (code, comments, docs, commit messages) is English only.
  2. CI minutes are scarce (user's explicit instruction).
    • Every commit message carries [skip ci].
    • Verify with one manual run per batch, always with inputs: {"full": "false"} — the workflow input defaults to true, so never dispatch without it. Never run full: true (Metal + RADV) without asking the user first.
    • A push of metal/** without [skip ci] starts the expensive Metal job.
  3. Port, don't invent. Each hardware change follows a named amdgpu function; name it in a comment. NullMoth-derived code stays AMD-only.
  4. Simulator first. Every hardware fix must be reproduced in tests/sim_navi32.c (make the model behave like the card in the report) and pass make test before it is pushed.
  5. One step per round. Fix the first failing ladder step only; later failures are often consequences.
  6. Commit trailers: follow the session's attribution instructions. Never put a model identifier in the repo.
  7. Never commit reports (reports/ is git-ignored: EDID, serials).

Tools and state outside the repo

  • Notion kanban: data source collection://2dad47f8-8a55-4222-96e5-39b401eb72ed (title property Zadanie; Status: 📋 Do zrobienia, 🔨 W toku, ✅ Zrobione (CI), 🧪 Przetestowane (symulator), 🟢 Działa na sprzęcie, 🔴 Zepsute / zablokowane; Etap: 0 Stanowisko/CI … 8 Pakowanie; Priorytet: Wysoki/Średni/Niski; text Pliki, Notatki). Status page with per-stage percentages: 3f4560305d0d81058363de48e7e2efbd. The board is in Polish (it is the user's, not the repo).
  • CI: .github/workflows/build.yml. Artifact RDNA3Accel-kext (kexts, rdna3_drminfo, libdrm.dylib) is what the user installs.
  • Local tests: make test RDNA3_FW_DIR=<dir>; fetch the firmware into the scratchpad first with the same loop as the host-tests job in build.yml (linux-firmware amdgpu/*.bin).

1. Intake (start of every round)

  1. Collect the user's files into reports/<yyyy-mm-dd>/ (usually one rdna3-result.tar.xz (.tar.gz when the owner's host has no xz) from rdna3vm result: tar xf it there (the looks are raw PPMs in the .xz, PNGs in the .gz; the triage reads both); its 00-triage.txt is the host's triage, 00-host.txt the commit and CI run). Expected: rdna3-report-*.tar.gz, *-tests.txt, serial-*.log, host-*.log, (bootwatch-*.txt when WindowServer never opened the GPU: see "WindowServer never opened the GPU" below) and once linux-reference/. If something needed is missing, ask for those exact files (in Polish) and stop.
  2. Run python3 tools/triage_reports.py reports/<date> --all. A FAIL STALE PLUGIN/RADV line means the VM ran an old Metal plugin or RADV: see its row in the failure table below before reading any Metal result. A FAIL STALE KEXTS line (also without any macOS report) means the run tested the OLD driver: the serial log's RDNA3Accelerator: build <hash> (or RDNA3-fb / RDNA3-agdc) differs from the commit tested, or an approve-*.txt came with the result (card runs 2026-10-18b, 2026-10-19b: the new build was installed but never allowed, macOS ran e35e78c275). Ask for rdna3vm approve (the next rdna3vm step also offers it by itself) and the step again before analysing anything else. A stale: <kext>: running X, installed Y line in a report's tests file gives the same FAIL by itself. 00-host.txt carries run-note: lines (the host's notes of the run: "tested the OLD driver", or "Allow was not clicked / did not take" when rdna3vm approve ran before and macOS still ran the old build); the queue keeps its marker as approve-seen-*.txt. An INFO stale check line means the check could not be done (no build line in the newest serial log, a source hash not computable, an unreadable marker). From step 2 on each report comes with <report>-scanout-pipe<N>.ppm: what that display pipe really scanned out ~2 min after boot (rdna3_drminfo --scanout, HUBP read back + a 320-wide picture). Triage prints the per-pipe state ("screens") and writes the PNGs next to them: look at them (the user is often not at the screen) before asking what the monitors showed. The looks are 320 wide; one look per report is hi-res (--scanout-hires: <report>-scanout3h-pipe<N> in a normal run, -scanoutplay5h- / -scanoutplay10h- in the play step, m6* in M6) for small text-cell rectangles and broken glyphs. Since 2026-10-26 it is the surface's NATIVE size, every pixel (1920x1080 for a 1080p panel, 6.2 MB as PPM; DRIVER-PLAN N56). A kext from before that gives a 1280x720 nearest-neighbour sample (2 of 3 pixels: thin strokes can vanish or double) and the look's output says native look: NOT available, the kext predates it: then do not judge single-pixel detail from it, rdna3vm update (+ approve) first. Exit 1 = a check failed. The first FAIL (in ladder order) is your target.
  3. From step 2 on, check discovery once: 
    make tools
mkdir -p /tmp/rep && tar -xzf reports/<date>/rdna3-report-*lvl-2*.tar.gz -C /tmp/rep
build/host/amd_discovery_dump /tmp/rep/*/discovery.bin > /tmp/mac.txt
build/host/amd_discovery_dump reports/<date>/linux-reference/discovery.bin > /tmp/linux.txt
diff /tmp/linux.txt /tmp/mac.txt    # must be empty, else fix step 2 first
  4. For every step that passed: add <step> <date> <vm|metal> to docs/status/hw-verified.txt and run python3 tools/progress.py (the README map turns those blocks green). A step that fails again later is removed from that file. A big step (big3, big4) runs many steps' features in one boot: judge each feature's tests in its report by that step's table in §3, and every step whose checks all pass goes in on its own line with the note (big4). A failing feature is bisected with its own step (rdna3vm step <name>).
  5. Notion: create a card HW test <date> (Etap 0 Stanowisko/CI) with the PASS/FAIL list; move cards of verified parts to 🟢 Działa na sprzęcie, the failing part to 🔴 Zepsute / zablokowane.
  6. Go to the failing step in §3.

2. Fix loop (every failure)

  1. Evidence. RDNA3,Stage2, the *Result value (negative errno: -5 EIO, -12 ENOMEM, -16 EBUSY, -19 ENODEV, -22 EINVAL, -62 ETIME), the first ERROR line with its register values, the last serial lines.
  2. Compare with amdgpu. Find the amdgpu function named in the comment at the failing spot (git grep -n), fetch it, diff the register sequence: offsets (hw/gen/*.h vs asic_reg), order, delays, polled bits.
  3. Evidence too thin → diagnostic build, not a guess. Add RLOG / publish("RDNA3,…") for the registers that separate the hypotheses, say "diagnostic build" in the commit message, and ask for that one step only.
  4. Reproduce in the simulator, watch it fail, fix, make test green.
  5. Ship: commit [skip ci], push, one CI run full: "false", wait for green. Fix and repeat if red (never hand the user a red build).
  6. Tell the user (Polish) with the template in §5.
  7. Record: Notion cards and the status page percentages, docs/ROADMAP.html, and add the new failure mode to §3 of this file.

3. Decision tables: symptom → likely cause → action

Hypotheses are ordered by likelihood. "VM:" = only possible with passthrough (bare metal boots the card cold from its GOP).

Step 0 (rdna3lvl=0): kext loads, touches nothing

SymptomLikely causeAction
no RDNA3 log lines, 03-kexts.txt without RDNA3AccelVM: no AMD device in 04-pci.txt (triage prints the AMD ids)the card must sit on pcie.0 (GPU_BUS=pcie0, default): behind a pcie-root-port macOS leaves it unconfigured. Check host-*.log "guest PCI bus": BAR0 mapped at 40 s
same, AMD device presentnot loaded: kernelmanagerd refuses ad-hoc kexts in the Auxiliary KC ("invalid codeful kernel extension")the VM injects the kexts with OpenCore (rdna3vm prepare, EFI/OC/Kexts); serial log must show "a prelinked copy … is already present" for the /Library/Extensions copy
loaded, not matchedIOPCIMatch / topology04-pci.txt; fix matching in kext/Info.plist
hang / black screen at step 0VM firmware, not the driverask for rdna3vm step off. If that hangs too: VM setup — re-dump VBIOS with amdgpu loaded, Resizable BAR off, larger X-PciMmio64Mb (PCI_MMIO64_MB), CPU_MODEL=Penryn,… in ~/rdna3-vm/config; a bigger BAR0 (RESIZE_BAR_LOG2 above 8) is the first thing to set back to 8

Step 1 (rdna3lvl=1): BAR5, IFWI wait, VRAM size

SymptomLikely causeAction
device not in D0, refusing to touch MMIOVM: card left in D3hothost: add vfio-pci disable_idle_d3=1 handling in rdna3vm; kext: bring the device to D0 (pci_set_power_state(D0) equivalent) in RDNA3Accel::readConfigSpace
MMIO reads all-onesBAR5 not decoded / wrong BAR / in resetlog BAR5 (MMIO) raw …, memory decode …; compare with linux-reference/lspci.txt Region 5; enable memory decode in mapMmio()
RCC_CONFIG_MEMSIZE = 0 … not posted, C2PMSG33 0card not posted (VM: GOP ROM not run)check romfile in host-*.log; port amdgpu_device_asic_init for GC ≥ 11 (psp13_wait_for_bootloader, then atom_asic_init from hw/atom.c) and call it from readVramSize() when MEMSIZE is 0
VRAMSizeMB ≠ Linux's mem_info_vram_total (16368, not 16384: amdgpu reports RCC_CONFIG_MEMSIZE as is)register/unitslinux-reference/sysfs-pm.txt mem_info_vram_total; fix readVramSize()

Step 2 (rdna3lvl=2): discovery, display paths, AUX/EDID

SymptomLikely causeAction
no discovery signature / scratch offset outside VRAMtable not at VRAM top − 64 KiBport amdgpu_discovery_get_tmr_info / amdgpu_discovery_read_binary_from_mem; compare dmesg-amdgpu.txt

Step 3 (rdna3lvl=3): GC registers, harvesting

SymptomLikely causeAction
ActiveCUs / GBAddrConfig ≠ Linuxharvesting / SE-SA select / golden valuecompare linux-reference/radv-info.txt; fix hw/gfx11_info.c (gfx11_get_cu_info) and its sim model; gfx_v11_0_gpu_early_init

Step 4 (rdna3lvl=4): PSP and firmware — most likely first wall

Inputs: RDNA3,Stage2, PSPResult, SOSVersion, NeedPost, PostResult, the PSP ERROR line.

IP versions of the real card (discovery, 2026-10-09): GC 11.0.3, MP0/MP1 13.0.10, DCN 3.2.0, VCN 4.0.0, MMHUB 3.0.0, SDMA 6.0.3, NBIO 4.3.0, SMUIO 13.0.6. Earlier code assumed Navi 33's 13.0.7 / 3.2.1 / 4.0.4; fixed: firmware names, MMHUB 3.0.0 registers, and hw/smu13.c now follows smu_v13_0_0_ppt.c (driver_if v13_0_0, SCPM, MP1 firmware flags at 0x3010024). The DCN register headers come from dcn_3_2_1_*; every define they hold (display, AUX/HPD, DMCUB: 1088) has the same value in dcn_3_2_0_*, and amdgpu uses the same DMUB code (dmub_dcn32.c) and pipe / AUX / HPD counts for both, so nothing changes for steps 5b and 9 apart from the firmware (dcn_3_2_0_dmcub.bin).

SymptomLikely causeAction
Stage2: starting for > 90 s, no firmware … linethe firmware read never returned (was: OSKextRequestResource unanswered for an injected kext)now read with the vnode KPI from /Library/Extensions/RDNA3Accel.kext/Contents/Resources; check that setup.sh ran after the last prepare
FB MC 0x0-0xffffff, NeedPost 0, KDB times out after C2PMSG_35 was 0x80000000fixed 2026-10-09: the FB base was read from GC (GCMC_VM_FB_LOCATION_*, 0 until the gfxhub is programmed) instead of MMHUB like gmc_v11_0_vram_gtt_location, and with MMHUB 3.0.2 offsets (Navi 32 is 3.0.0), so the PSP got a wrong fw_pri MC addressFB MC must now show the MMHUB base (typically 0x8000000000)
FB MC 0x0-0xffffff, NeedPost 1VM: OVMF did not run the option ROM's init, GPU not postedpostIfNeeded() runs atom_asic_init (amdgpu_device_asic_init); PostResult must be 0 and the FB location non-zero afterwards. PostResult ≠ 0: the ATOM interpreter (hw/atom.c) on the real ASIC_Init v2.1 table — find the opcode in the log
PSP: load KDB failed (-62), C2PMSG_35=0, SOSVersion 0VM, 2026-10-09: the bus reset after the host's amdgpu leaves the PSP with no sOS (C2PMSG_81 0) and the bootloader not ready (C2PMSG_35 0)resetIfPspStuck() (default rdna3reset=1) does a mode-1 reset first; read PSPStuckAtInit, InitResetResult and the two PSP state (…) log lines. No change after the reset: the PMFW did not take the debug message — try the host side (RESTORE_MODE=suspend before the step, so the card enters the VM fresh from a power cycle)
failed: no PSP firmware / firmware set incompletepackagingfirmware plan: log; kext/Makefile FW_FILES vs common/amd_fw.c amd_navi32_fw_names; fix fw_copy in tools/vm/rdna3vm
log sOS already alive, skipping bootloader, then any PSP failureVM: the card arrives initialised by Linux amdgpu. amdgpu never reuses that state: soc21_need_reset_on_init (sign of life MP0 C2PMSG_81 ≠ 0) → ASIC reset on load, then amdgpu_device_asic_init (amdgpu_device_init, Linux v6.12 amdgpu_device.c ~4265 and ~4300)at the start of RDNA3Bringup::hwInit() (first init only, not fReinit): if sign of life is set → smu13_mode1_reset (exists; used by recovery) → psp13_wait_for_bootloader → atom_asic_init → cold PSP path. Simulator: start the PSP model with SOL set and stale ring registers

Step vcn2 (rdna3vcn=2; rdna3vcn=3 by hand): VCN dynamic power gating

Hotplug (any step from 6b with rdna3msi=1)

Unplug and plug a monitor cable while macOS runs: the log shows HPD: hotplug interrupts enabled, then display: HPDn interrupt #k (connected / disconnected) (N42: until then the src_id was wrong, 0x2a instead of DCN_1_0__SRCID__DC_HPD1_INT = 9, and no HPD IV was ever claimed); RDNA3,HPDIrqs counts them. With rdna3hpdirq=0 (default) the line ends not acted on and the hotplug poll decides; with rdna3hpdirq=1 it is followed by display: HPDn connected / disconnected, hotplug on pins ... and RDNA3,HotplugEvents.

SymptomLikely causeAction
no display: HPDn interrupt line on unplug (RDNA3,HPDIrqs absent or 0)DCN interrupts do not reach the IH (DC_HPD_INT_EN set but no IV), or no MSIRDNA3,IHEarlyIVs, MSI counters, the display irq triage row (do vblank IVs arrive?); read HPDn_DC_HPD_INT_STATUS (bit 0 latched?)
many HPDn lines for one unplugpolarity / ack order (hpd_ack in irq_service_dcn32.c)send the log; check DC_HPD_INT_POLARITY after the ack
connected but the connector has no EDID afterwardsre-probe too early (sink not ready)delay the re-probe (amdgpu waits through dc_link_detect retries)

VBL and flip done from the DCN interrupts (N42; default on, rdna3vblirq=0 for the A/B)

With MSI (rdna3msi=1, in the profile) every DCN framebuffer registers with the core (display irq: head n (OTG o, HUBP p): the framebuffer registered for 0x3, enabled 0x3), gets its VBL from the OTG VSTARTUP interrupt (a HIGH ONCE thread call) and the accelerator's latch wait is ended by the HUBP flip interrupt; the OTG poll stays as the fallback after 3 silent frames. Triage: vblank irq (framebuffer, RDNA3,FBStats virq_* / latch_*), display irq (core, debug.rdna3_dispirq / RDNA3,DisplayIrq), the present queue's flip done from the pflip interrupt rows (swaps_pq_irq*_h<N>).

SymptomLikely causeAction
display irq: NO MSI handler / framebuffer why 2rdna3msi=0, or the MSI registration failed (MSI: cannot register)boot-args; the MSI: log lines. The poll delivered: nothing broken, nothing measured
vblank irq: NOT registered ... 0xe00002c7a core older than N42 (stale RDNA3Accel.kext: STALE KEXTS row) or rdna3vblirq=0 in the core onlyrdna3vm approve, the core's display irq: rdna3vblirq= line
display irq: VBLANK enabled on OTG n but NO interrupt reached the corethe source does not raise (VSTARTUP_INT_TYPE / the OTG's interrupt routing differ from amdgpu's assumptions) or the IH drops client DCEport check: irq_service_dcn32.c vblank_int_entry, optc1_* VSTARTUP setup; read OTGn_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS (EVENT_OCCURRED set? INT_STATUS?) in a register dump; leave rdna3vblirq=1 (the poll covers it)

vblank irq: fell back to the OTG poll N time(s)interrupts stop for > 3 frames (display sleep and wake, a mode set, the head blanked)compare the VBL: head n: no vblank interrupt for ... us lines with the mode-set / sleep lines; isolated fallbacks at those moments are fine
interrupt storm line, stormed= mask seta level source not cleared by the ack (CLEAR strobe semantics)send the log; debug.rdna3_vblirq=0 then =1 re-arms it; check dal_irq_service_ack_generic vs the register's CLEAR bit

FAILED, display: the mode-1 reset took n head(s) down); expected while the display is dark; with rdna3resume=1 a later good wake brings the sources back (the display is back)

Mode set and scaled modes (modeset, modeset2)

Every rdna3_drminfo --scanout look prints per powered pipe: DET n x 64 KiB (compbuf m), DISPCLK .. kHz, DPPCLK .. kHz (pipe), DSCL mode, LB, mem pwr, DLG after scaler y/x req .. line ... It reads the sticky underflow, clears it, and the second look 2 s later says UNDERFLOW again within 2 s (steady) or no underflow in the 2 s after clearing it (it came from a switch). rdna3_modetest cycle first tries each display's fastest timing of its own size (look mode-d<D>-<W>x<H>-<Hz>hz), then the smaller modes. modeset2 = modeset + rdna3dispclk=1 + rdna3scaledlg=1.

SymptomLikely causeAction

Compare the pipes: DET (the clone gets the smaller half, dcn32_clone_plan), mem pwr (LB_Gx force bits; the clone does not copy DSCL_MEM_PWR_CTRL), DPPCLK (pipe) below the pixel clock (DTO)the differing one: equal DET split (dcn32_determine_det_override), copy DSCL_MEM_PWR_CTRL / dpp1_power_on_dscl, or dcn32_clk_raise's DTO for the scaled pipe
no underflow in the 2 s after clearing it after a switchtransient while the scaler / viewport latchedpicture fine: record, no fix needed

DP link (dplink)

dplink = modeset + rdna3dplink=1. A DP SST head (DIG_MODE 0, the GOP lit a native DP monitor) is offered modes and switched like a TMDS head, through hw/dcn32_dp.c: link down (stream blank, SET_POWER D3, TransmitterControl DISABLE), the DP DTO at the new pixel clock, the link cap verified once (training at the sink's maximum, falling back), then the link up at the smallest setting the mode needs. Serial / log lines start with dp:; ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,DPLink sums the link up.

SymptomLikely causeAction
no dp: lines, the DP head offers only currentthe boot-arg is missing, the head is not DP SST (DCN: head n ... mode 0?), the VBIOS path has no DDC line for the transmitter (RDNA3,DisplayPath<n>), or the sink gave no caps (dp: ... gives no capabilities)RDNA3,Connector<n>,DPCD; a DP++ adaptor makes the head TMDS (mode 2/3): the modeset step covers it
every mode refused (8)no link setting of the (verified) link cap carries the modedp: AUX n: DPCD ..., max link ...; the VBIOS encoder caps (RDNA3,DisplayPath<n> HBR2/HBR3) limit it too
display: head N: the DP link lost its lock: trained again (0), RDNA3,DPLinkLosses > 0 (with rdna3hotplug=1)the sink dropped the link (monitor input switch, power save, a cable bump); the status DPCD 0x202..0x204 is read every 2 s in place of the HPD RX interrupt and the stream trained again (dp_handle_link_loss)pass if the picture came back; (-5) three times in a row stops the tries until a hotplug: send the dp: link training lines
wake: N DP display(s) blanked before the restoredcn32_init_hw's link_blank_all_dp_displays: a DP sink still awake after the sleep goes to D3 before the restore trains it againexpected after a power loss with a DP monitor
dp: link training HBR3x4 CR failed lane0 VS=3 then a pass at HBR2the fallback works: the cable / sink does not do HBR3pass; RDNA3,Head<n>,DPLink names the verified cap
every training CR failed lane0 VS=0, no VS changethe sink never saw the pattern: the PHY is not running at the link rate (TransmitterControl ENABLE through the DMCUB failed: dp: TransmitterControl action 1 ... failed), wrong transmitter, or DP_DPHY_TRAINING_PATTERN_SEL not reaching the PHYthe DMUB lines of the log; the head's DIG_BE_CNTL / DIG_BE_EN_CNTL in the look
CR done, EQ failed at every rate and lane countpre-emphasis requests not applied: SETUP_VSEMPH's lane set (TransmitterControl v1.7 mode field) is ignored by the VBIOS tablethe lane status / adjust debug lines; compare with the GOP's DPCD 0x103..0x106
trained, dp: DIG f -> UNIPHY b ... trained, monitor black or "no signal"stream side: M/N, MSA or the DP DTO (DPREFCLK)look: DP_DTOn_PHASE / MODULO (phase = pixel clock in Hz, modulo = DPREFCLK in Hz), DP_VID_M, DP_VID_STREAM_CNTL status; dp: DIG n: SYMCLK_FE_ON stays off = the FE is not connected to the BE

DSC and FEC (dsc)

dsc = dplink + rdna3dsc=1. On a native DP monitor that decodes DSC (DPCD 0x060 bit 0) a mode the link cannot carry uncompressed is offered and set with DSC; FEC runs on a FEC capable monitor whatever the mode. Lines: dp: AUX n: FEC .., DSC .. (rev .., slices .., ...) (the caps), modeset: head n: WxH needs DSC: a x b slices, c bpp, dsc: DSC n: ... (the engine's config), dp: DSC n on OPP .. -> OTG .. -> DIG .., dp: AUX n: SST_DSC: DSC enable on the RX, dp: UNIPHY n: FEC enabled (active 1); ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,DSC (slices, bpp, FEC state: 2 = enabled).

SymptomLikely causeAction
no mode needs DSC (rdna3_modetest list has the same modes as dplink)the monitor's modes all fit the trained link uncompressed, or its DPCD has no DSC (DSC no in the dp: AUX n: FEC .. line, or no such line)expected with such a monitor: test with a mode above the link (4K 120+ Hz on HBR3, or a cable / hub that limits the link)
FEC enabled (active 0)the PHY did not start FEC after the training (DP_DPHY_CNTL FEC_ACTIVE_STATUS)look: DP_DPHY_CNTL, DPCD 0x120; the training lines before it
DSC mode set, trained, monitor black or "no signal"the monitor did not get or accept the PPS: GSP11 off, the PPS bytes, DSC not on in the RX (DPCD 0x160)look: DP_SEC_CNTL / DP_SEC_CNTL2 / DP_GSP11_CNTL, DP_DSC_CNTL, OPTC_DATA_FORMAT_CONTROL DSC mode, the dsc: DSC n: line (slice size, bpp)
DSC mode set, picture garbled or shiftedrate control / slice config the encoder and the monitor disagree on, or the DSC engine not fed by the head's OPPthe dsc: line and the look's DSCC_PPS_CONFIG*, DSCRM_DSC_FORWARD_CONFIG (OPP source)
dsc: DSC n power up: PGFSM status ..DOMAIN16+n did not ungatelook: DOMAIN16..19_PG_STATUS, DC_IP_REQUEST_CNTL
modeset: ... underflows / does not run: back to only at DSC modesthe DLG rescale of the GOP's values at the compressed modeas for modeset2: the look's DLG line; full DML for that mode

Timing generator read-back (otg)

otg = 9c + rdna3otg=1. At display start dcn32_otg_check (hw/dcn32_otg.c) runs DC's optc functions on every head the GOP lit and publishes RDNA3,Head<n>,OTGCheck. Pass: 0: ..., the active size equal to the head's, counter moving, vblank 1 vactive 1, vline0 ... latched with the line read within a few lines of the one aimed at, underflow 0, ODM 1. Then mark otg passed: the optc functions the check reads count as working (tools/progress.py ITEM_STEPS).

SymptomLikely causeAction
no OTGCheck propertythe boot-arg is missing, or no head was lit at display start (RDNA3,DisplayHeads 0)the boot-args line of the report
-19 (RDNA3_ENODEV) / 0x0 sizethe OTG reads as off (OTG_MASTER_EN 0)the look's OTG_CONTROL; the head list in 20-log-rdna3.txt
active size differs from the head'sOTG_H/V_BLANK_START_END read differently by optc1_get_otg_active_size than by read_timing (dcn32.c)both register values from the look; fix whichever subtraction is off
counter STOPPEDtwo OTG_STATUS_POSITION reads were equal: the OTG is not counting (or the read is cached)RDNA3,Head<n>,... frame counter from rdna3_drminfo --scanout; the OTG clock (OTG_CLOCK_CONTROL)
vblank 0 or vactive 0OTG_STATUS never showed the state within 100 msthe look's OTG_STATUS; the V_BLANK bits' layout (OTG_V_BLANK / OTG_V_ACTIVE_DISP)
vline0 ... NOT latchedOTG_VERTICAL_INTERRUPT0_STATUS did not set within 60 ms with the line inside the rangethe line aimed at vs OTG_V_TOTAL; the look's OTG_VERTICAL_INTERRUPT0_CONTROL (OUTPUT_POLARITY, INT_TYPE)
underflow 1OPTC_UNDERFLOW_OCCURRED_STATUS is sticky: an underflow at any time since bootcompare with the HUBP underflow of rdna3_drminfo --scanout; not a failure of this step

amdgpu's DMCUB reset (dmubreset)

dmubreset = 9c + rdna3dmubreset=1: the DMCUB is stopped and reset by the exact dmub_dcn32_reset (hw/dmub32.c) before its windows are set up. ioreg RDNA3,DMUBAmdReset: bit 0 = STOP_FW acked (GPINT status cleared by the firmware), bit 1 = the firmware answered 0xDEADDEAD in SCRATCH7. The log has DMUB diag (after the amdgpu reset): SCRATCH0-16, fault addresses, inbox / outbox pointers, and up to 16 DMUB trace: entries. Pass: RDNA3,DMUB = running, mailbox up, the clone and the extended desktop as in 9c. Then mark dmubreset passed.

SymptomLikely causeAction
RDNA3,DMUBAmdReset = 0 or 2, DMUB running anywaythe firmware acks later than 30 register reads (amdgpu forces the reset then)harmless if the boot and the clone pass; note the value: the verified path waits up to 1 s
DMUB: auto-load timed out after the amdgpu resetDMUIF_SOFT_RESET left set, or the forced reset caught the firmware mid PHY sequencethe DMUB diag lines (soft-reset 1? fault addresses?), MMHUBBUB_SOFT_RESET in the look; step 5b's path (no boot-arg) still works
DMUB running, second monitor darkthe clone's VBIOS commands after the reset: as step 9b's triageclone: lines, RDNA3,MirrorResult

DC's HDMI stream (hdmi, hdmi20)

hdmi = modeset + rdna3hdmi=1, hdmi20 = modeset2 + rdna3hdmi=3. Every TMDS mode set then also runs the HDMI half of DC's stream setup (hw/dcn32_hdmi.c, called from program() in hw/dcn32_modeset.c). The log has, per mode list, display: head n HDMI: sink HDMI|DVI, SCDC s, LTE scramble l, max TMDS M MHz, HDMI 6G g, a audio modes (the EDID's HDMI VSDB / HF-VSDB, the VBIOS encoder cap), and per switch modeset: head n: HDMI_CONTROL 0x..., SCDC TMDS_CONFIG ...; ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,HDMI keeps the last one. Pass for hdmi: the switches of rdna3_modetest cycle pass as in modeset with the picture right (no over/underscan, no colour cast, no "no signal" after the switch back). Pass for hdmi20: additionally a mode above 340 MHz (4K60) was offered, set, and RDNA3,Head<n>,HDMI shows HDMI_CONTROL bits 1 and 2 (scrambler, 1/4 clock channel) with SCDC TMDS_CONFIG 0x03 scrambler 0x01. Then mark the step passed (the ITEM_STEPS rules map the ported enc / SCDC / validation functions to these steps).

SymptomLikely causeAction
picture overscanned / underscanned or colours washed out after a switchthe AVI infoframe (set_avi_info_frame: VIC, aspect, S = underscan, Q default) read differently by the TVthe mode's timing against the CTA VIC table (edid_match_vic); reproduce the packet with dcn32_hdmi_build_info_frames in tests/sim_display_dc.inc and compare byte by byte with CTA-861
SCDC ... (-n) with n = NACK on an HDMI 1.4 monitorno SCDC at I2C 0x54: expected, DC ignores the errornothing; on an HDMI 2.0 sink check the DDC line (hc->ddc, the VBIOS path)
SCDC ... (-n) ACQUIREthe DDC engine owned by the DMCUB/HWnote; the SCDC write is skipped, which only matters above 340 MHz
hdmi20: 4K60 not in rdna3_modetest listHDMI 6G 0 (no ATOM_ENCODER_CAP_RECORD_HDMI6Gbps for the connector), the EDID's max TMDS below 594 MHz, or DISPCLK limits (RD_MODESET_CLOCKS in display: head n mode ... refused (4))the refusal code in the mode list log: 5 = TMDS limit, 4 = clocks
hdmi20: "no signal" at 4K60, old mode backscrambling not matched by the sink (scrambler 0x00), or the PHY PLL at 594 MHzSCDC values in the log; HDMICHARCLK / PHY registers in the look; retry with a certified cable
audio device appears but silentwithout rdna3audio=1 the azalia endpoint is never enabled (only the audio info frame line is set)run step audio

Display audio (audio)

audio = hdmi + rdna3dplink=1 rdna3audio=1. The first mode list logs display: head n audio: "NAME", m modes, speakers 0x.., latency v/a ms (the EDID's SADs and speaker allocation, dcn32_audio_info_from_edid), the first switch audio: n azalia endpoints, straps 0x.. (HDMI audio native) (dcn32_audio_pool_init), every switch display: head n audio: endpoint e, HDMI|DP (DIO)|DP (HPO), on|off, m modes, speakers 0x.., DTO source d, c channels at P kHz pixel clock, hot plug 0x.., PME p; ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,Audio keeps the last one. Pass: on, m modes > 0, the picture switches as in hdmi; with an HDA driver attached to the card's audio function, System Settings > Sound > Output lists the monitor by its EDID name and a test sound plays. Without such a driver the card side alone passes the step (on and hot plug 0x8.......: bit 31 = AUDIO_ENABLED of the endpoint, set by dce_aud_az_enable); then mark audio passed (the ITEM_STEPS rules map the enc / AFMT / APG / audio DTO functions to it).

Output pixel formats (fmt)

fmt = hdmi + rdna3fmt=1 (=3: max 8 bpc, =5: YCbCr 4:2:2 forced). Every HDMI switch logs and sets RDNA3,Head<n>,Format = ENC N bpc, colour space c, sink bpc b formats 0x.. (amdgpu_dm's choice for the mode: dcn32_output_format). Pass: the switches of rdna3_modetest cycle as in hdmi with the right colours (no cast, no extra banding) and, where the sink offers it, YCbCr 4:4:4 and more than 8 bpc in the property (the monitor's info menu agrees). Then mark fmt passed.

DP MST (mst)

mst = dplink + rdna3mst=1; needs an MST hub (or a daisy-chain monitor) on the card's native DP output with at least one monitor behind it. At the first mode switch on that head the driver turns MST on in the hub, reads the topology and every monitor's EDID over sideband messages (mst: lines, ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,MSTPorts = port:peer type:full PBN:name per output port), and sends the head's stream to one monitor (rdna3mstport=N picks port N; default the first). After that switch the mode list is that monitor's. ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,MST = port, VCPI, PBN, time slots, X.Y, ACT handled.

uhbr = dplink + modeset2's clocks + rdna3uhbr=1; needs a DP 2.1 monitor on a DP80 (UHBR20) or DP54 (UHBR13.5) cable straight into the card, and a mode above HBR3 x4 (25.9 Gbps) in its EDID. Pass: the mode above HBR3 runs with the picture right and goes back to the old mode cleanly. Then mark uhbr passed.

Lines: modeset: head n: WxH on 8b/10b (DIO) | 128b/132b (HPO), ODM 2:1 (runs 1:1), ODM pipe acquired, odm: OTG o: pipe p / OPP o + pipe q / OPP q / DSC q, DET a/b -> c/d, odm: pipe p + q on OTG o: slices w0 + w1 of WxH, refcyc_h_blank_end .., with DSC dsc: DSC p: (W/2)xH .. 2 x .. slices for both engines and dp: DSC p on OPP .. 2 ODM segment(s); ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,ODM. Pass: the 8K mode runs with the whole picture (both halves, no seam), the cursor crosses the middle, and the switch back to a one-pipe mode turns the second pipe off (odm: pipe q off). Then mark odm passed.

Display power (mall)

Two heads: the UCLK pin of the default path (N74)

Without rdna3mall (the default) the core pins UCLK's hard minimum at the top level while two or more heads are lit (amdgpu's dcn32_update_clocks for p_state_change_support false: two unsynchronised heads have no shared vblank for the PMFW's switch). Switch: boot-arg rdna3uclkpin=0, live sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3_uclk_pin=0|1. Triage row UCLK pin (N74).

SubVP (subvp)

subvp = mall + rdna3subvp=1 with one monitor (rdna3mirror=0), its high-refresh mode set with rdna3_modetest. Lines: subvp: phantom of pipe p (OTG o, WxH) on pipe q: n lines (vtotal t, back porch b, VSTARTUP v), MALL index 0, subvp: on, 1 phantom(s), 2 DMCUB lock commands: 0, display power: mode set: state s, UCLK switching at .. MHz (vactive + SubVP) ..., SubVP 1; ioreg RDNA3,Head<n>,PState (, SubVP main). Pass: the high-refresh mode runs without flicker or tearing for a few minutes (move windows: every flip sends the new address to the firmware), UCLK at a low level in the PM: lines, no HUBP underflow; the switch back to 60 Hz turns the phantom off (subvp: phantom pipe q off, subvp: off (was on)). Then mark subvp passed.

Idle power (rdna3pmexp=1)

Triage prints one line per variant (10 s each): baseline, GFXOFF allowed, workload POWER_SAVING, HDP/ATHUB gating, GFXCLK soft max lowest. Read activity and watts per line: the first variant that drops activity below ~20 % or the watts below ~25 W is the lever - make it the default (port the matching amdgpu default if it was a workaround). If only the GFXCLK cap lowers the watts while activity stays 100 %, the PMFW's busy signal is the problem (RLC/CP idle reporting), not the clock policy.

Hang test (rdna3hangtest=1)

HANG TEST: PASSED = both the SDMA and the GFX ring recovered within the 10 s cap and ran a verified job afterwards. A NOT recovered line: the hang: lines in the serial log name the path taken (soft recovery, SDMA soft reset, GFX soft reset, mode-1 reset). After each recovery the display is checked (RDNA3,Head<n>,State): a GFX/SDMA soft reset must leave every head intact (display: GFX/SDMA reset does not reach DCN: ... LOST (unexpected) = the reset reaches DCN after all: send the serial log); after the mode-1 reset the heads are LOST unless rdna3resume=1 restored them.

Step sleep (rdna3dpms=2 rdna3resume=1): monitor standby, system sleep

Serial lines: state: head N saved/link down/link up/restore/restored (hw/dcn32_state.c), display: head N <why>: ... (the check after each event), wake #N: PCI ..., MEMSIZE ..., ASIC_Init ..., PSP sOS ..., resume #N ... (RDNA3Bringup::resumeAfterPowerLoss). With rdna3resume=1 the sleep also runs amdgpu_device_suspend (suspend #N: rings idle, then the IPs down in reverse order, RDNA3,SystemSuspends), so every wake runs the resume, also when the card kept its power (resume #N: after amdgpu_device_suspend). Before the suspend the VRAM clients use is copied to system memory (suspend: N VRAM range(s), M MiB saved, RDNA3,EvictedMiB); after a power loss it goes back before the display restore (resume: N VRAM range(s) back from system memory, then every evicted VRAM range is back: no VRAMLOST for the clients). With rdna3hotplug=1 the HPD looks pause for the sleep and come back with hotplug: wake: N HPD filter(s) programmed again (link_resume) (RDNA3,HotplugResumes); a hotplug: unplug of a monitor that stayed connected right after a wake means the pins were looked at too early. display: the picture in VRAM was lost (...) after a wake means the eviction did not bring everything back (see the suspend: / resume: VRAM lines); the framebuffers then log the picture in VRAM was lost: connect change and WindowServer should redraw within a second - a desktop that stays black or garbled after that line is a WindowServer question.

SymptomLikely causeAction

Step vcnsleep (sleep + rdna3vcn=2): the video block through a system sleep

Step sleep's table first. The video part: the report's --vcn-test before the sleep and --vcn-test (after the wake) with the VCN properties before it; RDNA3,VCNSuspends, RDNA3,VCNSuspendResult, RDNA3,VCNResumes, the log lines VCN: suspended (JPEG 0, VCN 0), VCPU BOs saved to system memory and VCN: resume (...): 0. Order as amdgpu: the display suspends first, then JPEG and VCN; on the wake VCN and JPEG come back after GFX (in the bring-up after a power loss, from the wake work when the card kept its power). Plain step sleep with any rdna3vcn= set by hand runs the same path.

Step amdgpu (default profile + rdna3amdgpu=1): amdgpu's own sequence

The boot-arg switches every place where the profile verified on the card does something else than amdgpu to amdgpu's own code (stage 4 item J; rdna3_dev.amdgpu_exact). Read the report like step default first; RDNA3,AmdgpuExact = 1 says the boot-arg was seen. Then, area by area:

4. When steps 0–8 pass: next work, in order

  1. Default rdna3lvl to the highest level that passed twice (boot-args stay overrides); update the testing docs and Notion percentages.
  2. RADV on the card: add rdna3vm step 11 (RADV from the radv-macos CI job into the bundle; vulkaninfo --summary; a dEQP-VK compute.basic.* subset). Needs one full: true run — ask first.
  3. Display Core: diagnostic build dumping DCN state at the GOP mode, compared with Linux; then DCCG pixel clock, DP training, DML, modeset, hotplug (docs/PLAN-NEXT.html §3).
  4. Metal: mtlprobe, first sample.
  5. Power: UCLK/FCLK/DCN DPM after Display Core; BACO/sleep.
  6. MES only when needed. 7. Packaging: OpenCore guide, release zip.

5. Message to the user (Polish template)

Keep it short, no internals beyond one sentence of cause.

Wynik: kroki 0–N przeszły, krok M nie (<jedno zdanie: co i dlaczego>).
Poprawka: <jedno zdanie>. CI: <link do przebiegu> (zielone).

Co zrobić:
1. Pobierz artefakt RDNA3Accel-kext z <link>.
2. rdna3vm prepare --artifact ~/Downloads/RDNA3Accel-kext.zip
3. rdna3vm window → w Terminalu macOS: curl -s http://10.0.2.2:8000/setup.sh | sudo sh
   → sudo shutdown -h now
4. rdna3vm step M (potem step M-1 jako kontrola)
5. Prześlij nowe pliki z ~/rdna3-vm/reports.

For a diagnostic build say so explicitly ("to wersja diagnostyczna, nic nie naprawia, potrzebuję tylko raportu z kroku M").

6. Bare metal vs VM

VM failures that bare metal would not have: warm PSP (step 4 "sOS already alive"), leftover interrupts (step 6), unposted card (step 1), VM setup (step 0). A failure in both environments is ours. A stage counts as done only after steps 4–8 also passed once on bare metal (or the user decides the VM is enough).

7. Known pitfalls of this environment

  • Shell heredocs: a line EOF inside a Python heredoc ends it — write longer scripts to a scratch file instead.
  • grep -P '[ąę…]' and \b match arrows/dashes byte-wise here; check Polish text with a Unicode-aware Python scan.
  • The kext cannot be compiled locally (no macOS SDK): kext-side changes are verified only by the CI run — read them twice before pushing.

WindowServer never opened the GPU (the boot watchdog)

Card runs 2026-10-26e/f: the kext and the AGDC came up, the serial log ends at VMID 1 for accelerator (no WindowServer VMID, no newUserClient), once with busy timeout[0], (60s): 'AGPMEnabler', the screen stayed black, the owner killed the VM and macOS's unified log of that boot was gone. Since then every VM boot that runs tests starts a watchdog in the background (# BOOTWATCH-BEGIN in tools/vm/macos/autoreport.sh; it never blocks the boot and ends itself):

  • Every 5 s it asks "does WindowServer own a user client of RDNA3Accelerator / RDNA3Accel in the ioreg" (IOUserClientCreator = "pid N, WindowServer") and "is anybody logged in". Either one ends the watch.
  • rdna3bootwatch=N s after its start (boot-arg, default 75, rdna3bootwatch=0 switches it off) with the accelerator in the ioreg and neither true, it collects bootwatch-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>-<n>.txt and sends it to the host share at once (after each section, so a killed VM leaves what was done; a failed upload waits in /var/db/rdna3-pending-* for the next boot), then again every 60 s (at most 30 times / 40 min), and calls sync so the unified log reaches the disk. A boot below the accelerator's level (no RDNA3Accelerator) and the install / approve boots write nothing.
  • A file holds: the BOOTWATCH: header (n, trigger_s, after_s, uptime_s, console_user), ps of WindowServer / loginwindow / AGPM (state, wchan), launchctl list, the RDNA3Accelerator / RDNA3Accel ioreg essentials (clients with their creators, MetalPlugin keys, RDNA3,* properties), kextstat, the plugin's rdna3mtl.conf and allow list, the kernel's own dmesg RDNA3/AGDC/AGPM lines, sample WindowServer 5 (spindump when sample gives nothing; "NOT RUNNING" when there is no WindowServer), and log show --last 3m of WindowServer, loginwindow and the RDNA3 / NVMTL / AGPM / AGDC lines. Missing tools are named in the file.
  • When WindowServer gets its client (or somebody logs in) after all, the last file is BOOTWATCH-END: state=gpu|login after_s=...; gave-up / timeout means the watch ended with WindowServer still without the GPU.
  • The VM's own log of the watchdog: /var/db/rdna3-bootwatch.log (a rdna3vm window boot, by hand).

Triage (tools/triage_bootwatch.py, printed before everything else, also when macOS never sent a report): FAIL BOOTWATCH: WindowServer did not reach the GPU within 75 s (...) with the top of WindowServer's main-thread stack (the frames that held every sample: deepest first), the number of snapshots and the console user; WARN BOOTWATCH when it got there only later (the time is in the row). Read the stack first: a thread in IOServiceOpen / IOAccelOpen -> the kernel side (serial-*.log around newUserClient, the core's debug.rdna3_* counters); in dlopen / the Metal plugin (RDNA3MTLDriver, NVMTL: lines in the log show section) -> the plugin (conf in the same file, rdna3mtl.conf); mach_msg to AGPMEnabler / the AGDC -> ioreg section and the AGPM lines; WindowServer NOT RUNNING -> launchd / loginwindow lines. KERNEL DEADLOCK (spindump) in the row: the watchdog's bootwatch-*.spindump holds the kernel's own Deadlock: verdict; the detail lines list the images in each deadlocked thread's stack. Our kext in the cycle is a lock-order bug of ours: resolve the frames (RDNA3Accelerator + N is the offset from the kext's __TEXT; the Apple kexts' from their fileset entries in reference/macos-26.4.1/kc/SystemKernelExtensions.kc) as in DRIVER-PLAN N58 (card run 2026-10-26j: the boot-arg thread call against AppleGPUWrangler; since then the serial log has N58 ...: inside <n> framebuffer controller gate(s) before each registration; a head "WITHOUT a controller workloop" there or a "refused the action" line is the first thing to read). The files are not part of the rdna3-report-* tarball (that never comes in this failure); rdna3vm result packs them with the rest. The first boot after an autoreport.sh update runs the old script until it has fetched the new one (the watchdog starts with the new one).

Plugin draw capture (DRIVER-PLAN N55)

When the owner set NVMTL_CAPTURE_DRAW=<shader>:<first>[:<count>[:<every>]][@<process>] (rdna3mtl.conf or the environment; e.g. buffer_image_blit:60:24:11@Terminal), the report has 33-plugin-capture/capture-*.bin, one per captured draw (metal/plugin/nvmtl_capture.h), and the process log the capture: lines (switch ON / MALFORMED, each draw taken and written, anything DROPPED or NOT written). Decode:

python3 tools/capture_decode.py rdna3-report-*.tar.gz --out /tmp/cap

Read it as: per buffer, cpu_draw vs gpu_done DIFFER over bytes the draw reads (for buffer_image_blit the per-rect lines say which glyph read which differing bytes) = the GPU drew from bytes the app did not give it: an upload or coherence bug, chase the managed upload / kernel path for that range; cpu_draw vs cpu_done changed = the app or the plugin rewrote the bytes while the GPU needed them; everything SAME = the inputs were right: replay the draw (SPIR-V, constants, buffers in the capture) on the host's RADV and compare with /tmp/cap/*-cpu_draw.pgm. Caveats: cpu_done / gpu_done are taken at completion, so a slice the app reused right away (Core Animation's buffer pool) may differ for the app's own reasons - trust differences only over bytes the draw read and cpu_done did not change; the read-back is a synchronous copy submitted from the completion handler WITHOUT the pre-submit hook (it does not flush pending managed uploads), but it is an extra submit, so a captured run is not timed exactly like an uncaptured one. The collector keeps at most 64 captures and 512 MiB (NVMTL_COLLECT_CAPTOTAL). A capture whose target is an IOSurface in VRAM (N56) also has target.gpu_done, the scissor rows of the surface at completion: the decoder's target: line says whether the GPU's pixels show the text the CPU coverage says (MATCHES, or how many covered pixels show no text and which columns) - this splits "the draw goes wrong" from "something after the draw breaks it". Replay a capture on the host's GPU with tests/vk_capture_replay.c (cc -O2 -o /tmp/replay tests/vk_capture_replay.c -lvulkan -ldl -lm; /tmp/replay /tmp/cap/capture-Terminal-<pid>-<k> table -l <libnvmtl_translate.so>): SAME = the shaders and RADV draw it right here. No capture-*.bin but the switch was set: the capture: lines say why (MALFORMED, another process named, budget, write failure).

Plugin frame trace

Every process that loads the Metal plugin writes a binary trace of what it did with IOSurfaces, command buffers and Vulkan: /var/tmp/rdna3-nvmtl/<process>-<pid>.trace (format: metal/plugin/nvmtl_trace.h). tools/macos/collect_plugin_trace.sh puts them in the report as 31-plugin-trace/ and saves WindowServer's NVMTL log whole as 32-log-nvmtl-windowserver.txt (once collect.sh calls it; until then run it by hand on the card machine, as root: sudo sh tools/macos/collect_plugin_trace.sh <dir>; the files it adds are handed back to the invoking user). The text log is rate limited; the trace is the record. Decode on the host:

python3 tools/plugin_trace.py rdna3-report-*.tar.gz # all processes, WindowServer first python3 tools/plugin_trace.py REPORT --proc WindowServer --frames 40 python3 tools/plugin_trace.py REPORT --surface 1234 --all # one IOSurface's whole timeline + failures python3 tools/plugin_trace.py REPORT --rates # counts per second of process life

Questions it answers (the "findings:" block names each, per IOSurface):

  • Which surface did WindowServer render into, frame by frame? The frame view lists, per command buffer, the pass (surf, load/store action, clear colour) and what it turned out to be: DRAWN, CLEAR-ONLY (cleared and nothing drawn; clear-only finding when the surface is display sized and the colour is transparent black), or untouched.
  • Was it published? never-published: a command buffer wrote the surface and no dirty mark and no copy-out ran for it (FAIL). The per IOSurface table counts o:dirt (dirty mark, no copy), o:copy (copy-out), o:refu (copy-out because the kernel refused the dirty mark), o:fail, EMPTY.
  • Did the kernel refuse? dirty-refused lists the errno (macOS numbers; 19 = ENODEV: no VRAM behind the surface); kreq-import the refused surface imports.
  • Was the published picture empty? (FAIL only when the same command buffer's pass DREW into the surface and the copy-out is still empty; an empty picture after a clear-only pass is INFO, one whose pass is not in the trace WARN.) empty-publish: the band hash of the data written into the IOSurface (the same 8 bands x 48 columns FNV hash as the kernel's rd3AccelBandHash, so a hash can be matched with the kext's swap #N band lines) has every sampled pixel zero. A back buffer that is always CLEAR-ONLY + EMPTY while its twin is DRAWN + dirty is the flicker.
  • Surface-in skipped while the VRAM was stale? skip-stale: a "shared VRAM current" skip right after a failed, empty or refused-then-copied publish of that surface; skip-foreign (INFO): a skip that relies on a VRAM tag written by another process. NVMTL_TRACE_HASH=2 additionally hashes the unlocked CPU copy of skipped / dirty surfaces (not a default: it reads memory the plugin did not lock).
  • Did Vulkan fail? vk-submit (vkQueueSubmit not VK_SUCCESS, with the function that submitted), vk-wait (fence timeout / error, wait time), vk-disable ("disabling backend" decisions), alloc-fail (vkCreate*/ vkAllocateMemory/newBufferWithBytesNoCopy), cb-error.

Reading a block: +S.mmm is seconds since the process opened its trace, next to it the wall clock (UTC; --utc-offset H for the card's zone; the process's NVMTLDevice +load log line gives the same instant). events: N written, M kept says how much the rings still hold (main ring 16384 records, vk ring 8192: about the last 10 to 30 s of WindowServer); lifetime totals come from the per-second summary (8192 s) and never wrap, so a rate is trustworthy even when the events are gone. SURF_OUT rc is the dirty request's errno (negative: -2 dirty path off, -3 no Vulkan device); seeds are IOSurfaceGetSeed before -> after the lock. A frame with NOT FINISHED was still in flight when the report was taken. If 31-plugin-trace/info.txt says 0 traces: the plugin is older than the trace, or NVMTL_TRACE=0 is set (also readable from the trace: line in the plugin log). Switch off: NVMTL_TRACE=0 in /Library/GPUBundles/rdna3mtl/rdna3mtl.conf. The directory /var/tmp/rdna3-nvmtl is created root:admin, mode 1777, by tools/macos/fix_tracedir.sh (run by the package's postinstall, by build_metal.sh and the VM's autoreport.sh --sync-metal: a symlink, a file or a foreign directory planted there is removed first, never followed; an existing directory of root's or WindowServer's is kept with its live traces and only chown'ed, so WindowServer cannot make it _windowserver:wheel and hide the traces from the admin); the files are mode 0640, so the admin group (and root) can read them and other users cannot. If an app created the directory first, root, WindowServer (uid 88) and that app's user are accepted as owner (a root process accepts only root's), anyone else's is refused (trace: cannot use the trace directory ... in the plugin log); without the postinstall's group the files carry the creator's group, so collect as root (sudo) then; a file the collector cannot read is listed as REJECT ... unreadable: run with sudo. The collector takes only regular files with one link, the NVMTLTR1 magic, owned by root/uid 88 or by the uid their own header names (never a link, never a file named WindowServer* of another user), and says what it refused in 31-plugin-trace/info.txt (REJECT ... reason). The decoder warns (ws-uid) about a WindowServer trace not written by uid 88. Privacy: a trace holds timestamps, IOSurface ids and unsalted hashes of sampled pixels (needed to match the kext's band hash), i.e. a coarse record of screen activity: keep reports private. NVMTL_TRACE_* knobs are ignored by set-uid processes; there is no /private/tmp fallback any more. Residual risks: the trace is an mmap'd file, so a process that truncates it (as root, by hand) kills the traced process with SIGBUS (deleting it is safe); NVMTL_TRACE_HASH=2 reads IOSurface memory the plugin did not lock, so only use it on a run where that is the question. Triage: plugin_trace.triage_lines(report).

Metal plugin: blit copies, framebuffer fetch, encode-time split (N48)

Card run 2026-10-25c: Chess died three times in glReadPixels (crash reports: __assert_rtn in RADV's gfx_or_compute_copy_memory_to_image, which asserts image->vk.samples == 1, called from the plugin's copyFromBuffer:toTexture: on a 4-sample texture; DRIVER-PLAN N48). The plugin now uploads into multisample colour textures itself (msupload: lines below). Read 26-log-metal-plugin.txt for the Chess pid (tools/triage_n48.py prints the rows plugin copy refused, multisample upload, multisample download, framebuffer fetch, plugin copy bracket):

LineMeaningAction

Chess stencil resolve (N52, card run 2026-10-25f; the cyan streaks on the pieces and red specks in EVERY rung of the A/B ladder, PARANOID included). Apple's GL resolves the stencil of its 4-sample D32_S8 framebuffer with five blits, and the plugin used to drop the first two (the lines are in every Chess process, 8 to 16 times per minute): copy_image_to_buffer: REFUSED - the image is multisampled ... (vk format 130, 4 sample(s), type 4, aspect option 4, buffer offset 0, region 971x680) then copy_buffer_to_image: REFUSED - a 4-sample image: this colour format is not carried ... (vk format 13, ..., row 3884 B ...); the rest of the chain (begin_render: par1 4-sample pass resolves colour 0x0 of 0x1, copy_image_to_buffer: recording vk format 13 ..., copy_buffer_to_image: recording (depth/stencil, ...) vk format 130 ... 1 sample(s) ... aspect 4 element 1 B) then moved an uninitialised texture into the stencil. With the pass in, a Chess process must show, per frame that resolves: msdl: SUMMARY d_stencil=N, msupload: SUMMARY up_inter_13=N, resolve_r8u=N all about equal (row multisample chain INFO), no plugin copy refused row for vk format 130 / vk format 13, no msdl: FAILED. If the streaks stay although the lines are there, the layout guess is wrong (see DRIVER-PLAN N52, Risks): compare the msupload:/msdl: row values (3884 = 971 * 4 samples * 1 B) with what the blit asked for.

Framebuffer fetch ([[color(n)]], binding 192+n; card run 2026-10-25d: rectangles over text cells with a red/green gradient and broken glyphs in Terminal/Maps/Settings were reads of an undefined descriptor; now implemented with render-pass input attachments, DRIVER-PLAN N48, unverified on the card). Lines, in the order a run prints them:

LineMeaningAction

The individual lines below are capped per literal, so a benign flood cannot hide a real limit (each reason has its own literal)triage reads it: WARN when skipped, zero_failed, scan_failed, move_failed, att_refused, alloc_failed, any zr_ms/int/tess/nolayout/forced is above 0, or restarts > 100

vk: fbfetch: a pipeline that reads [[color(n)]] 0xA was bound in a pass with input attachments 0xH - the pass is RESTARTED (LOAD) with 0xUthe pass began with a pipeline that did not read; INFO, but many restarts per frame cost timefbfetch: the render pass could not be RESTARTED ... = WARN, the rest of that encoder is not drawn
vk: fbfetch: input-attachment descriptor set allocation FAILED / descriptor layouts FAILEDpool/ layout failure: the draws that read are skipped / every read is zeroedWARN

Triage row framebuffer fetch (INFO = implemented with the draw counters, WARN = something above). If the artifacts persist while the row is INFO with draws counted: the fetch itself is wrong (compare with tests/vk_fbfetch_probe.c, which passes on the dev host's stock RADV; the plugin's Mesa d381c361 has not been compared) - then try NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1 (environment or a line in rdna3mtl.conf) to see whether the pattern changes. If the row is INFO but draws are 0: the pipelines that read were never drawn (check UNSUPPORTED lines).

Trace record kind 17 (NVT_K_ENC_SPLIT, one per render encoder that drew; tools/plugin_trace.py prints it as kind 17 until its decoder is taught): dur_us encoder begin -> end, rc pipeline-state variants built while drawing, a0 draws, a1 pipeline lookup us, a2 descriptor writes us, a3 surface-in checks us over a4 calls, a5 / a6 the slowest single draw's lookup / descriptor writes us (raw fields of nvt_rec; seed0 is the traced pass id). The accumulators are per thread (an encoder is encoded by one thread; one encoded from two would be undercounted) and run only between a render encoder's creation and its endEncoding; tools/plugin_trace.py prints the line encoder cb N: D draw(s) in ... and an encode-time split summary block.

Rendering artefacts: the A/B ladder (N50)

Card run 2026-10-25e (macOS 26.4.1, step play), after the framebuffer fetch fix: Terminal and Maps still show glyphs that are random unreadable strokes inside their cell (selecting the text redraws it right); Chess (GL over the plugin) shows square tile-shaped blocks of wrong colour on the board (they move from photo to photo, so they are per frame, not in the board's texture) and cyan streaks on pieces. The root cause is NOT established; what the report excludes and what stays open:

HypothesisVerdict from the 2026-10-25e reportWhy
H2a: no-copy buffers copied whole before each submission and back afterexcluded for Terminal, Chess and WindowServernewBufferWithBytesNoCopy: COPIED ... is logged once per process and is in no log; WindowServer logs IMPORTED ... zero copy, Terminal and Chess never call it (neither line)
H2c: Terminal's atlas buffer texturethe line is not an atlasbuffer texture: 608x353 VkFormat 44 pitch 2560 ... DRM linear is the window's IOSurface (item 7: surface 294 608x353 pitch 2560 -> the family's VRAM), linear, no compression, never written by the CPU (hash=EMPTY in the trace)
H2b: replaceRegion uploadsnot shown either way for TerminalTerminal logs no plain newTextureWithDescriptor: line at all (the plugin logs every plain texture, the first 8 per literal), but its textures may be heap textures, whose lines the collector used to filter out (heap texture; now only heap texture: per-texture lines are, the census lines heap texture (placed): N placed new, ... reach 26-log-metal-plugin.txt), and it has too few submissions for a submit census line; the census of the others: nvmtl_vk_image_write_region_level_layer Chess 249 in 16k submits, WindowServer 91 and constant
compression metadata (DCC / HTILE / FMASK) uninitialised or stalenot reproduced on the dev host's RADV 26.2.5tests/vk_meta_probe.c: plugin-style sequences (LOAD passes declared TRANSFER_SRC whatever the image rests in, first use as a LOAD pass, replaceRegion-style rectangle uploads, compute writes through an rgba8 / an R32_UINT view, sampling through an R32_UINT view, an sRGB image rendered through a UNORM view with fast clears, 4x multisample + resolve), 1920x1080 / 1024x1024 / 971x680 images (DCC on: the allocation is bigger than with RADV_DEBUG=nodcc), memory filled with 0xCD first, 1 to 32 operations per command buffer: no wrong pixel. Its negative control (NOC2C=1, no barrier between two compute dispatches) fails with 16x16-tile-shaped wrong regions, so the probe can see such a thing
a managed upload read while the CPU rewrites itopen, a counter and a switchTerminal's pixels come from 3 managed buffers of 256 KB (newBufferWithLength:262144 storage 1) read by Apple's buffer_image_blit_frag_lpf shader by device address; the plugin's upload is a GPU copy from the CPU shadow that reads the shadow when the GPU runs it, and scheduled is signalled at submit, not at execution
the fence returns before the GPU's last write is visibleseen onceChess: keep witness 3313 landed only 14 us AFTER the flush returned (a CPU read of GPU output right after a fence wait can see stale bytes; every consumer in the plugin waits for the completion witness, anything outside is exposed)

The switches (all in /Library/GPUBundles/rdna3mtl/rdna3mtl.conf, one NAME=value per line, sudo to edit; a process reads the file when it loads the plugin, so quit the app, and for WindowServer log out and in). Every one is logged by the process that uses it, and triage prints a WARN row while a profile is on, so a profile left in the file cannot hide in a report:

Line in rdna3mtl.confWhat it doesReading
NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG=nodcc,nohiz,nofmask,nofastclearsMesa RADV's compression metadata (DCC, HiZ/HTILE, FMASK, fast clears) off for every process (NVMTL_RADV_PERFTEST= the same for RADV_PERFTEST; only a-z 0-9 _ ,)blocks / strokes gone = compression metadata; unchanged = not metadata
NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0command buffers are submitted at commit and waited for (no submit-ahead)gone = an ordering race of the async worker
NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1hashes each managed upload's shadow range at the flush and again when the GPU is donetriage managed shadow WARN with a count = the CPU rewrote the range while the copy was in flight
NVMTL_MANAGED_SNAPSHOT=1a managed upload's GPU copy reads a private copy of the shadow range made at the flush (ranges up to 1 MiB) instead of the shadow itselfgone = the app rewrites the range after didModifyRange / commit and the GPU copy read a mix (tests/vk_cpu_write_probe.c shows the window on the dev host: a copy reads its CPU source when the GPU runs it, here 73 ms after the rewrite)
NVMTL_LOAD_BARRIER=1makes the declared initial layout of every LOAD pass true (a barrier from the tracked layout)gone = the layout lie matters on the plugin's Mesa (it does not on the dev host's)
NVMTL_NO_ALIGN_GUARD=1(older) the translator's SAFE module variants (built for shaders whose buffer reads rest on a promised 16-byte alignment: Terminal logs align guard: 4 render module(s) built SAFE ... (bindings 0x2) 427 accesses) are not usedthe strokes go away = the SAFE lowering reads wrong bytes at the offsets the dirty rectangles land on (not in NVMTL_PARANOID: it removes a safety, not an optimisation)
NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1(N48) every framebuffer fetch read is a zero vectorthe pattern changes = the fetch is in the picture
NVMTL_PARANOID=1the RADV flags above, NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0, plus: no pool / address-page reuse, no buffer / buffer-texture / heap-texture recycling or placement, no descriptor-set reuse or dirty-only copies, no barrier folding (ENC78_OFF=all, G79_OFF=all), no pre-submit prologue, no residency paging, one part in flight, no surface-in cache (NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE, N51) (15 switches; the log line lists the ones it set and the ones it found set already and kept: a knob of its own, in the file or the environment, wins over the profile). NOT in it: NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK / NVMTL_MANAGED_SNAPSHOT (they live in the pre-submit prologue it removes, so they would log ON and do nothing: the managed rung is its own), NVMTL_NO_ALIGN_GUARD, NVMTL_LOAD_BARRIER, NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZEROstill broken = it is not the plugin's scheduling or reuse: the kernel side (cache / coherency of CPU-written system memory, fences) or the translator / RADV build

Lines and rows (tools/triage_glyphs.py, tests/test_triage_glyphs.py):

LineMeaningTriage row
vk: RADV_DEBUG=<v> RADV_PERFTEST=<v> (NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG=..., NVMTL_RADV_PERFTEST=...)once per process, before the driver opens: the flags RADV starts with (the plugin puts its own into the environment only while RADV reads them at instance creation, then puts the process's own back; a flag name RADV does not know is refused, vk: NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG=... REJECTED)radv debug: INFO unset, WARN set (or no such line at all = a plugin older than N50)
