(UNDEFINED GENERAL COLOR DS DSRO SRO SRC DST PREINIT other) wit_early=... wit_ok=... wit_blind=... wit_worst_us=...a TIME-based line (nvlog_uncapped: at most every 10 s while a counter moved, at the first event and at exit; the capped vk: load pass ... lines are only the first 8 per process): how many LOAD passes began on an image whose tracked layout is not the declared one (depth: judged against the declared depth OR stencil load op), how many of them were seen with a render pass already open (not counted), how many a barrier fixed, and the fence witness totalsload pass layouts INFO with the counts, fence early WARN / INFO. A process the OS ends with SIGTERM prints no exit line: the last tick (10 s) is what survives; the plugin also ticks from the witness-OK path and at the start of every render pass, so a clean process prints its totals too. A process that told its switches (vk: ab switches) and printed no SUMMARY gets the INFO row ab summary: its counters are UNKNOWN, no early fence / no mismatch say nothing about it
vk: keep witness N landed only X us AFTER the flush returned ...first lines only; the count and the worst value are in vk: ab: SUMMARY (wit_early, wit_worst_us)fence early WARN; INFO "pool reuse is OFF there" when NVMTL_NO_POOL_REUSE took the witness away ("no early fence" is then no evidence)
managed: SUMMARY check=C snapshot=S flushes=... uploads=... prologue_uploads=... sync_uploads=... checked=... changed_exact=... changed_env=... check_skipped=... snapped=... snap_failed=... snap_skipped_big=... snap_skipped_noshadow=... and managed: the CPU shadow of a N-byte buffer CHANGED at bytes A..B ...NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1 / NVMTL_MANAGED_SNAPSHOT=1: the totals (time-based, like the line above); sync_uploads are uploads of a synchronous submit, where both switches are INERTmanaged shadow: WARN changed_exact > 0, WARN sync_uploads > 0 (INERT), WARN skipped / failed counts, INFO checked clean

The order to try: NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG=nodcc,nohiz,nofmask,nofastclears; NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0; NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1 and NVMTL_MANAGED_SNAPSHOT=1; NVMTL_NO_ALIGN_GUARD=1; NVMTL_PARANOID=1; NVMTL_LOAD_BARRIER=1. If PARANOID does not change the picture the cause is not in anything the plugin schedules or reuses.

The whole ladder in one card run: rdna3vm step ladder

One run instead of one report per switch (autoreport.sh ladder_run, boot-arg rdna3ladder=1; the profile of the play step, nothing else runs, about 29 minutes; by hand in the guest's Terminal: sudo sh "/Library/Application Support/RDNA3/autoreport.sh" --standalone --ladder). Eight rungs: baseline, attrring (NVMTL_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0, DRIVER-PLAN N57: below), radv (NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG=nodcc,nohiz,nofmask,nofastclears), sync (NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0), managed (NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1 + NVMTL_MANAGED_SNAPSHOT=1), alignguard (NVMTL_NO_ALIGN_GUARD=1), paranoid (NVMTL_PARANOID=1), loadbarrier (NVMTL_LOAD_BARRIER=1). Per rung the run opens Terminal (a colour listing, then a tick line every second in one fixed colour, bright white, and every 4th second a line of mixed glyphs) and Chess as NEW instances with that rung's switches in their environment (open -n -a Terminal --env NVMTL_X=1: the plugin reads its switches from the process's environment as well as from the conf file, the environment wins), waits 25 s (Terminal is opened with -ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES: no window restored from the previous rung), takes a 320-wide and a hi-res (native size) scanout look (ladder-<rung>a, ladder-<rung>h), keeps the plugin's log of exactly those two processes (by pid, labelled by rung), after ending the two instances with SIGINT, then SIGTERM, then SIGKILL (a wait after each; no System Events: that needs the owner's Automation consent, which card run 2026-10-25f never got, quit: still running after 8s in every rung). 41-ladder.txt has a line per instance (quit: pid N ended by SIGINT after 1s), the triage row ladder quit counts them. A signal skips the plugin's exit SUMMARY; the 10 s time-based lines (vk: ab: SUMMARY, managed: SUMMARY, census: SUMMARY) are the evidence, and each rung waits 12 s before it quits. The instances that were already running when the run began (the Terminal you started it from, your own Chess) are never touched. rdna3mtl.conf is not written and WindowServer is not restarted: the switch reaches the two apps only, which is where the artefacts show. At the desktop, the run asks nothing; from a Terminal (--standalone --ladder) it asks y (picture right) / n (broken) / Enter per rung (20 s each) and the answers go into the table.

rdna3vm step ladder-ws (rdna3ladder=2, --ladder-ws, about 45 minutes) adds radv-ws and paranoid-ws: the same switches for WindowServer itself. These edit rdna3mtl.conf (the file's other lines stay, a same-named line is replaced), restart WindowServer (log in at the login window; Terminal and Chess then open under it) and put the conf back exactly (also with no conf file at all, on Ctrl-C / SIGTERM, and at the next start of autoreport.sh after a dead run); the desktop is restarted once more at the end so that it runs with the default conf. Run ladder-ws only when the app rungs say the switch matters.

The attribute ring (attrring rung, DRIVER-PLAN N57). Card run 2026-10-26g: the glyphs break on the GPU at draw time, a contiguous run of 18 instances of one draw gets garbage varyings. On GFX11 the varyings pass through RADV's attribute ring, a DISCARDABLE VRAM BO declared with SPI_ATTRIBUTE_RING_SIZE.BIG_PAGE (64 KiB translation). The rung sets NVMTL_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0; the plugin hands RADV RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0 (Mesa patch 0010) and logs vk: RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE="0" (NVMTL_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE="0") - the attribute ring's BIG_PAGE is OFF .... Rows: ladder attrring WARNs when that line is missing (an older plugin: the rung is the baseline again); ladder glyphs attrring appears when the rung is CLEAN under a BROKEN baseline. The kernel logs every DISCARDABLE BO (GEM DISCARDABLE #n: 4224 KiB, alignment 2048 KiB, VRAM beyond BAR0 at 0x... (64 KiB aligned yes, 2 MiB aligned yes); in GTT: ... GTT, VRAM was asked for and refused - WRONG for the attribute ring ...) and its mapping (GEM_VA DISCARDABLE #n: VA 0x... + 4224 KiB -> contiguous VRAM 0x... (PA 0x...), PTE fragment F (... KiB, 4 KiB PTEs, rdna3amdgpu=0): BIG_PAGE ok | WRONG: ...); the triage row attribute ring recomputes the verdict: PASS = VRAM, 64 KiB aligned, VA and PA congruent modulo 64 KiB, PTE fragment >= 4 (64 KiB); FAIL otherwise (a kext before card run 2026-10-26h's fix, or rdna3vmfrag=0, writes 4 KiB PTEs, fragment 0: FAIL); WARN = no line in the archive's kernel log nor in the newest serial-*.log next to the report (a kext before N57). The row reads both (2026-10-26h: the lines were only in the serial log) and says [from serial-....log ...] when only the serial log had them. attr ring switch lists the processes where RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0 was on. Card run 2026-10-26h: attrring CLEAN (glyph verdict 17/17), baseline BROKEN, the ring contiguous VRAM 2 MiB aligned with PTE fragment 0: the cause. The fix (DRIVER-PLAN N57): the default GPUVM path maps VRAM with amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment fragments (at most 1 MiB per PTE, capped at the physical alignment); the init line VM: VRAM mappings with amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment PTE fragments ... and RDNA3,VMFragments = 1 say it is on, the ring's line now reads PTE fragment 5 (128 KiB, amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment in 4 KiB PTEs, rdna3amdgpu=0): BIG_PAGE ok. From then on: baseline CLEAN and attribute ring PASS = fixed (attrring stays in the ladder as the regression check, it must be clean too); baseline still BROKEN with attribute ring PASS -> the TLB or the SPI wants more than the PTE fragment: rdna3vm step amdgpu (huge PDEs, amdgpu's own update path) as the cross-check; new VM faults (RDNA3,VMFaults, LastFaultAddr inside a BO) after the fix -> boot once with rdna3vmfrag=0 to tell the fragments apart, send the log. attrring still BROKEN -> not BIG_PAGE: replay a capture on the card's own RADV (N56).

Evidence: ladder.txt (archive member 41-ladder.txt, uploaded after every rung as <report>-ladder.txt), each rung's 41-ladder-<rung>.log and the looks <report>-scanoutladder-<rung>[a|h]-pipe<P>.ppm. tools/triage_reports.py prints one row per rung (ladder <rung>: what it set, whether the plugin's own vk: ab switches line in that rung's log shows the switch: WARN when a key the rung set is missing in one of the processes ("did not reach the plugin") or when the process has a key the rung did NOT ask for ("not default: X", also a conf line left over in the baseline), WARN when a process printed no SUMMARY; the counters of the slice, the look count, your answer) and a ladder row: "the baseline picture was broken and the picture was RIGHT with: X" names the discriminator. Reading it: a rung whose WARN says the switch did not reach the plugin proves nothing (the --env was not taken: check 34-metal-conf.txt and 26-log-metal-plugin.txt); without your answers, compare ladder-<rung>h pictures by eye (the text is the quickest test).

The glyph verdict (automatic). Each rung's hi-res look (native size; 1280 wide from a kext before 2026-10-26; <report>-scanoutladder-<rung>h-pipe<N>.ppm, or .png when rdna3vm result had no xz and packed PNGs; next to the archive) is read by tools/glyph_verdict.py (pure Python, PPM and PNG via tools/lookimg.py; the triage prints it in the ladder <rung> row as glyphs (automatic, tools/glyph_verdict.py): ..., and the ladder glyphs row says which rungs made the baseline's broken text clean). Terminal prints a tick line a second that ends in the same text, in one colour; drawn right, two such lines are the same picture, drawn wrong (random strokes) every line is different. The tool finds stacks of at least 8 text lines of an even pitch in the look (colour agnostic, no knowledge of the window's place), keeps the lines that end at the same column (a log window, a listing: ragged ends, never taken for tick lines), cuts the right half of each (after the time stamp) as a black-and-white strip and counts the lines that have a TWIN (another line within 12 % of its ink; in a native look the tick lines are the same pixels, in an old 1280-wide look the 1920 -> 1280 sampling drops one row in three, so lines fall into three classes of rows seen and each class has several lines; the tool's line heights, pitches and margins scale with the look's width / 1280, so both kinds are judged alike). CLEAN = at least 80 % of the lines have a twin, BROKEN = at most 50 %, unclear between, none = no tick stack (the text window was not on a screen: the rung proves nothing; card run 2026-10-26b: all 13 rungs none, the pictures show Chess on top of the tick Terminal, which the ladder opened first; it now opens Chess first, so the Terminal is the window in front: open the pictures before trusting a none). Every stack on the screens is judged and the WORST decides: a window that Terminal restored from its saved state shows the text of the previous rung, static and drawn clean, next to the live one (the ladder now opens Terminal with -ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES, but a look of an older run has them). Read the numbers: live broken text 9 to 30 % of the lines have a twin; static (restored) text 95 to 100 %. Checked on the card run 2026-10-25f's looks (tests/test_glyph_verdict.py, also on synthetic looks).

Card run 2026-10-25f, read with it. All seven rungs' LIVE windows are broken: baseline 5 of 23 lines have a twin, radv 2 of 23, sync 2 of 23, managed 2 of 23, alignguard 2 of 21, paranoid 2 of 23, loadbarrier 7 of 23. The one clean-looking paranoid picture (the montage of the rungs' looks) is the window Terminal restored from the alignguard rung (ticks of 14:03:2x to 14:03:38, 19 of 19 lines with a twin, static text); the live paranoid window (ticks 14:04:21 to 14:04:43 on pipe 0, ...paranoidh-pipe0.ppm) is as broken as the baseline (the glyphs from the eighth letter of the alphabet on are random strokes). So NVMTL_PARANOID does not cure the text, and none of its 15 switches can be the cure by itself. What the logs add (Terminal, baseline slice): no buffer was parked or reused, no texture was made from a heap, no buffer-backed texture was made, the residency pager never ran, nothing was purged, a single submit was ever in flight; the code of NVMTL_NO_BUFREUSE, NO_HEAPTEX_PLACE / RECYCLE, NO_BUFTEX_RECYCLE, NO_RES2, NO_PURGE_RECLAIM and GSUB_INFLIGHT=1 never ran or changed nothing there (the earlier ladder-paranoid run, replaced by ladder-glyphs, had a ladder knobs row for that). The managed rung ran with the snapshot on (121 of 121 uploads snapshotted, 119 checked, 0 changed): the CPU did not rewrite a managed range while its copy was in flight. Static text is drawn right and live text is not: look at what changes per frame (the per-frame uploads, the framebuffer-fetch blend over the LOAD of the window's surface), not at what the knobs reuse.

The glyph hunt in one run: rdna3vm step ladder-glyphs

Card run 2026-10-25f: the live text of Terminal is broken in every rung, paranoid included; text drawn as a whole window (restored by the next Terminal) is clean. ladder-glyphs (rdna3ladder=3, --ladder-glyphs, 13 rungs of about 95 s, 25 minutes; the former ladder-paranoid is gone, its rungs are in commit 90da0d0) tests the suspects that are left. Hypotheses: A = the app writes managed-buffer bytes outside its didModifyRange (Apple's drivers copy whole pages, ours is byte-exact); B = the incremental path (managed buffer, then Apple's buffer_image_blit_* shader or a framebuffer-fetch draw into the retained window surface) is wrong where the whole-window path is right; C = the CPU/GPU coherency of the window IOSurface shared with WindowServer fails (page-off, surface-in seed); D = a copy with one image on both sides (fixed, see below). Each rung's knob is read by the plugin with the value the rung gives (the host test compares them with the source). What a CLEAN rung (baseline BROKEN) means is printed by the triage as ladder glyphs <rung>:

Rung (knob)Clean means
managed-whole (NVMTL_MANAGED_WHOLE=1)the GPU needs bytes the app did not declare (A). Run managed-pages and read undeclared writes
managed-check (NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1)the detector (hash + mirror at the flush) changed the timing: a race between the CPU and the upload
no-surface-share (NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_SHARE=1)the window surface shared in family VRAM is where it breaks (C)
no-surface-pageoff (NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_PAGEOFF=1)the VRAM-newest / page-off protocol is where it breaks (C)
fbfetch-zero (NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1)the framebuffer-fetch draw is the source of the strokes (B). A clean zero-fetch text has no half-blended edges, so also read the picture
managed-pages (NVMTL_MANAGED_PAGES=1)whole 4 KiB pages are enough: the undeclared bytes lie in pages the declared ranges touch (A, Apple's page granularity)
managed-eager-zero (NVMTL_MANAGED_EAGER_ZERO=1; =1 is the OLD memset + upload at creation)the creation-time clear of managed buffers matters
no-texbuf, no-bindless, dontcare-clears (NVMTL_DONTCARE_CLEARS=1), skip-empty-0 (NVMTL_SKIP_EMPTY=0)that path is the cause
paranoidthe NEGATIVE control. Clean here = the run is not comparable (ladder glyphs control WARN): the live-window identification or the artefact is unstable

The verdict of a rung looks at the window that CHANGED between its two hi-res looks (...h and ...h2, three seconds apart; the text script prints a line a second): a tick-line window that did not change (a restored one, another rung's, a frozen screen) is listed as STATIC ... ignored and never decides; no live window at all is none (ladder glyphs none names the rungs).

A rung only counts if its knob was ENGAGED. A key that reached the plugin (vk: ab switches) can still change nothing: the feature was already off, or the app never uses it. The triage compares the Terminal's effective-state lines and counters with the baseline's and says knob engaged (...) or KNOB NOT ENGAGED: <why> (WARN; "engagement could not be shown" when a line is missing): no-surface-share / no-surface-pageoff / managed-eager-zero / no-texbuf / no-bindless / skip-empty-0 (the item 7: ..., after a GPU write ..., zero-fill: ..., texture buffers ON/OFF, bindless textures ON/OFF, commit: empty submission parts ... lines, and commit: N empty submission parts NOT submitted in the baseline), managed-whole / managed-pages (widened_uploads > 0 or ud_whole_marks > 0), managed-check (scans > 0), fbfetch-zero (zr_forced > 0 in vk: fbfetch: SUMMARY), dontcare-clears (dontcare_cleared > 0 in vk: ab: SUMMARY; dontcare_seen counts every don't-care attachment), paranoid (the profile line). A clean rung that did not engage is a WARN, not a cure, and the "no knob is the cure" sentence lists the engaged rungs and says the others exclude nothing. Every rung, the baseline too, must also show that ITS Terminal (the pid in pids:) logged vk: ab switches in the slice, otherwise the rung "proves nothing"; a missing log slice, a Terminal that did not start, a look whose exit: was not 0, a log slice cut at 600000 bytes (the END is kept), ppm files that were not sent (push: N ppm file(s) not sent), a run ended by a signal (ABORTED by SIGx at rung N) and a tick text script of an earlier run still running are all WARN rows. A rung with a hires look and no glyph verdict is WARNed (never left out).

The detector's blind spot and what WHOLE covers. The detector watches a managed buffer from its first didModifyRange or its first -contents call (the only way an app can write a buffer without ever declaring a range), so a buffer written only through a cached contents pointer is watched too, from the moment it was first read. A buffer that was neither declared nor read is not watched: ud_managed counts the managed buffers made, the triage row says how many were never watched, and softens "hypothesis A is not supported" to "NOT excluded" when a third or more were blind. NVMTL_MANAGED_WHOLE=1 also covers the buffers that never declare a range: at every submit a watched buffer whose shadow differs from the mirror is marked dirty and uploaded whole (ud_whole_marks), as Apple's copy of "everything" would. NVMTL_MANAGED_PAGES=1 only widens the ranges the app DID declare (Apple copies what is modified, in pages; a buffer with no declared range is not copied there either). The detector perturbs timing: with NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK (and NVMTL_MANAGED_WHOLE) every flush hashes the uploaded range and compares/copies the whole shadow against the mirror, every submit scans every watched buffer; a Terminal at 256 KB x 3 buffers pays tens of microseconds per submit. A rung that comes out clean with managed-check alone means a race the timing hides, not a missing declaration.

Summaries are timer-driven. One 10 s timer (armed when the device is made) prints managed:, census:, vk: ab: and vk: fbfetch: (the surface-in cache has its own), each only when a counter moved; the last tick before a signal is the last evidence (a signal skips atexit). The surface-in SUMMARY is two lines (SUMMARY and SUMMARY2 with the same seq; nvlog cuts at 1023 characters); the triage merges them. Rows carry the process and pid (Terminal[907]).

What the new counters say (all in the plugin log of the process; the triage prints a row for each):

Line / rowMeaningAction
managed: SUMMARY ... whole=W pages=P decl_bytes=.. up_bytes=.. widened_uploads=.. ; row managed upload (WARN while whole/pages is on)bytes the app declared against bytes uploaded; widened_uploads = uploads that carried more than declaredwith the knob off, up_bytes == decl_bytes
managed: SUMMARY ... ud_scans ud_idle_scans ud_hits ud_idle_hits ud_bytes ud_samepage_hits ud_late_bytes ud_buffers ud_gpu_off ud_nomirror ud_mirrors ud_mirror_bytes (needs NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1); row undeclared writesthe detector keeps a mirror of each managed buffer's shadow as the last scan saw it (zero at creation); at every flush it compares the shadow OUTSIDE the declared range with it, and at every submit it scans the buffers that have no range at all. ud_bytes = bytes that changed without a didModifyRange; ud_late_bytes of them were declared at the NEXT flush (another thread's submit saw them first: a race, not a missing declaration); ud_samepage_hits = hits in a page the declared range touches; ud_gpu_off = buffers the GPU writes back (left out); ud_nomirror = buffers over 8 MiB or past 64 MiB of mirrorsud_bytes - ud_late_bytes > 0: A is real, the upload leaves those bytes stale in VRAM; managed-whole / managed-pages rungs say whether the GPU needs them. 0 with thousands of scans: A is excluded for these buffers
managed: UNDECLARED CPU write in a N-byte buffer (scan at the flush / at a submit): K byte(s) changed outside the declared range, at offsets A..B, the nearest D byte(s) from itthe first 4 hits of a buffer (64 lines per process)the offsets say which part of Terminal's 256 KB buffers is written without being declared
vk: same-image copy WxHxD fmt F from (x,y,z) level L slice S to (...): no overlap, both sides in GENERAL / OVERLAP, through a scratch imagethe first 8 (then every 1024th) copies with one image on both sides; vk: ab: SUMMARY ... same_image_copies same_image_overlap same_image_general same_image_tmp same_image_tmpfail same_image_unsafe; row same-image copy (WARN when an overlapping copy had to run in place)same_image_overlap > 0 in Terminal: it copies a region of its window onto itself (a scroll), see the fix below
census: SUMMARY seq=.. t_ms=.. surfaces=.. sin_up_vram sin_up_priv sin_up_mb sin_skip sin_fail pass pass_clear draws draw_blit draw_fbf blitenc replace compute present out_dirty out_copy out_fail top=ID:WxH:V|P:u..,P.. ; row surface censuswhat touched the process's IOSurface render targets: surface-in uploads (u family VRAM, U private; the CPU copy was newer than the VRAM tag, i.e. a CPU lock, a window capture or the display pipe moved the seed - the only trace of a page-off this process sees), skips s, render passes P (cleared c), draws D, Apple's buffer_image_blit draws B, framebuffer-fetch draws F, blit-encoder copies E, replaceRegion R, compute writes X, presents T, GPU writes published as a dirty mark O (VRAM newest: the next CPU lock pages it off) or copied out C. V = the surface lives in family VRAM, P privateTerminal's window: B and F with u > 0 on the same surface means the CPU copy was uploaded between GPU draws (the mix of C and B); u = 0 and O high means the CPU never touched it
ladder quit row, quit: pid N ended by SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGKILL after Nshow every instance ended; WARN when one survived SIGKILL

Reading it. (1) The control paranoid must stay broken, the baseline broken (else the run proves nothing). (2) undeclared writes in the baseline Terminal: bytes > 0 and managed-whole clean = A, confirmed; bytes > 0 and managed-whole still broken = the undeclared bytes are not what the GPU lacks (the app does write them, Apple's copy would carry them, and the picture does not need them); bytes = 0 over many scans = A excluded for these buffers. (3) no-surface-share / no-surface-pageoff clean = C. (4) Everything broken: the cause is none of these knobs; the census says whether the CPU ever touched the window surface between GPU draws (u on a V surface), fence early says whether the kernel's fence completed before the GPU's writes were visible (WindowServer 5 times in 91994 witnessed submits, worst 867 us late, Chess once, in 25f: a CPU readback right after a fence, e.g. a page-off copy, could see stale bytes - a kernel visibility suspect that no rung here switches; NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0 / the sync rung of ladder is the nearest plugin-side check, and the count of early fences per process is the number to watch), and the host probe tests/vk_same_image_probe.c / tests/vk_meta_probe.c say what RADV does on the dev host.

The same-image copy (fixed in nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_image_sub3). A copy with one VkImage on both sides moved the image to TRANSFER_SRC and then to TRANSFER_DST and recorded vkCmdCopyImage with TRANSFER_SRC for the source, and an overlapping region (a scroll by N rows) is undefined in Vulkan. Host probe tests/vk_same_image_probe.c on the RX 7800 XT (RADV 26.2.5, DCC on and RADV_DEBUG=nodcc, 1024x768 RGBA8, 400 to 800 random copies per mode): disjoint copies are right even with the old layouts, an OVERLAPPING copy in place (old layouts or GENERAL) leaves wrong regions (dozens to thousands of pixels) in several readbacks of every run tried (seeds 2 to 7, 1 to 16 operations per command buffer, DCC on and off), and the new sequence (GENERAL on both sides when the regions are disjoint, a scratch image when they overlap) never. If Terminal scrolls its window by copying it onto itself (the same_image_overlap counter of its vk: ab: SUMMARY says), scrolled text was a candidate; it does not explain the progress Terminal (tail -f, not yet scrolling) or the glyphs of card runs 25..25c, which stay open.

Encode time (kind 17 records, tools/plugin_trace.py, the same report): WindowServer, 3948 render encoders and 26152 draws in 101618: 4256 ms in encoders, of which the surface-in checks are 1605 ms (38 %, 104 us per call; 81 % of it in 205 encoders that include a real upload, 465 uploads of 856 us each = 398 ms; in the 3533 encoders without a big upload the calls average 43 us: a lock, a seed query and a VRAM tag check per bound texture and draw), descriptor writes 99 ms (4 us per draw), pipeline lookup 27 ms (1 us per draw); the other 60 % is time the split does not cover (the app's own calls, the recording of passes, framebuffers, views, 32768 images made and destroyed in 13 minutes). Terminal's first encoders spend 95 to 350 us per draw in descriptor writes (the first draw of a process builds the pools and sets), later ones 4.5 us.

Kernel coherence switches (N54): rdna3vm step ladder-coh

Card run 2026-10-26b: every plugin switch (ladder-glyphs, PARANOID included) leaves Terminal's live text and Chess's pieces broken, so the layer below is next: the kernel's GPU memory ordering and caches. Each rule can be turned up to its strongest form at runtime (no reboot), one sysctl each (common/rdna3_coh.h; the boot-arg rdna3coh=<mask> sets them from the start; default 0 = the driver before N54):

sysctl debug.rdna3_coh_...what it does (the amdgpu function it strengthens)if the text is clean with it
fence_systhe CPU trusts the system-memory copy of the ring fence (amdgpu keeps the fence in adev->wb, GTT) instead of the VRAM wb slota CPU read after a fence saw system memory before the GPU's writes landed: make it the default
fence_invthe end-of-job RELEASE_MEM also invalidates GL2 / GL1 / GLV (gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_fence only writes back)a stale GPU cache line survives a job
pipe_syncevery job waits for the previous one's fence (gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_pipeline_sync; amdgpu emits it for same-ring dependencies, which this driver never tracks)two jobs (two processes sharing a surface) overlapped on the ring: implicit sync is missing
wait_idlethe CS ioctl returns after its own fence (CPU wait with the DRM lock dropped, 200 ms bound: other clients go on)as pipe_sync, or the CPU acted on a job too early
ucnew user mappings MTYPE_UC (gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte's override for every BO). Only mappings made AFTER the switch: the rung's new Terminal / Chess get it, WindowServer's existing mappings (its windows, the surfaces it imported before) stay NCthe GL2 holds stale lines of a surface another VMID or the CPU wrote
hdp_endan HDP flush after the IBs too (HDP 6.0 has no read cache: amdgpu has nothing to invalidate)CPU writes through the BAR reached VRAM late
vm_flusha TLB flush at every job (amdgpu_vm_flush only when the VM changed)a stale translation after a map / unmap
sdma_gcrkernel SDMA copies (page-off, copy forward, clears) between GCR_REQ GL2 write-back+invalidate and an HDP flushthe kernel's copies read or left stale lines
all / maskevery switch at once / the switches as a maskall is the positive control

rdna3vm step ladder-coh (rdna3ladder=4, standalone sudo sh autoreport.sh --standalone --ladder-coh) runs the rungs baseline, coh-all, then one switch each (fence-sys, pipe-sync, wait-idle, uc, fence-inv, sdma-gcr, vm-flush, hdp-end), Terminal + Chess as new instances per rung, two hi-res looks, the switch set before the apps start, read back, put back after the rung (ladder.txt: sysctl: NAME=1 (was 0, read back 1), coh_start: / coh_end: = debug.rdna3_coh around EVERY rung, the baseline too; a put-back is read back, retried, and a WARN: sysctl ... NOT restored keeps it on the undo list). A rung counts as engaged only when it started with exactly its own flags (0 for baseline, all for coh-all: anything else is CONTAMINATED, e.g. a switch an earlier rung left on or the boot-arg rdna3coh) and its switch DID work during the rung (work_<name> grew: answers taken from the fence copy, pipeline syncs / HDP flushes / invalidating fences emitted, CS waits that really waited, UC mappings made, forced VM flushes of submitted jobs, kernel copies wrapped in GCR; a missing coh_start / coh_end is not engaged). The triage prints ladder-coh <rung> rows (flags, the work, the glyph verdict, the answer), ladder-coh put-back for a switch that did not go back, and ladder-coh verdict (only engaged rungs can name a cure; a baseline that is not a clean default gives no verdict): FAIL naming the switch whose text is clean while the baseline's is broken (make that rule the default after one confirming run), FAIL "only with every switch" (two rules: bisect debug.rdna3_coh_mask), WARN "broken even with every switch": the kernel's ordering and caches are excluded, the translator and the plugin are left. By hand: sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3_coh_pipe_sync=1, open a NEW Terminal (and Chess), look, sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3_coh_pipe_sync=0.

Every report (collect.sh 42-coherence.txt, triage rows coherence switches, fence copy, fence skew, wait idle, uncached maps, sdma gcr):

  • fence copy PASS: the gfx, comp AND sdma fences also land in a system page (self-test 1 on all three at bring-up and after every reset / wake) and fence checks read the copies. FAIL: a copy did not land (-1; kernel line coherence: <ring> ring: the fence's system copy did NOT land): that ring keeps the VRAM fence only. WARN: -2 (no system page or GART slot), -3 (the ring was not up), -4 (the ring did not run the self-test's fence at all), 0 (never set up), or no check read a copy. With fence_sys the kernel's own waits (the page-off / evict / clear copies on SDMA, gfx11_wait_fence) need the copy too within their bound; coherence work WARNs on kwait_fallback (a copy that did not land in time, logged). A reset's forced fence completion writes the copies as well (forced).
  • fence skew WARN: skews times a VRAM fence value was visible before its system copy, which the ring writes FIRST (coherence: <ring> fence N: VRAM says ..., its system copy ..., first 8 then every 1024th): the window in which the plugin's completion witness saw "fence early". The next run then sets fence_sys. PASS: none in checks checks. FAIL stale: a copy stayed 50 ms behind (broken page or GART slot); the VRAM fence answers there.
  • coherence switches WARN: a switch was on at the end of the run (not the default driver); the per-switch job counts say whether it ran under jobs.

Fixed in N54 without a switch (ports): the kernel's SDMA transfer window (page-off, evict / restore: hw/xfer.c) flushed only the MMHUB's TLB, while SDMA 6.0 translates through the GFXHUB (sdma_v6_0_sw_init: vm_hub = AMDGPU_GFXHUB(0); amdgpu_gart_invalidate_tlb flushes every hub): a copy larger than the window, or one after another copy with other pages, went to / came from the previous pages (sim: S.sdma_tlb_stale). And a BO created COHERENT / UNCACHED / EXT_COHERENT is mapped MTYPE_UC whatever its VA flags say (gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte; uc_bo counts them). And (ECC round 1) the CPU read every GPU-written 32-bit value (the ring fences, the IH wptr writeback) one BYTE at a time through the write-combined BAR mapping: a carry the GPU wrote between two byte loads gave a value no write ever had (0x1FF -> 0x200 read as 0x2FF: a fence up to 255 jobs early, once per 256 fences at most, when the read races the write): rdna3_vram_read32 is one aligned load, as amdgpu_fence_read. This is a second, certain candidate for the witness's "fence early".

The app survey: rdna3vm step apps

(N59: Safari's fourth stage frames opens the frame-order page and reads its strips from the scanout in two bursts, see "Animations step back to an earlier frame" below.)

After card run 2026-10-26 the driver draws Terminal and Chess on the card, and those were almost the only apps tried. The apps step opens fifteen real apps one after another with the default driver and plugin settings (no NVMTL_* switch, no sysctl, no conf edit, no WindowServer restart) and leaves evidence of each, so that one run gives the list of what is still visibly broken. It is ladder variant 5 (rdna3ladder=5, autoreport.sh --apps; the APPS block of tools/vm/macos/autoreport.sh), about 25 minutes. The apps, in order, and what each is given (the content is made by the run itself, offline; open is all it can do headless: key presses and menu clicks need System Events, which asks the owner for Accessibility / Automation consent, so there are none):

app (app <name> row)what is on screen when it is looked at
chess, terminalthe known-good references (Terminal: the ladder's colour listing and a tick line a second; the only app with an automatic glyph verdict)
texteditan RTF with fonts, sizes, colours, bold / italic / underline
calculator, notesthe app alone (Notes may show its welcome sheet)
safarithree stages in one Safari: text (CSS gradients, shadows, blur, a rotating and a fading box, SVG, a table, text from 9 to 28 px), canvas (a 2D canvas animation with a frame counter on the page), webgl (a rotating cube; the page prints the WebGL renderer string or NO WEBGL)
previewpdf (two pages: two fonts, an axial and a radial shading) and image (the PDF's first page as a JPEG made by sips)
mapsthe app alone (no network: the map stays empty; record what it shows)

appstore, photos, imagecapturethe app alone (they may ask for an account or show a welcome sheet: record what they show)

What the run records per app (41-ladder.txt, one == rung <app> block; the triage prints one row app <name>): the stages, a 320-wide look and a hi-res look of both pipes per stage (<report>-scanoutapps-<app>[-<stage>]a|h-pipe<N>.ppm, the result file has them as PNGs; the Terminal has a second hi-res look h2 for the glyph verdict), the pids and whether each was still alive after each stage (died: pid N was gone after stage X), which processes have the Metal plugin mapped (plugin:; Safari's WebKit GPU process is listed under helpers:, DRIVER-PLAN N1 says its sandbox may refuse the plugin), WindowServer's pid before and after (ws_before / ws_after; a change is WindowServer restarted: the survey waits up to two minutes for the desktop and stops if nobody is logged in again), the crash reports (.ips) written meanwhile (crash: lines; a report of the app or of WindowServer / Dock is summarised in 41-ladder-<app>-crash.log), and two log slices: 41-ladder-<app>.log (the plugin's NVMTL lines of the app's processes) and 41-ladder-<app>-sys.log (the app's own Metal / GPU / error lines, WindowServer's and the kernel's RDNA3 lines of the same minutes). Then the app is quit (SIGINT, TERM, KILL; an instance that was running before the rung is the owner's and is left alone).

preview-image and CoreImage's CIKL kernels (DRIVER-PLAN N60). Preview's JPEG view builds a CIColorKernel from source (_clampImageAlphaTo01); card run 2026-10-26k crashed in CIKernelReflection::consolidate there. In 41-ladder-preview-image.log look for the plugin's reading of that kernel's function: CI PLAIN REFL <fn>: functionType 5, returns ..., N argument(s) (or CI REFL / CI STITCH REFL) = arguments found, the image should show; CI REFL FAILED <fn>: no argument list could be read ... followed by CI REFL FAILED diag <fn>: <AIR line> lines = nothing parsed: CoreImage refuses the reflection and builds the kernel from its FOSL reflection alone (no crash is the point); the diag lines are the AIR shape to port next. A crash in CIKernelReflection::consolidate again means a new path answered an empty argument list: the .ips frames and the log line of the function name tell which.

How to read the result.

  1. rdna3vm result, then the app <name> rows of the triage (00-triage.txt). The first word after the app's title is the run's verdict: ran (alive at the end, no crash report), crashed (report: ...) (FAIL; the crash summary is in the row, the frames in 41-ladder-<app>-crash.log), exited by itself ... with no crash report (FAIL: killed by the system or quit; read -sys.log), did not open (WARN: the app is missing on this macOS, or never started: the row names the open exit code), was already running (WARN: the owner's own instance, nothing was quit), WindowServer restarted / WindowServer / Dock crashed (FAIL: the desktop session was rebuilt under that app: that app, or the one before it, is the suspect; read 23-windowserver-crash.txt and the kernel log of the run).
  2. A row that says only ran does not say it drew. Whether an app DREW and how is the screenshots' verdict: open the scanoutapps-<app>...h-pipe0.png of each app (and pipe1 for the second monitor) and compare with the Terminal and Chess rows, which are the references. Note per app: window missing, black / stale rectangles, broken glyphs, tile blocks, wrong colours, an animation that did not move between a and h (or play and later), a window that stayed after the quit.
  3. The WARN notes of a row that ran: the Metal plugin is NOT loaded in any process of the app (the app draws on Apple's software path or not on the GPU), no census: SUMMARY (it drew nothing through the plugin, or ended before the plugin's 10 s tick), Metal/NVMTL error, GPU restart/hang and VM fault with the first matching line (the line is in 41-ladder-<app>-sys.log; a VM fault with a gfx ring line is the kernel's: go to [Kernel coherence switches](#kernel-coherence-switches-n54-rdna3vm-step-ladder-coh) and the VM-fault rows of the same report), N other error/fault line(s) (counted, usually harmless).
  4. What stays broken goes into DRIVER-PLAN as one line per app with the screenshot's name; a broken glyph in an app other than Terminal is judged by eye, and the fix path is the glyph hunt (ladder-glyphs, ladder-coh) run with that app instead of Terminal.

The host tests are tests/test_apps_step.sh (the guest script on the host with stubs, under sh / bash / dash) and tests/test_triage_apps.py.

Surface-in cost and the cache (N51)

Card run 2026-10-25e, WindowServer: the surface-in checks were 38 % of the encode time (1605 ms of 4256 in 3948 encoders, 104 us per call). Joined by command buffer (the ENC_SPLIT records with the SURF_IN records) that time was: 466 surface uploads 399 ms (25 %, 855 us each, a 971x680 window surface re-uploaded per frame: the producer's copy-out moved the seed), ~12 ms in cheap seed checks (1.6 to 5.5 us per call) and about 1100 ms in calls of 30 to 300 us that leave NO record: buffer textures the GPU cannot alias (112 of the first 128, bytesPerRow not a multiple of 256) are private images re-uploaded from the buffer's bytes once per command buffer, each a synchronous submit and wait. Measured on macOS 26.4.1: IOSurfaceGetSeed 0.003 us, the tag query 0.8 us, the pending-write scan 1.3 us, the per-surface monitor 0.06 us: the decision itself was never the cost. The plugin now (all behind NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1, also in the NVMTL_PARANOID profile):

  1. answers a surface texture whose seed (and tag generation) did not move before the pending-write scan and the monitor (fast, misses counted by reason);
  2. skips a buffer texture's refresh when the bytes it would upload equal the bytes it last uploaded (a shadow, up to 1 MiB per image and 32 MiB per process; the upload reads the shadow, so it is exactly what the image holds; dropped after a failed upload and by every writer of the image: blit, replaceRegion, mipmaps, resolve);
  3. parks the private copy images by shape when their texture dies and reuses them (NVMTL_NO_BUFTEX_RECYCLE also turns this off).
LineMeaningAction
vk: surfin cache ON/OFF (NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=...)once per processOFF: this run does not show the default plugin
vk: surfin: SUMMARY seq=.. t_ms=.. cache=1 cimg_on=1 verify=N calls=.. sin_us_total=.. fast=.. fast_us=.. miss_unsynced=.. miss_seed=.. miss_gen=.. locked_upload=.. locked_skip_vram=.. locked_skip_pending=.. locked_skip_overwrite=.. locked_fail=.. bufcopy=.. bufcopy_dedup=.. bufcopy_uploaded=.. bufcopy_up_us=.. bufcopy_skipped=.. bufcopy_skip_us=.. bufcopy_noshadow=.. bufcopy_noshadow_us=.. bufcopy_failed=.. bufcopy_forgotten=.. bufcopy_skip_refused_layout=.. shadow_bytes=.. shadow_refused=.. verify_checked=.. verify_mismatch=.. verify_failed=.. rsample_by_layout=u,g,sro,src,dst,other cimg_new=.. cimg_recycled=.. cimg_parked=.. cimg_evicted=.. cimg_refused_size=.. cimg_refused_form=.. cimg_refused_full=..totals from a 10 s timer while one moved (WindowServer never exits cleanly) and at exit; the newest seq of a process counts. sin_us_total is the time in all surface-in calls, bufcopy_*_us the refreshes: the share is the "75 %" check. bufcopy_uploaded / _noshadow count successful uploads (with / without a shadow), failures are bufcopy_failedbufcopy_skipped high and bufcopy_up_us low = the saving; bufcopy_failed > 0 = WARN; bufcopy_noshadow ~ bufcopy = images over 1 MiB or the budget; bufcopy_skip_refused_layout > 0 = the image rested in TRANSFER_SRC / DST / GENERAL (a skip is only allowed in SHADER_READ_ONLY)
vk: surfin verify ON/off (NVMTL_SURFIN_VERIFY=N)NVMTL_SURFIN_VERIFY=N (default off, in vk: ab switches): every Nth SKIPPED refresh reads the image back, compares it with the shadow and uploads anywayvk: surfin VERIFY: the image of a WxH buffer texture ... DIFFERS from its shadow = a stale image the cache would have kept: triage FAIL surface-in cache; verify_checked > 0 with verify_mismatch=0 is the hard "no stale image" number
vk: surfin copy-image pool ON/OFFonce; the pool is off with the cache or with NVMTL_NO_BUFTEX_RECYCLE (cimg_on= in the SUMMARY)
buffer texture copy: no shadow for a WxH image ...the first 4, then powers of twothe image is uploaded every command buffer, as before

Triage row surface-in cache (INFO with the counts and the refresh share of the surface-in time; WARN for the kill switch, failed refreshes, no shadow, and a WindowServer without a SUMMARY; FAIL for a verified stale image) and row sampled layout (see below). The trace's ENC_SPLIT record now also says what the surface-in calls were (tools/plugin_trace.py: [buffer-texture refresh N uploaded X ms + M unchanged Y ms, K probe hit(s), U surface upload(s), monitor wait Z ms] and the surface-in calls (...) line of the summary block). If an artefact appears only with the cache on, NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1 in rdna3mtl.conf restores the old behaviour. Layout lie of render-encoder samples (found while reviewing the cache): a render encoder binds a sampled texture's view and records NO layout transition (only compute does, nvmtl_vk_cmd_prepare_sampled_image), while the descriptor declares SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL; a render target rests in TRANSFER_SRC after its pass (the render pass's finalLayout), a compute-written texture in GENERAL. The SUMMARY's rsample_by_layout counts the layout at every render-encoder sample and triage prints it (sampled layout). tests/vk_meta_probe.c with LIE=1 / LIE=2 (also leaves uploads in TRANSFER_DST) samples in render passes without the transition on the dev host's stock RADV 26.2.5 (RX 7800 XT, DCC on and RADV_DEBUG=nodcc, 3 seeds x BATCH 1/16 x 200 ops, 1920x1080 BGRA, RGBA, R8, sRGB-through-UNORM, 4x MS): no wrong pixel (RADV's DCC state does not depend on the layout on gfx11). The plugin's own Mesa d381c361 was not compared. What is NOT cut: the surface uploads themselves (a producer that rewrites the surface every frame; there is no dirty range, only the seed), and the synchronous submit + wait of every upload that is still needed (the next step would be to record small uploads into the command buffer instead).

Linux reference dump (tools/linux/card_dump.sh)

sudo bash tools/linux/card_dump.sh on the Linux host (amdgpu, idle desktop) gives ~/Downloads/rdna3-card-dump.tar.gz; ~/Downloads/rdna3-card-dump.progress names the last phase if the machine hangs. Use it as ground truth:

Linux reference of this card (dump 2026-10-10, kernel amdgpu, idle, both monitors)

  • SMU feature mask 0x0003ebbc71ffffff: the same as ours (SCPM runs the same features whatever we allow).
  • Idle: ~12 W, GFX activity 7-10 % (gpu_metrics), GFXCLK 135-226 MHz (SCLK deep sleep 40 MHz), UCLK 96 MHz, FCLK 601 MHz with two 1080p60 heads.
  • GFX clock gating flags: MGCG, CGCG, CGLS, REPEATER_FGCG (= ours) plus BIF MGCG, HDP shutdown, IH CG, ATHUB MGCG/LS (not ported yet).
  • DC watermarks (us): set A urgent 17.32, sr_enter 59.72, sr_exit 52.74, dram_clk_change 417.32; set C dram_clk_change 67.32. DCFCLK 199 MHz, DISPCLK 215 MHz, DPPCLK 212 MHz. OTG 2591x1127 total (1080p60 CVT-RB).
  • amdgpu runs the MES (uni_mes=1); our default is the legacy RB0 ring.
  • Our activity was already 46-49 % at level 5 (before any GFX bring-up of ours), so the PMFW's busy reading comes from the RLC/GFX state, not work.
  • rdna3_vktest on Linux RADV: ALL PASSED 8/8 (the test itself is right).

Card run 11 (build #90, 2026-10-10)

  • Step 8: rdna3_vktest ALL PASSED 8/8 on the card (RADV built in the VM).
  • 8u / 9cu (rdna3pm=2): UCLK 96 MHz, 52-54 W instead of 77 W; the user reported no flicker on 9cu.
  • Step 10: the accelerator starts; WindowServer picks it, makes three 1920x1080 surfaces in VRAM, but RD3DisplayPipe::initFramebufferResource: scanout gate CLOSED - nothing reaches the panel: first head off, second black with the hardware cursor. Next: step 10s opens the gates from boot-args (rdna3accelfb=3 rdna3acceliop=1).
  • PM trend: RLC_GPM_STAT 0x00940016 (GFX power/clock on) at every sample, activity 100 %. The sample itself reads GC registers (which wakes the clock), so it does not prove CGCG never engages; still open.

Card run 12 (build #91): step 10s

Same black desktop - but the run used the OLD accelerator (accelerator build FAIL 1e8fedbbfc): the queue's first boot installed the new bundle, after rdna3vm approve had already run, so nothing was waiting for approval yet. autoreport now opens Privacy & Security with a dialog right after it installs a new bundle on an interactive boot (approve / window).

Card run 13 (build #91, step 10s with the new accelerator)

The boot-arg gates work: iop ... hooks OPEN, the display machine has head 0's pipe (initFramebufferResource ... over the LIVE SCANOUT), WindowServer swaps frames - but every swapped frame is empty (SWAP PROBE ... nonzero 0/32400), getApertureMemoryDescriptor: aperture gate CLOSED, and WindowServer restarts in a loop (PID 153, 299, ...): black screen, no cursor. Next: 10a (rdna3accelfb=4, aperture open); the report now carries the WindowServer crash reports (23-windowserver-crash.txt). The Metal plugin (RDNA3MTLDriver.bundle, Stage 6) is not installed in the VM yet.

Card run 14 (step 10a): the desktop through the accelerator

With the aperture gate open (rdna3accelfb=4): getApertureMemoryDescriptor -> over BAR1, SWAP PROBE 3 ... HAS CONTENT, rd3AccelFlipTo: FLIPPED, FRONT SURFACE ... A LIVE WRITER IS PAINTING - the user saw the desktop on the GOP's monitor; no WindowServer crash in this boot (the .ips reports in it were from run 13). Next: 10m, both heads; boot gates now apply per head.

Card run 15 (step 10m): two heads piped, swaps refused

Both framebuffers got a display pipe (heads piped 0x3), WindowServer composited (swap a2 ... HAS CONTENT), but the legacy swap path (RD3Surface::submitSwapCopy) names no display, and with two heads the flip/copy refused (no way to tell whose surface this is); WindowServer never used performTransaction (which knows the pipe). Both screens black with the cursor. Fix: rd3AccelSwapToPanel resolves the head (only head piped / argument is the pipe resource / shares its vidmem / surface seen before / next unclaimed head) and logs swap: surface ... -> head N (how). From now on one step per round: big.

Card run 16-17 (step big, scanout pictures)

Both heads routed and flipped (run 16). Run 17's pictures: both pipes lit with the macOS login screen, but at the end both scanned the first monitor's surface, and the user saw the second monitor switch between its own picture and the first one's. WindowServer re-creates pipe resources and swap surfaces; the router matched stale pointers. Fixed in 2bcd676 (surface keeps its head, freed objects leave the tables, route changes logged); the report now looks three times, 20 s apart.

Card run 18 (step big2, CI build 95, Metal plugin installed)

Every part ran (serial log: scanout looks, modetest, DMA/GFX, vktest, mtlprobe reaching MTLCompilerService, soak, hang test), the VM shut down by itself, but no report reached the host: the tests file was uploaded only after collect.sh's archive, and collect.sh did not finish. Fixed in 93cd123 (tests file and pictures first, collect.sh 360 s, rdna3vm result shows the upload server's log). Power from the serial log: baseline 47 W at 2.7 GHz, GFXOFF / POWER_SAVING / HDP+ATHUB CG change nothing (48-56 W), GFXCLK soft max 500 MHz gives 21 W, the idle cap holds 15 W at 539 MHz (Linux: 12 W); activity reads 100 % throughout. Hang test: the DMA hang was detected and SDMA0 soft-reset, but the follow-up jobs ran on the guilty client's connection and got -ECANCELED, so the GFX hang never ran; each job now has its own connection.

Card run 19 (step big2, CI build 96): the GFX hang froze the host

Scaled modes (1600x900 and 960x540 on each head, then native), display sleep/wake, DMA/GFX, vktest, mtlprobe and the soak all ran. The hang test, now with one connection per job, detected and recovered the DMA hang, ran the next DMA job, then submitted the GFX hang: no hang: GFX line followed and the whole computer froze (the serial log ends in zeros: never written to disk). The GFX hang is now its own step (hanggfx, rdna3hangtest=2); big2's hang test is DMA only. Every recovery step is logged with 100 ms for the log to drain before it runs, and the host fsyncs the serial log every 2 s, so the next attempt shows the step that takes the bus down.

Card run 20 (step big2, CI build 97, Metal plugin installed)

Everything but Metal passed: three looks and display sleep/wake (both pipes blanked, lit again), four scaled-mode switches and back to native (pipe 1 flags UNDERFLOW at 960x540, picture intact: watermarks for the scaled source are not programmed), DMA/GFX jobs, rdna3_vktest 12/12 (triangle, GFX and SDMA image copies, timeline semaphore), soak 283 rounds 0 failed, DMA hang recovered, idle 14 W. Metal lists the card ("AMD Radeon RX 7800 XT (RDNA3MTL over ...)", families up to Metal3); newLibraryWithSource failed in the plugin: no air-runtime/libLLVM.dylib in the bundle and no air-opt. The plugin now falls back to Apple's libLLVM in GPUCompiler.framework (the plugin's GL path already uses its C API).

Card run 21 (step metal, plugin with the system libLLVM)

The kernel compiled: Apple's front end, AIR read through GPUCompiler's libLLVM, translated. Pipeline creation then hit Assertion failed: (mutable_size && mutable_align), radv_CreateDescriptorSetLayout: RADV indexes pMutableDescriptorTypeLists with the binding's position after sorting by number, the spec ties list i to pBindings[i]; the plugin's layout has binding 640 before 480..607. Reproduced by rdna3_vktest on the host loopback; fixed in Mesa (patches/0007) and worked around in the plugin (its bindings sorted by number), so the VM's RADV need not be rebuilt for the next try.

Card run 22 (step metal): Metal compute passes

METAL: ALL PASSED: the kernel compiled from source ran on the card through the plugin (AIR read with GPUCompiler's libLLVM, translated to SPIR-V, RADV pipeline with the bindings sorted), 4096 words right, GPU time 0.012 ms. Vulkan 12/12, DMA/GFX verified in the same boot.

Card run 23 (step metal, build 98): Metal render passes

METAL: ALL PASSED with the rendered triangle (M3): vertex + fragment functions compiled from source, render pipeline, a draw into a 64x64 texture blitted back, 2016 red pixels, 0 wrong off the edge. rdna3_vktest this time hit its own mutable-layout check (added after run 21) and died on the same RADV assert after 8 PASS: the VM's RADV was built before patches/0007. Not a driver failure; rebuild RADV in the VM (build_radv.sh, which applies every patch) to clear it. The check now runs last in rdna3_vktest, so a RADV without 0007 only loses that line, and triage reports "CRASHED after N PASS" instead of "not run".

Card run 24 (queue modeset modeset2 gamma sleep hotplug, build 101)

Step modeset passed: each head offered 19 of its EDID timings (1080p72 at 210 MHz refused: DISPCLK 165 MHz, step modeset2 raises it), the cycle set 1680x1050@59.954 and 640x480@59.940 on both heads with their own timings ("own timing" in the fb log), the desktop in every picture, no underflow, back to native. The DLG reference clock the GOP's REF_FREQ_TO_PIX_FREQ implies is 24245 kHz, not 100 MHz: the scaling from the GOP's values absorbed it. Vulkan 13/13 (RADV rebuilt with 0007), Metal passed. The queue then stopped on the host: before modeset2 vfio-pci did not take the card (driver/unbind: No such file): amdgpu was still probing it after the previous step's restore. detach now waits for udev, sets the override first and retries; the queue retries a step that did not start once; rdna3vm result adds the queue runner's journal.

Card run 25 (queue modeset2 gamma sleep hotplug, build 101)

Step modeset2 passed: the DAL mailbox answered (PMFW 0x505c00, DISPCLK and DPPCLK up to 1950 MHz), the hard mins raised DISPCLK 165 -> 220 MHz and DPPCLK -> 215 MHz, 1080p@71.91 ran on both heads, the 60 Hz modes and native as before, Vulkan 13/13, Metal passed. The queue stopped again on the host: the passthrough of modeset2 was SIGKILLed 6 s after the restore's suspend/resume, gamma's detach found the card still not handed to vfio-pci after 5 tries, and the queue runner's journal ends with the next suspend. rdna3vm result now adds the host kernel's amdgpu/vfio/suspend lines of this boot and the one before; detach says which driver holds the card; the pause between steps is 60 s.

Cause (the host's kernel log): at the first detach amdgpu logged "finishing device" but its release ("ttm finalized") came 11 s later, after vfio-pci had the card - a DRM file was still open (a killed compositor waiting for its GPU work). The late release ran on vfio-pci's device; the vfio-pci unbind after the VM then oopsed in vfio_pci_core_sriov_configure, which SIGKILLed the passthrough, left the device locked (no vfio-pci for gamma) and froze the host on the next suspend. detach now waits for amdgpu's release (the kernel log, 30 s at most, killing every process that still holds /dev/dri or /dev/kfd) before vfio-pci may bind, and gives up rather than bind early.

Card run 26 (queue gamma sleep hotplug, build 101)

The host held (detach waited for amdgpu's release). Step gamma FAILED: no gamma line from the framebuffer or the core at all - IOFramebuffer never called setGammaTable, because the framebuffer did not publish IOFBGammaWidth / IOFBGammaCount. Pipe 1's CRC moved once and stayed (the cursor; the picture did not change), pipe 0's never moved. The framebuffer now publishes 12-bit x 256 with rdna3gamma=1 and logs the first call. The result was collected in the pause between two steps (queue still running, sleep hotplug left): rdna3vm result now says so, rdna3vm queue status shows the queue.

Card run 27 (queue gamma sleep hotplug, build 102)

  • gamma: ran on the stale framebuffer (498e4d3fdc; the new one loads from the Auxiliary KC only from the next boot), no result. autoreport now stops a step that installed new kexts and the host's queue runs it again.
  • sleep: display sleep/wake passed (both pipes blanked, both lit again). The system sleep woke on its RTC alarm with the card powered off (PSP sOS gone, both heads lost); with rdna3resume=1 the bring-up ran PSP, firmware and SMU and then hung for good: resumeAfterPowerLoss runs under fDrmLock, and pmInit's drmSetClocks took it again (IOLock, not recursive). gpuReset (from recover(), also under fDrmLock) had the same deadlock - the likely reason the GFX hang test froze the VM. The drm* setters now skip the lock when the reinit's caller holds it (drmHeldHere/drmHeldByMe).
  • hotplug: passed - DP-3 pulled, its pipe down; plugged back, EDID re-read and the pipe lit again; the framebuffer told; both pipes lit at the end.

Card run 28 (queue gamma sleep hanggfx, build 103)

  • gamma: the new framebuffer ran (IOFBGammaWidth 12 / IOFBGammaCount 256 in ioreg) and still no setGammaTable call. With the accelerator's display pipe (rdna3acceliop=1) the tables probably come in display-pipe transactions without a plane. Diagnostic build: the report shows the pipe counters around the gamma test, IOAcceleratorFamily2's transaction and gamma symbols and the first empty transactions dumped; step gamma0 runs the same test without the accelerator.
  • sleep: system sleep with the card powered off; the resume bring-up ran to the end ("resume #1 done, rings back up", the reinit deadlock fixed), head 0 restored and running, head 1 restored but its check says LOST (routing). The tests after the wake ran (drminfo, vktest) but nothing reached the host: the VM's vmxnet3 did not come back from S3. autoreport now restarts the interface after the wake, and results that could not go up are kept and sent at the next boot.
  • hanggfx: passed - GFX hang noticed, waves killed, GFX soft reset, the next GFX job verified (this step froze the VM before the deadlock fix).

Card run 29 (queue gamma gamma0 sleep, build 104)

  • The new RDNA3Accelerator (gamma diagnostics) waited for approval in macOS: the old one ran, no transaction dump. Needs rdna3vm approve.
  • gamma and gamma0: rdna3_modetest saw 0 displays (both pipes lit, WindowServer had both framebuffers open); run 28 saw 2. The report's daemon now runs rdna3_modetest in the console user's session (launchctl asuser) and the tool asks CoreGraphics again for 30 s. Display pipe counters: 65 plane-less transactions at boot, none during the (not run) gamma test.
  • sleep: two system sleeps in the boot, one with the card powered, one without; both times the GPU came back (resume with the full bring-up), head 0 restored and running, head 1 restored but its check says LOST (routing). Again no network after the wake: when nothing went up, the VM now restarts once and that boot only sends the waiting results.

Second monitor after the system sleep: no signal. Cause: the clone's standby (rdna3dpms=2, dcn32_clone_link) did not mark its link down, so the snapshot before the system sleep caught the clone with its transmitter off and the wake restored exactly that. The clone path now snapshots the running head before the link goes down and marks it (fHeadLinkDown), the wake brings back the link of every head macOS unblanked while asleep (displayLinkUp), and hwInit's displayMirror leaves a lit clone alone on a reset or resume.

Step gamma with the accelerator: plane-less transactions to the base class

Card run 30: with the accelerator's display pipe, macOS sends the gamma tables as display-pipe transactions without a plane (debug.rdna3accel_iop_empty +6: 3 tables x 2 heads) and never calls setGammaTable; without the accelerator (gamma0) setGammaTable reaches the framebuffer and the OGAM works. With rdna3gamma=1, RD3DisplayPipe::performTransaction now hands a plane-less transaction to IOAcceleratorFamily2's own performTransaction (IOAccelLegacyDisplayPipe's if it exports one, else IOAccelDisplayPipe's; weak imports, so a missing symbol cannot stop the kext loading) and returns its result. The next run of step gamma should show the accelerator log line iop: plane-less transaction handed to <class>::performTransaction -> 0x0, debug.rdna3accel_iop_empty_base going up with iop_empty, and then the framebuffer's first setGammaTable / core display: head N gamma lines and the CRCs moving as in gamma0. iop_empty_base_err going up (or the log line with a non-zero result) means the base refused the transaction; IOAcceleratorFamily2 exports no performTransaction means neither symbol resolved (the transaction is then answered as before). A refusal goes back to WindowServer as the base's error; booting without rdna3gamma=1 brings back the old answer.

Card run 31 (steps userq, pmctl, vcn, vcn2; build 90734eb)

These steps boot without rdna3fb, so macOS drives only the head the GOP lit, through its generic framebuffer: one monitor, named "Unknown" in Settings. That is expected for them, not a display regression.

  • userq: ADD_QUEUE type 1 doorbell 0xc00 PASID 6: 0, the compute user queue never fetched (rptr 0 of 14, dst 0), no VM fault. Diagnostic build: the ADD_QUEUE log line carries the PD, MQD, wptr (VA and GART MC), process and gang context addresses, and userq diag: lines dump the doorbell ranges and every active compute HQD of ME 1 before the REMOVE_QUEUE (no active HQD = the MES never mapped the queue; an active one with WPTR 0 = the wptr poll address; RPTR = WPTR > 0 = it ran but the fence path failed).
  • pmctl: rdna3_pmctl was missing from the CI artifact and from the VM's bin/, so the step tested nothing. Both fixed; run it again.
  • vcn (rdna3vcn=1, static PG): VCN[0] is not responding, giving up!!! (UVD_STATUS 0x00000004): the VCPU does not boot on the static path. Linux runs DPG on this card; vcn stays as a comparison only.
  • vcn2 (rdna3vcn=2, DPG): the VCPUs boot, both unified ring tests and the JPEG ring and IB tests pass, and --vcn-test's JPEG job from a client VM signals. Both encode IB tests time out (fence 2 timeout (last 0)), so no VCN_ENC ring is offered (--vcn-test reported PASSED on JPEG alone; it now fails without a VCN_ENC ring). Diagnostic build: a fence-only submission before the IB test (VCNn: fence-only test passed/FAILED) tells a fence the VCPU cannot write apart from an IB it does not finish, and a failed IB test logs rptr / wptr, UVD_STATUS, UVD_POWER_STATUS and the first session-buffer dwords.
  • vcn2 power-off: after system power off a nested panic ("Ticket lock ... unexpectedly owned") left the VM on a black screen. The VCN idle tick kept rescheduling itself (a fence never came back) into the halt; the power-off now stops VCN / JPEG first (amdgpu_pci_shutdown -> amdgpu_device_ip_suspend), log VCN: stopped for the power-off.

Card run 33 (steps userq, pmctl, vcn2, amdgpu; build 852bbef)

  • userq: the MES mapped the compute user queue (userq diag: ME1 pipe 0 queue 2 active: VMID 8, DOORBELL_CONTROL 0xe0003000, PQ_BASE 0x00004000, RPTR 0xe, WPTR 0x0_0000000e, WPTR_POLL ... 0x00401000): the doorbell, the wptr poll through the process VM and the fetch all work, the CP consumed all 14 dwords. Yet dst stayed 0 and the fence did not signal, with no VM fault. The test's RELEASE_MEM had no GL2 / GLM write-back, so its writes could stay in the GPU caches: it now uses the GCR bits of gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_fence. Diagnostic: before REMOVE_QUEUE the kext logs the interrupt counters and the last unclaimed IVs (userq diag: IRQs user queue N ..., unclaimed IV client ...): no CP EOP IV with the queue's doorbell means the EOP interrupt never came (or is decoded differently).

Fails were the test's: on PMFW 80.92 the FAN_CONTROL feature off is answered OK but the PMFW keeps the fan (mode stays auto, PWM refused) - amdgpu uses the OD fan curve on these boards; now reported as info. The fan curve point was 0 C / 5 %, below OD_RANGE (25..100 C, 15..100 %) and rightly refused; it now uses the middle of the range.
  • vcn2: VCNn: fence-only test passed: the VCPU writes fences. The first encode IB never completes (rptr stays at its start, 0x80; wptr 0x100). Diagnostic: instance 1 now runs the decode IB test first, instance 0 the encode one first, and the whole session buffer is zeroed (the ffffffff in the dump was never-written memory).
  • amdgpu (rdna3amdgpu=1 on the default profile): everything passed (displays, sleep / wake, mode switches, DMA, GFX, Vulkan, Metal), but the log had 662 GFX: ring full (rptr 0x0, wptr 0x700) / GMC: failed to write reg ... wait reg ...: the KIQ (on MES pipe 1) runs the TLB-flush packets but writes no rptr report, and with amdgpu's 256-dword padding the ring looked full after four flushes. The ring space now also counts from the last completed KIQ fence. The report archive did not arrive in time (collect.sh slowed by the 5 s waits): run the step again.

Card run 34 (step big4, build 0977b02)

  • Boot stalled (the user stopped it after ~1.5 min, no report): the serial log had 142 GFX: ring full (rptr 0x0, wptr 0x380) / MES ring buffer is full. / MES: failed to reg_write_reg_wait from the moment WindowServer started. With rdna3amdgpu=1 and rdna3mes=1 together the TLB flushes go through the MES scheduler ring (amdgpu_mes_reg_write_reg_wait), and the MES scheduler pipe, like the KIQ in run 33, writes no rptr report: each API call takes 2 x 64 dwords, so the 4 KiB ring looked full after seven calls and every later flush waited out its timeout. The ring space now also counts from the last answered QUERY_SCHEDULER_STATUS fence; the simulator reproduces it. Everything before (PSP, SMU, DMCUB with rdna3dmubreset=1, the clone, display power, GMC, IH, SDMA, KIQ / KCQ / GFX ring tests, MES up, VCN rings) came up. VCN1: decode IB test failed (-62) as in run 33 (diagnostic). Run big4 again.

Card run 35 (step big4, build c5aab01)

  • The MES fix held: no ring full / MES ring buffer is full in the serial log; the boot reached the Apple progress bar and WindowServer.
  • Kernel panic ~17 s into the desktop: NX page fault at a kernel data address called from IOAccelDisplayPipe::event_interrupt_gated, right after the first plane-less display-pipe transaction (iop: empty #0). With rdna3gamma=1 the accelerator hands such a transaction to the base class's performTransaction through weak imports; on 24H32 neither IOAccelLegacyDisplayPipe:: nor IOAccelDisplayPipe::performTransaction is exported, the Auxiliary KC bound them to a kernel data address and the gOSKextUnresolved test let the call through. A weak candidate must now also lie inside IOAcceleratorFamily2 (within 1 MiB of the strongly imported signalTransactionInterrupt); otherwise the transaction is answered as before and the log names the rejected addresses (iop: rdna3gamma=1 but IOAcceleratorFamily2 exports no performTransaction (weak 0x... / 0x..., unresolved 0x...)). The gamma test then fails without a panic: gamma through the accelerator needs another route. Run big4 again.

Card run 36 (step big4, build 7e883c9)

Root cause of runs 41, 45, 46 (analysis of all four AGDC runs)

  • The abort (41, 45) is not about K5's contents. Disassembled on the self-hosted macOS runner (same build, 15.8.1 24H32; tools/probe/coredisplay.sh): CoreDisplay_CreateDisplayForCGXDisplayDevice builds a CoreDisplay::Display, which first looks for a display pipe for the framebuffer (FindDisplayPipeForService, remembered at +0x14c) and then calls CreateMetalDevice(accelerator). When that throws, the catch logs "Failed to create MetalDevice for accelerator" and goes on if there is no display pipe, but asserts "Failed to create MetalDevice (%s)" if there is one. A display pipe exists only with an AGDC object up when WindowServer starts.
  • Our Metal plugin gives WindowServer no device: it is not on rdna3mtl-allow.txt. NullMoth's installer documents the same abort for an allow list WindowServer could not read.
  • Run 42 had the desktop only because AGDC came up after WindowServer (GPUWrangler: agdc 0x000000000); in 41/45 it shows /RD3DeviceControl. CoreDisplay's [ERROR] backtrace (Framebuffer ctor) is in run 42 too: harmless.
  • The hang (46): the object came up while WindowServer was starting (5 log lines after IOConsoleUsers), K5 off; WindowServer stopped after 9 vendor calls. NullMoth holds the registry busy (NVRM::fBootHold) until all heads published, the accelerator armed, K5 set, AGDC up and iop on, so WindowServer's IOKitWaitQuiet returns on a finished stack. We had no such hold, so where AGDC lands relative to WindowServer was luck (before: 41/45, during: 46, after: 42).
  • My earlier claim that NullMoth keeps K5 off was wrong: its NVRM writes debug.nvrmfb_agdc_k5=1 before debug.nvrmfb_agdc=1.
  • The report hid the cause: the crash file was cut at 24 KB before the asi (assertion text), and the WindowServer log filter dropped CoreDisplay's info lines.
  • Fixes: @WindowServer in the allow list (the plugin gives WindowServer a device only while debug.rdna3agdc reads 1), the plugin and list readable by _windowserver (build_metal.sh); the AGDC loader holds the registry busy until the object is up and its slot table settled 5 s (cap 40 s, RDNA3,AGDCBootHold); step agdc back to K5 from the start (rdna3agdc=3); collect.sh prints the crash's asi and 24-log-coredisplay.txt (CoreDisplay, IOPresentment, GPUWrangler, NVMTL, info level); triage shows the assertion and the plugin's WindowServer lines.
SymptomLikely causeAction
agdc: WindowServer restarts, assert "Failed to create MetalDevice"WindowServer got no Metal device from the plugin while a display pipe existsthe allow list has @WindowServer, mode 644; the plugin rebuilt with it (build_metal.sh); NVMTL: lines of WindowServer in 24-log-coredisplay.txt say why
apps mode (the %apps line of rdna3mtl-allow.txt, N1): an application misbehaves or the GPU address spaces run outevery application on the desktop now gets the device while the card is armed and WindowServer is on the plugin34-metal-allow.txt (triage "Metal apps mode"): which processes got it and why the others did not; the kernel log's VM: VMID n for <process> lines and VM: no VMID (15 VMIDs, one per open file; 7 with rdna3userq). Deny the app with -name, or rdna3mtlapps=0 / no %apps line for the old explicit list. Safari's WebKit.GPU cannot load the plugin at all (sandbox, card run 56): not a driver bug
agdc: WindowServer never opens the accelerator, no reportAGDC appeared while WindowServer startedRDNA3,AGDCBootHold must say "display stack armed"; "CAP" = the object or the table was late

Card run 47 (step agdc, rdna3agdc=3, build 177)

  • The new diagnostics named the next layer at once: WindowServer is allow-listed and the plugin's device initialises, but RADV inside WindowServer enumerates no GPU (vkEnumeratePhysicalDevices -> count 0, "NVK did not come up in this process"), so CoreDisplay still asserts "Failed to create MetalDevice" (16 WindowServer starts).
  • Why: the serial log has IOUC RDNA3UserClient failed MACF in process pid 157, WindowServer: WindowServer's sandbox refuses our DRM client class. NullMoth's NVK reaches its GPU from WindowServer through the accelerator: newUserClient type 0x4E56xxxx returns IOAccelNVRMBridge attached to a carrier, a class name the sandbox lets through. Our accelerator already had that path (type RD3K_UC_TYPE_BASE, carriers) but the core answered rdna3NewAccelClient with Unsupported.
  • Fix: the core makes an IOAccelRDNA3Bridge (an RDNA3UserClient with the device set explicitly, its provider is a carrier); libdrm_xnu opens RDNA3Accelerator with type 0x52440000 when opening RDNA3Accel fails. Only the core kext (OpenCore-injected) and libdrm changed: no approval.

No GPU hang or reset. The report kept neither which process owned VMID 3 nor why the later WindowServers died (the log's middle was cut, one crash file kept).
