Card run 50 (step agdc + probes, build 181)

  • Install boot (new bundle, then shutdown): kernel panic at shutdown, NULL read in IOAccelLegacySurface::clientClose+0x1cc (RAX 0xffffffdf) from iokit_client_died while WindowServer (pid 158) exited, after "VMID 2 of WindowServer freed". The test boot's shutdown passed the same path without a panic. tools/probe/iofam.sh disassembles the function on the macOS runner; the installs now run without the card anyway (rdna3vm _install).
  • Test boot: modes 4/4, display sleep/wake, gamma PASS on both pipes, Vulkan 13/13, Metal compute + render, A3, M4 on the screen (8470 magenta pixels), WindowServer on Metal for 9 minutes with 40 zero-copy flips.
  • The M6 cause, found with the VMID owners: Chess (pid 1571) got VMIDs 3 and 4, crashed 3 s after its launch, and its file was closed with a job still on the GFX ring: "VMID 3 of Chess freed", then the GFXHUB fault at 0x8001006a8000 on VMID 3 "which no client owns" (GRBM and CP busy). The job ran on into torn-down page tables; WindowServer's Vulkan device went down ("MTLTexture creation failed - vulkan down"), it aborted and every later WindowServer died with "Vulkan is not up". amdgpu never frees a VM under its jobs (amdgpu_flush waits for the contexts' entities, amdgpu_vm_fini for the VM's last fences, TTM frees the page tables only once the fences in their reservations signal). Fix: rdna3_drm_file_close waits for the file's last submission on every ring before it unmaps, destroys the VM and drops the BOs (the OS's fence wait: its 10 s job timeout recovers a ring that never gets there), and takes no new ioctls meanwhile (test_uapi).
  • Why Chess crashed in the first place is still open (its .ips was cut after the header in the tests file; the next run has the full exits).

Card run 51 (step agdc + probes, build 182, first install without the card)

  • The bundle went in through the headless boot (bundle-*.txt came back), the card step ran the new build straight away.
  • The M6 fix holds: no page fault, one WindowServer for the whole boot (run 50: seven), Maps ran on the plugin without a crash.
  • Chess (an OpenGL app on Metal) aborted in GLDDeviceRec::getBuiltinRenderPipelineStateFromDescriptor (MTLReleaseAssertionFailure): the plugin returned no render pipeline for the GL layer's present blit. Its plugin log stopped after four lines: the report kept only plugin lines that named RDNA3; collect.sh now keeps the plugin's log of every process (26-log-metal-plugin.txt).
  • mtlprobe FAILED in vkCreateComputePipelines: RADV's descriptor pool (VRAM with CPU access) got VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY. Only 144 MiB of VRAM are CPU-visible (BAR0 cut to 256 MiB for the VM), and a VRAM-only request had no fallback. amdgpu_gem_create_ioctl retries with GTT added; the UAPI does that now (its other retry, dropping CPU_ACCESS_REQUIRED, relies on TTM moving the BO on a CPU fault, which nothing does here). The kext logs how full both VRAM heaps were when one did not fit. Chess's pipeline likely failed the same way.
  • False FAILs fixed: M4 is decided by the screen (8238 magenta pixels), mtlprobe never gets presented callbacks on the flip path; M5 read lsof's name cut to "WindowSer" as "not loaded".

Card run 52 (step agdc + probes, build 183)

  • Metal compute + render PASS again (the GTT fallback), M4 and M5 PASS, Maps on the plugin, one WindowServer, gamma and modes as before.
  • Chess still aborted: the whole plugin log (26-log-metal-plugin.txt) shows the GL layer's shaders lowered and translated, then vkCreateDescriptorPool -> -2 before every pipeline, and the kernel logged no VRAM-full line. RADV makes its descriptor pools VRAM BOs with neither CPU_ACCESS_REQUIRED nor NO_CPU_ACCESS and maps them at once; the kext let such a BO land outside the BAR when the 144 MiB visible part was full, and a BO there cannot be mapped (amdgpu moves it into the BAR on the CPU fault, amdgpu_bo_fault_reserve_notify; nothing does here). Only NO_CPU_ACCESS BOs go outside the BAR now; the rest falls back to GTT. A refused map of a VRAM BO is logged.
  • Shutdown panic (run 50): probe run 4 showed clientClose loading the surface's accelerator (+0x12c8) at +0x1e and finding it NULL at +0x1c5; probe run 5 lists its writers: IOAccelSurface::stopLocked, ::stop and ::detach_surface. Probe run 6: stop takes lock_busy like clientClose (no race); detach_surface does not and runs from IOAccelSurface::didTerminate, i.e. when IOKit terminates the surface. Run 50's install boot had run kmutil load on the new accelerator just before its shutdown, which terminates the running instance while WindowServer's surfaces close. Fix: RD3Surface runs clientClose and didTerminate under one recursive lock (rd3AccelSurfaceGuard, logged when one waits), and autoreport no longer kmutil-loads a kext that is running (the new build loads from the next boot anyway; installs run without the card now).

Card run 53 (step agdc + probes, build 184)

  • M6: both apps ran on the plugin without a crash - Chess (OpenGL on Metal) too, after the GTT placement of CPU-mappable BOs; Metal compute + render, A3, M4 on the screen (8238 magenta pixels), M5, one WindowServer.
  • The result still said "something failed" for two probe exits that are not failures: mtlprobe's M4 verdict waits for presented callbacks the flip path never sends, and V3 records that RADV has no present path on macOS. autoreport now labels the M4 line "NO PRESENT CALLBACK ... the m4 scanout look decides" and exits 0 for both; triage ignores the probes' exit codes (M4 is decided by the screen in the summary).

Card run 54 (step agdc + probes, build 187)

  • Every reached check passed (M6 2/2), but the user saw three faults: System Settings' sidebar did not update while it scrolled (only after a resize), Chess's window was see-through with no title bar, Maps' text was misplaced.
  • WindowServer logged copy_forward_region: copy forward failed (0xe00002c7) ... disabling 813 times. Probe runs 7-10 (CoreDisplay, IOAcceleratorFamily2 on the runner): CoreDisplay calls IOAccelSetSurfaceFramebufferShape(surface, rects, 0x1000, fb); shape bit 12 goes to IOAccelLegacySurface::copy_forward, which calls the virtual submitCopyForward(event, fb index, buffer 0 = front, buffer 1 = back, rects, count). The family's own is a bare return kIOReturnUnsupported, and CoreDisplay ignores the failure: the back buffer keeps regions two frames old. RD3Surface now implements it (rd3AccelCopyForward: SDMA by rdna3CopyVram, the CPU for small rects; rdna3accelcf=0 restores the refusal).
  • Open question for the next run: WindowServer composites into per-process copies of its framebuffer IOSurfaces (G13 surface-in/out, 8-10 ms per 1920x1080 frame), so the next surface-out may write the stale copy over the kernel's copy. copy forward check #n ... KEPT / REVERTED in 27-copy-forward.txt answers it: KEPT means the fix holds, REVERTED means the copy forward has to reach the plugin's copy (or the IOSurfaces have to live in VRAM, item 7 for RADV).
  • The report gains 1280-wide scanout looks (--scanout-hires) of Chess, Maps, System Settings and the desktop, 27-copy-forward.txt, 28-plugin-warnings.txt, and logs collected for up to 180 s (45 s cut the plugin log off before Maps).

Card run 56 (step agdc + probes, build 191)

  • The screen went black but for what changed. 27-copy-forward.txt: every check read copied 0x00000000 ... KEPT. The copy reached the flip, but its source was empty: a legacy swap is presented by flipping its back buffer itself (zero-copy, rd3AccelFlipTo on a2), never by the swap copy into the front buffer, so buffer 0 (the front) never holds a frame and buffer 1 (the back) already holds the last one. Copy forward from a buffer that was never shown is now a no-op; it copies only from a buffer that went to a panel. Copy forward was therefore not what kept the System Settings sidebar stale.
  • Chess crashed in RADV: radv_shader_part_cache_get (NULL) from lookup_ps_epilog during glReadPixels' blit. The plugin makes depth and stencil state dynamic (Vulkan 1.3 core) without enabling extendedDynamicState, and RADV creates its PS epilog cache only with that feature. The plugin now enables VK_EXT_extended_dynamic_state, and mesa patch 0008 creates the cache always. The run before it drew Chess almost right (a few cyan slivers on the knights).
  • Point and line draws were skipped in WindowServer ("G14 draw: point/line primitives ... NOT drawn"): RADV reports dynamicPrimitiveTopologyUnrestricted = false. Mesa patch 0009 compiles pipelines with a dynamic topology for an unknown topology class (as with graphics pipeline libraries) and reports the feature.
  • Every host-pointer import was refused ("vkAllocateMemory refused the imported host pointer", including 14.7 MB buffers). RADV imports read-write, and WindowServer gets the apps' CoreAnimation backing stores mapped read-only. GEM_USERPTR now wires such pages read-only after a read-write refusal (the VA mapping drops write), and logs refusals with their IOReturn. This is the lead for the stale sidebar: the CPU-drawn layers reached WindowServer only as copies.
  • Buffer textures came back nil (glyph / icon textures: "bytesPerRow ... not a multiple of 32", "refused on memory types"): RADV cannot alias them as the plugin's NVK path did. A read-only buffer texture over CPU-visible bytes is now a private image refreshed from the buffer once per command buffer that uses it.
  • Known limit: Safari's GPU process (com.apple.WebKit.GPU) cannot load the plugin: its sandbox blocks mapping code from /Library/GPUBundles ("file system sandbox blocked mmap()"), SIP off or not. Apple's own GPU bundles live on the sealed system volume; M6 for Safari needs the bundle there or WebKit's GPU process off.

Card run 57 (step agdc + probes, build 8175274, 2026-10-15)

  • The HDMI monitor (head 0, the boot head: OTG0 -> DIGD -> UNIPHYD) said "no signal"; the DP-3 one (clone / extended head through a DP++ adaptor) worked and macOS made it the secondary display. Every look read pipe 0 LIT, its OTG CRC moving and the desktop in its picture; mode switches, display sleep / wake and gamma worked by the registers. The run before it (same build) showed the boot screen, then WindowServer never started (one-off).
  • The core's display log is the same as card run 56's (build a96c9a1, both monitors fine): the same route, clone (PIXEL_RATE 0x0 PHYPLL 0x1), snapshots, gamma tables, DPP-scaler mode switches (no timing change), no hotplug, the head-0 wake check intact, 0 regs differ (the snapshot holds the DIG front end's HDMI / packet registers, the TMDS controls and the OTG's V_TOTAL_MIN / MAX / CONTROL). The FreeSync batch (N6) changes nothing without rdna3vrr=1: no caps read (the mode list came back unsupported), no DRR, the HDMI SPD only from a zeroed stream. No cause in the driver's code or logs; the report could not say when the monitor went dark nor what left the DIG.
  • Diagnostic build: hw/dcn32_txstate.c reads what each head sends after its OTG (DC's DTN-log read-backs), the core logs it at the start, after the clone and every change (2 s, rdna3txwatch=0 off), publishes RDNA3,Head<n>,TX, and the screen looks print it (TX lines, RD_NR_XNU_TXSTATE); triage prints the lines, the warnings and a TX watch summary. The simulator showed the states it names (DRR bounds of 0, a DRR range, a DIO FIFO error, AV mute, the back end off, a DIG chain that cannot be followed). After the ECC review: a chain not followed is never "none" (every DIG's routing is read instead), the watch runs without the SMU metrics, skips a tick when the display lock is held (counted), counts its ticks (RDNA3,TXWatchTicks), always logs a change of the problems or of the routing / enables / clock selects, ignores status and self-clearing fields, and watches the clone of rdna3mirror=1 too.

Card run 58 (step agdc + probes, build 98582a0, 2026-10-15c)

  • The first run with the current Metal plugin and RADV (A3 and N1 on the card). The main (HDMI) screen kept an old frame - the wallpaper and the menu bar - while the windows on it (Terminal, Settings, Chess, the menus) flashed in for a moment; the DP screen looked fine (it only showed the static desktop). The looks agree: pipe 0 scanned out one buffer (0x800b100000, later 0x800bc50000) whose picture lacked the open Terminal and never changed in 2 s; all four M6 hi-res pictures of pipe 0 were byte-identical.
  • Cause: A3 made WindowServer's 1920x1080 compositing IOSurfaces GEM BOs over their own device-cache VRAM (GEM: IOSurface 1..4 ... at 0xd0f0000 / 0xd8e0000 / 0xe0d0000 / 0xe8d0000, each allocated by the import's wire). The legacy surface swap scans out other allocations - the IOAccelSurface's buffers (0xb100000, 0xb8f0000, 0xc0e0000, 0xc8d0000), which the family fills from the IOSurface's system memory (page-on through the aperture). No flip ever went to an A3 BO. The plugin's "surface-out -> dirty, no copy" left that system memory stale, so the panel showed whatever a CPU lock had paged off last (three 8 MB page-offs at 17:46:48, the early desktop).
  • Interim fix: WindowServer surfaces of a head's scanout size stay out of A3 (common/rdna3_a3_display.h, status RD3K_SS_DISPLAY, errno EPERM to the plugin, which composites them through copies as before A3); rdna3a3display=1 / sysctl debug.rdna3accel_a3display=1 lets them in again. The next run must show surfgem: ... refused: WindowServer's display framebuffer for the 1920x1080 surfaces, a3 display: head 0: ... kept out of A3, the plugin's import refused (... kernel errno 1 ...) - private image + copies for them, and pipe 0's M6 pictures differing (triage M6 screens, FAIL M6 screen otherwise).
  • The 45 VMFaults: Terminal (VMID 5, apps mode) TCP reads at an unmapped VA 0x80018223f000, 9 times from the 960x540 mode switch on (and after its VM was marked guilty), each followed by 4 CPC writes at address 0 on VMID 0 ("which no client owns"). At the first one WindowServer's GFX job hung for 10 s (soft reset, WindowServer restarted). Open (DRIVER-PLAN N27); triage now groups the faults (VM faults in the summary).

Card run 58b (step agdc + probes, build 751f5b8, 2026-10-15d)

  • With WindowServer's display framebuffers kept out of A3 both screens updated again, but windows and menus flickered on both screens. The result came without the report archive or pictures: the VM's 16-minute limit stopped the run inside M6 (no M6: line); the serial log and the tests file are all there is.
  • Evidence: WindowServer imported 615 of its other inputs as BOs (debug.rdna3accel_a3display_other 615: the menu bar strips 1920x24, status items 44x24, window backing stores), and its plugin wrote into those shared VRAM copies itself: surface-in SKIPPED (shared VRAM current at seed 3): 2 skipped, 747 copied - 747 uploads of the IOSurfaces' system memory into VRAM the owners also draw into through A3 (their "surface-out -> dirty, no copy" leaves that system memory stale), plus dirty refused: errno 19 copy-outs for surfaces the accelerator never drew. Nothing orders an owner's GPU or CPU write with WindowServer's upload, so a window shows its newest frame and an older one in turns. In run 58 (2026-10-15c) the same imports existed, but the main screen was frozen and the second static, which hid it.
  • Fix: every WindowServer import stays out of A3 by default (rd3_a3_ws_policy, status RD3K_SS_POLICY, EPERM to the plugin, which goes through copies as before A3); applications keep A3 for their own and shared surfaces. rdna3a3ws=1 lets WindowServer's inputs in again, rdna3a3=0 turns A3 off for every process. The kernel now measures flicker: per head and band of the picture the swaps that show the picture of two swaps before (flicker: lines, debug.rdna3accel_swaps_abab_h<N> / swaps_hashed_h<N>, triage flicker; second review: the baseline is a dump right before the probes, which run with the test on for every swap, and one band taking turns on one head - a blinking caret - is a WARN, not a FAIL). The run's VM limit grows by 5 min for the probes (M6), and the queue says when a step was cut by it.
  • GAMMA FAIL was the flicker (CRC before NOT stable, the dark table reached both pipes): rdna3_modetest now says GAMMA: UNJUDGED then. Display sleep FAIL was the system going to sleep after the display: the kernel blanked both heads first (head 0/1 blanked (display sleep), system sleep #1); autoreport keeps the system awake through the look.
  • The main display is the DP head (pipe 1: CG display 0's mode switch resized pipe 1 in both runs); triage takes the main pipe from that look.

Card run 58b, second run (step agdc + probes, build c010ebb, 2026-10-16)

  • The flicker stayed on both screens with every WindowServer input kept out of A3 (surfgem: ... refused: a WindowServer input, the display framebuffers refused): A3 was not the cause. The user stopped the VM about 1.5 min after the login (Esc x3), so the serial log is all there is; triage now reads its swap and flicker lines (swap/flicker).
  • Evidence: the flicker test fired on head 1 (120 of 204 hashed swaps, A B A B in 6 of 8 bands, 53 in more than one band) and later on head 0 (7 of 8 bands). After the login the user's WindowServer made its display surfaces with two back buffers (shapeSurface(mode 0x6): buffers 1 and 2; buffer 0, the front, is the head's framebuffer resource with its own VRAM, 0x87040000 / 0x88060000), and head 1's swaps took turns between 0x8b100000 and 0x8b8f0000 from swap #113 on; head 0 stayed on one buffer (few A B A B) until it too took turns. Card runs 15b-15d show the same turns; card run 56 (before eb5e3e8) used one back per head.
  • Cause: the accelerator flips the panel to the back (zero-copy), so the front never holds a frame, and since eb5e3e8 copy forward copied nothing when its source, the front, had never been shown ("the back buffer already holds the last frame"). That is true with one back only. With two, the back WindowServer draws next holds the frame of two swaps before; WindowServer redraws only this frame's damage and relies on copy forward for the regions the last frame changed, so each back kept them two frames old and the panel showed the two backs in turns. The cross head line at swap #84 ("head 0 now gets head 1's frame") was not a mis-route: the old WindowServer's surfaces were freed and the new ones got the same VRAM.
  • Fix (common/rdna3_swap_front.h, tested by tests/test_swap_front.c, which reproduces the A B A B with the old rule): copy forward takes the last frame from the buffer the head's last swap presented, only when it is one of the calling surface's own buffers, read through that live resource (never another surface's or process's frame), nothing when that buffer is its target (one back), nothing before the head showed anything (never the front then), and the front only when it was shown. A copy that left rects stale (or a call that copied nothing) leaves them pending for that buffer; the next copy forward into it copies them too, from a source that did not miss them itself (not reported to CoreDisplay, which would switch copy forward off for good). A swap never sends the front to the panel (it did when the back read empty); an empty back is shown once the head's last 3 swaps in a row were skipped (a black or clear frame; two empty backs taking turns count too). Per head the buffers shown are logged by phys (swap #n: head N shows a new buffer, pointers read <ptr>) and summed up with copy forward's outcomes and the flicker counts (swap: head N: ... swaps over K buffer(s) ...); every skip and refusal has its own reason line and counter: swaps_skipped_h<N>, swaps_frontskip_h<N>, swaps_emptyshown_h<N>, swaps_bufchg_h<N>, bufs_new_h<N>, swaps_skipped_all, swaps_nohead, cf_shown_h<N>, cf_src_h<N>, cf_backlast_h<N>, cf_nothingshown_h<N>, cf_foreign_h<N>, cf_miss_h<N>, cf_healed_h<N>, cf_calls, cf_nohead, cf_shown_stale, cf_refused, cf_same, cf_disabled (triage copy forward, swap/flicker).
  • The next run must show copy forward ... from the buffer it shows with cf_shown_h<N> growing on a head whose swaps take turns, cf_miss_h<N> no larger than cf_healed_h<N>, cf_foreign_h<N> / cf_nohead / cf_shown_stale at 0, no flicker FAIL (triage flicker / swap/flicker), and no flicker on the screens.

Card run 2026-10-17 (step agdc + probes, build 98b1abe)

  • About the 10th run where windows and menus flicker on both screens: a window shows for a moment, then the wallpaper where it was; the desktop is unusable. Copy forward works (head 1: 3080 from the shown buffer, 0 missed), the A B A B test sees 1 % (head 1) / 15 % (head 0): the old alternation is not what the user sees, and that test hashes a buffer only at its swap. Head 0 skipped 320 of 1030 swaps for an empty back. WindowServer crashed at 11:37 after a mode switch (the plugin's fence wait gave up after 5000 ms, GPU hang): a separate bug (N27/N28).
  • Code finding: rd3AccelFlipTo -> RDNA3Framebuffer::flipToLocked programs the HUBP address and returns; the flip latches at the next vblank, up to a frame later. submitSwapCopy returned at once, so WindowServer got the old buffer back (copy forward into it, the next frame paged on) while the panel still read it - a half-drawn frame, the wallpaper first. amdgpu sends the flip event from dm_pflip_high_irq once the flip latched and userspace reuses the old buffer only then.
  • Fix (diagnostic build, common/rdna3_flip_latch.h, tests/test_flip_latch.c reproduces the writes into the scanned buffer without the wait and none with it): the swap waits once, after its own flip, until it latched (1 ms sleeps, up to two OTG frames or 2.5 of the head's frames clamped to 35-100 ms; debug.rdna3accel_swap_latch / boot-arg rdna3swaplatch, default 1, 0 = the old behaviour for an A/B run). Counted per head: the previous flip pending at a swap's entry and the flip-to-latch times, copy forwards into the buffer on the panel, the shown buffer's band hash at its swap against the head's next swap (torn, or rewritten in place with one back buffer; debug.rdna3accel_swap_torn), buffers freed while scanned, empty backs read again 4 ms after a skipped swap. Triage: flip latch.
  • H2 (the flip before the GPU finished the frame): no mechanism in the default configuration. WindowServer's display framebuffers stay out of A3; its Metal composite goes through the plugin's private image, nvmtl_vk_submit_wait (the GPU job waited for), then surface-out reads it back into the IOSurface's system memory; the family pages that into the legacy buffer through the aperture (CPU) and copy forward copies by SDMA synchronously, both before the swap. No GPU-wait sysctl.
  • The next run must show flip latch with 0 of M swaps: the previous flip had not latched (swap_latch 1), no torn FAIL, and the user's verdict on the flicker. If it stays: the torn / in-place counts say which buffer is written on the panel; a run with sysctl debug.rdna3accel_swap_latch=0 (or rdna3swaplatch=0) gives the comparison.

Card run 2026-10-17b (step agdc + probes, build ad55679)

  • Both monitors mostly black, content now and then, everything flickers; the side monitor is fine for a while, then breaks too. The flip latch works (0 flips pending at a swap's entry, 0 timeouts): it was not the cause.
  • Evidence: head 0's legacy swaps name the head's framebuffer resource as a1 (the front, 0x87040000) and two backs as a2 in turns: 0x8b100000 (pixels) and 0x8b8f0000 (64 samples all zero at every swap, also 4 ms later). The family's aperture access - addToAperture, its page-on, the only CPU path into a legacy buffer - was logged for the first back only, never for the second (four log lines per resource allowed). 85 of 229 shown frames on head 0 were rewritten in place (WindowServer drew into the buffer on the panel), 14 + 10 buffers were freed while scanned. At the mode switch back to 1920x1080 the core refused VRAM (allocPhysical ... REFUSED (0xe00002db), the CPU-visible heap) and CoreDisplay logged copy forward failed (0xe00002bc) ... disabling - the driver's submitCopyForward answers only success (or 0xe00002c7 with rdna3accelcf=0): the family failed before calling it.
  • Code findings: IOAccelLegacySurface::submitSwapCopy(event, front, back) asks for a COPY of the back into the front (Apple's front is the scanout) and hands the back to WindowServer when it returns; the driver flipped to the back instead (H-COPY: the in-place rewrites, copy forward's own "shown buffer" rules, buffers freed under the panel). The front resource is not the scanout on this card: the family maps it into the GPU page table, which the scanout vidmem cannot be, frees it and gives it ordinary VRAM. Nothing in the driver writes a legacy back (copy forward targeted only the first back; no GPU engine renders into a legacy buffer), so the second back's emptiness is WindowServer's / the family's (H-B2: certain that no driver path fills it; why WindowServer names it needs the card - the trace below answers it).
  • Fix: common/rdna3_swap_copy.h (tests/test_swap_copy.c), swap mode 1 (default; debug.rdna3accel_swap_mode, boot-arg rdna3swapmode): the back is copied by SDMA into the scanout buffer the panel does NOT show (the framebuffer's own, "home", or the accelerator's second buffer D), the head flips to it (latched at vblank: tear-free), the back is copied into the front too (copy forward's source, as Apple's family expects); before the next copy the swap waits until that flip latched (bounded by the latch cap). An empty back is not copied over a picture (swaps_copyempty_h<N>, the first ones logged with where WindowServer's frame did land); mode 2 copies everything, mode 0 is the flip.
  • The per-frame trace (common/rdna3_trace.h, tools/rdna3_trace.py): every swap, copy forward, shape, VRAM allocation / free, aperture access, page-off, IOSurface answer, mode change, flip and what each HUBP scanned, in a ring of 32768 events (2.5 MiB) plus a per-second table; the core kext keeps a second ring of RADV's submissions, fences, waits, resets and GPU faults. tools/macos/collect.sh puts them in the archive (36-frame-trace.bin, 36-frame-trace-sec.bin, 36-drm-trace.bin, 36-frame-trace.txt); autoreport.sh streams both rings every 3 s through the whole run (<report>-trace-stream.bin, -drmtrace-stream.bin, -trace-sec.bin; snapshots go up after each A/B phase). The kernel log gets the first 300 events of a boot, 600 more at the A/B's end and 600 at the end of the tests (debug.rdna3accel_trace_dump; "tr" lines, triage decodes them when the files are missing).
  • The A/B (autoreport.sh, opt-in since card run 2026-10-17d: boot-arg rdna3swapab=1; it used to run in every accelerator step): 60 s in mode 1, a look (look ab1) and the counters, 60 s in mode 0 (look ab0), back. Mode 1 broke both monitors in the bisect, so the A/B deliberately breaks the picture for a minute. A normal step does none (== swap A/B: NOT ASKED; triage: swap A/B not asked, INFO frame trace: A/B not asked, no A/B did not run). Triage prints both side by side (frame trace: A/B mode N ...) with a verdict.

APERTURE v2 1-4, CS arg 0xffevery getApertureMemoryDescriptor return (gate, cached, no VRAM, withRange) and every refused AMDGPU_CS (drm: AMDGPU_CS from pid p refused (e))Q3 / Q9
== frame trace: <oid> NOT BINARY (tests file)sysctl -b gave text: the stream files cannot be decodedtriage trace incomplete: Q8 (... NOT a trace ...)
FAIL frame trace: A/B did not run / A/B inconclusive: phase X had n swaps on head h (need 20) / no MODESET to phase X / mode 0 never applied in phase 2the A/B is judged only from phases 1-2 events, with at least 20 swaps per head and phasean inconclusive A/B prints its verdict as INFO A/B verdict (NOT valid ...); == swap A/B: activity: Activity Monitor is NOT running explains a phase without swaps
trace incomplete: Q1 (... FEWER than the counters: head N: a in the trace, b counted over the A/B)the trace lost swaps the counters saw (swaps_h<N> before phase 1 and after mode 0)
trace incomplete: Q1-Q11 (debug.rdna3accel_trace NOT read by collect.sh ...)36-frame-trace.txt says a root-only read failedrun collect.sh as root
plugin trace: ...tools/plugin_trace.py's verdicts (the Metal plugin's own trace, collected by tools/macos/collect_plugin_trace.sh into the archive)INFO not in this report when the plugin loaded but its trace is missing

The kernel stream is pumped every 3 s through the whole run now (not only the A/B), into $HOME/rdna3-trace/ on the machine, the archive (36-stream-*.bin and .size), the host (<report>-trace-stream.bin, -drmtrace-stream.bin, -trace-sec.bin and their .size) and /var/db/rdna3-pending-* until sent. Reads copy 256 events at a time with the ring's lock dropped between; the stream's cursor moves only after the copyout. Content samples are read through the BAR with no HDP invalidate, as amdgpu does on Navi 32 (amdgpu_device_invalidate_hdp is a no-op on HDP 6.0); the headers say so (flag HDP_NOINV). The other buffers of a swapping surface are sampled at every swap for a head's first 600 swaps, during the A/B and at empty backs, else every 8th (debug.rdna3accel_trace_scan: 0 only empty backs, 2 always).

== frame trace: pump read errors, ... reached 536870912 bytesthe pump's failed reads and its 512 MB cap per file

Round 3 (end-to-end, kernel, security): the plugin trace's copies of one process (the A/B snapshots, the archive's final copy) are merged by (ring, seq), so the join keeps what an earlier snapshot saw and a wrap the snapshots cover is no WARN (main ring N records (k copies merged)); a lit pipe whose picture is not readable is NOT READABLE (not judged), never a PASS, A/B screens WARN NOT JUDGED / look ab1 missing although the A/B ran, and A/B main display <verdict> mode 1 vs mode 0 on the main display (pipe P, how it was found): a% vs b% not black; join lines carry the A/B phase split and the EMPTY swaps, and a back the plugin never saw although its trace covers the A/B is a WARN when it had empty swaps; backs to join without any WindowServer plugin trace are plugin trace MISSING (<reason>) - the join lines below carry no plugin verdict; the SUMMARY row swap A/B (see the table above); both "before this boot" checks allow 120 s (a clock set after the boot); the collector decides from the directory entry before it opens anything and reads the header from its own copy; autoreport makes the standalone folder with mkdir (no mv, no install -d through a link; a fresh mktemp directory otherwise), deletes plugin traces of earlier boots at each run's start, keeps every snapshot's pending copy; a posted panel move is timed before its flip is issued.

Kernel side of round 2: a panel move posted from outside the family's lock is ignored when it is older than the swaps' own last flip (it would undo that flip's record); D is freed only after a latch was SEEN (a wait that gave up does not count); leaving modes 1/2 waits for the flip to D before copying D home, and that copy feeds the SDMA breaker; the breaker retries after 10 s of the clock (not 600 swaps: a quiet desktop swaps rarely); object names are SipHash-2-4 under a per-boot key (the round 1 salt could be inverted), the headers carry a random boot id instead of the salt check; refused AMDGPU_CS are traced 64 times per error, then every 64th (v1 the count, v2 those skipped); the aperture's cached path 4 times per resource, then every 256th; shapes sample their buffers for the first 256 shapes.

Card run 2026-10-17c (step agdc + probes, build 1311b22)

  • Seen: head 0's swaps name the framebuffer resource as the front and two backs in turns: 0x8b100000 (IOSurface 37, resource type 0xc0) and 0x8b8f0000 (type 0x40, no IOSurface). The second read EMPTY at all 159 swaps that named it (16 aperture writes into it, 272 into the first); WindowServer's frame was in 0x8b100000 at 159 of 159 swaps. Mode 1 held the panel at each of them (swaps_copyempty_h0 437).
  • The trace says when (certain from the run's stream): each swap of 0x8b8f0000 came 0.2-0.3 ms (median 0.30) after the swap of 0x8b100000, which mode 1 had just copied; at none of them had 0x8b100000 (or any other buffer) changed again (2 changes of it between two swaps in the whole run, both copy forward's writes). So the empty back's swap is WindowServer's second swap of a frame the swap before presented: the hold lost no frame. Mode 0 skipped those swaps the same way.
  • Change (evidence-driven, no amdgpu analogue: macOS legacy surface semantics; common/rdna3_swap_copy.h rd3_sc_landed_wanted / rd3_sc_landed_pick, tests/test_swap_copy.c): in modes 1/2, at a swap whose back reads empty - or has no IOSurface (type 0x40) and did not change since the head last sampled it - the surface's other buffers (its 0x11C0 array minus the back and the head's framebuffer resource, plus a1 when it is neither) are sampled at that swap (at most 6, 64 BAR reads each, the same reads the trace's SCAN events use), and the one that changed since the head's last look, has pixels and is not the picture on the panel is copied instead (an IOSurface-backed one first). Without one the old rule stands (hold over a picture). In the pattern of this run it does not fire (nothing new at those swaps); it presents a frame that really sits in a buffer the swap did not name. The risk: a buffer WindowServer is drawing the next frame into also "changed" - a half-drawn frame would show. So it is OFF by default (debug.rdna3accel_swap_landed / boot-arg rdna3swaplanded, default 0): the swap keeps the old rule and only counts, traces and logs what it would have presented (swaps_copylanded_would_h<N>, SWAP v1 bit 22, would present landed buffer ...). A next report with that counter at 0 says the rule does not matter; above 0, try rdna3swaplanded=1 and watch swaps_copylanded_h<N> against the looks.
  • The plugin's trace was missing because the VM's plugin was stale (built from metal/ at 8540be5, before the plugin's frame trace, metal/plugin/NVMTLTrace.m, came with e168385): rebuild it in the VM (tools/vm/macos/build_metal.sh) before the next run.

The stale back: the side monitor flickers, windows appear and disappear (2026-10-17c)

  • Seen (certain from the run's stream, tools/rdna3_trace.py): head 1's swaps come in pairs per frame - a copy forward into B1, the swap of B1 (the frame), then 5-14 ms later the swap of B2, which WindowServer did not draw this frame. B2 (0x8d0c0000, later 0x8d8b0000) received pixels once, from a window animation that rotated three buffers, and never again. Mode 1 copied it at every frame: the panel showed the old frame for most of each frame period, and the copy into the front spread it into the next frame through copy forward. 259 such copies on head 1 by the decoder's count (130 of 0x8d0c0000, 102 of 0x8d8b0000, the rest the same pattern on other buffer sets), none on head 0; 14 A-B-A patterns on head 1's scanout samples, 0 on head 0. No GPU work of WindowServer was pending at any of those swaps (not a render race); SYNC's dirty bits are 0.
  • Change (evidence-driven, no amdgpu analogue: macOS legacy surface semantics; common/rdna3_swap_copy.h rd3_sc_stale_check / rd3_sc_stale_confirm, tests/test_swap_copy.c stale_*): mode 1 remembers per head and per buffer address the hashes of two grids of 64 samples of the back when the head last presented it (grid 0 the trace's, grid 1 shifted by half a cell) and whether a copy forward targeted it since (marked in every head's table). A back with pixels is held - no copy to the panel, none into the front, the panel keeps the newer frame
    • when its grid 0 is unchanged, no copy forward targeted it, another buffer was presented after it, the panel holds a frame mode 1 copied, and grid 1, read again then, is unchanged too (else: copied, counted as a miss). Never presented: copied. Mode 2 stays the copy-everything control (never holds), mode 0 is unchanged. debug.rdna3accel_swap_stale / boot-arg rdna3swapstale (default 1) set to 0 copies such a back as before and only counts and traces it. The records are forgotten when the surface or the head's framebuffer resource goes, at a display or swap mode change, when D is reallocated, and per address when the VRAM is freed. A held back is read again ~4 ms later (the empty back's re-sample budget): a change there is a frame that landed after the hold.
  • The decoder's bands are RGB only, the kernel's hashes include alpha: an alpha-only change reads "unchanged" to the decoder (it may count a stale copy the kernel rightly made).

Card run 2026-10-17c (the CPU-visible VRAM ran out; step agdc + probes)

  • Evidence (serial-*.log): the VM's BAR0 is 256 MiB (rdna3vm shrinks it, OVMF 64-bit PCI window: 0 MiB (BAR0 256 MiB)), so the CPU-visible heap is 144 MiB (DRM/amdgpu UAPI ready: ... 144 MiB visible). RADV's BOs the CPU may map (36992 KiB and 4096 KiB each) filled it (GEM: ... did not fit (visible heap 9568 KiB free ...): GTT); then RD3VidMemory:: allocPhysical: 2658304 / 3145728 / 8294400 bytes REFUSED by the framebuffer (0xe00002db) for WindowServer's framebuffers: the main display went black and froze (gamma "pipe 1 2 % not black", M6 FROZEN).
  • Code findings (certain from code): (1) the accelerator's allocations (rdna3AllocVram: WindowServer's framebuffers, the framebuffer kext's surfaces and cursor, the swap copy's D) and GEM BOs shared one first-fit visible heap, GEM first come first served; (2) a BO created without CPU access went beyond BAR0 first but fell back into the visible heap when that refused it (4 KiB of VRAM without CPU access did not fit (visible heap 8872 KiB free, the rest 252472 KiB free)); (3) range_alloc_get cut a free range to [s,a) before it found its free list (1024 ranges) had no slot for the tail: the failed allocation lost the tail for good - the 15.4 GiB drop of the "rest" between two log lines is that or a 15 GiB BO nobody logged; which of the two the next run says (below); (4) a 4 KiB BO with a large alignment hint needed a large aligned hole.
  • Fixed (common/rdna3_vram_place.h, tests/test_vram_place.c): BOs without CPU access never use the visible heap (amdgpu_bo_placement_from_domain); the visible heap has a reserve at its start for the accelerator's allocations (default two thirds of it, at most 128 MiB; rdna3visreserve=<MiB>, 0 = none) that GEM BOs never take; the alignment never exceeds the allocation's own power of two (amdgpu_vram_mgr_new); the free lists hold 4096 ranges, a split with no slot changes nothing, a range that cannot be given back is counted.
  • Log lines (kernel): VRAM REFUSED #n: <who>, <size> KiB by <owner> (alignment .., placed at ..): free reserve .. visible heap .. beyond BAR0 .. (largest ..); free ranges a/g/h, lost .. KiB; held: <owner> <KiB> xN (peak, refused) (first 16, then every 64th), VRAM heap: ... (at most every 30 s while allocations happen), GEM: a N MiB VRAM BO ... placed at (BOs of 1 GiB and more), VRAM: could not give N KiB .. back. Counters: debug.rdna3_vram (one line of key=value; autoreport adds it to every counter dump of the tests file, collect.sh writes 38-vram-heap.txt).

The size of that heap comes first (DRIVER-PLAN N62): the triage row VRAM aperture reads the core's VRAM aperture: line (BAR0's size, the kernel window mapped of it, the visible heap) and the bring-up's map.

WARN .. range(s) retired for gooda VRAM clear timed out (VRAM clear .. range retired): the SDMA fill may still land, so the range is never reused and no owner holds itSDMA hang: see the ring/reset lines before it
WARN .. client BO(s) found no VRAM and no GTT eithergtt_fail: VRAM refused and GTT was over budget or its allocation failed; the client got an error (GEM: a N KiB BO found no VRAM and no GTT either has the GTT usage)RADV then fails with out of memory: GTT is 8 GiB wired; look for a leak of GTT BOs
WARN log shows N refusals, newest dump says Mthe dump was read before the run endedthe log lines are the later truth

Step bisect / bisect-plugin (the display bug: black screens, windows that flash, flicker)

The owner runs rdna3vm step bisect ([TESTING-VM](TESTING-VM.html), "Display bisect"): instead of the other tests every new feature with a runtime switch is turned off (step 1), then on again one at a time, oldest first, 30 s each (steps 2-6; step 6 = the defaults = step 0, since 2026-10-17d swap_mode 0 and trace_scan 0), five controls that are off by default (7-11: swap mode 1, trace_scan 1, mode 1 with swap_landed, mode 2, a3ws) and the defaults again (12). The report has the table (39-bisect.txt in the archive, <report>-bisect.txt uploaded after every step, the looks as <report>-scanoutbisect<N>[a]-pipe<P>.ppm), the tests file only the step headers. tools/triage_reports.py prints it (tools/triage_bisect.py): step, what changed, the owner's answer (tty only; - on the timer), pipe 0 / 1 % not black, whether the picture moved between the step's two looks (move / still), the share of pipe 0 that is the grey (170,170,170) placeholder (grey0), the step's verdict (right, right,slow, BROKEN, ? = not judged) and the deltas of the swaps per head, the copies, the buffers rewritten in place and the empty backs since the step before. A step is judged on its own (see "Card run 2026-10-18b: the bisect judgement" below); the owner's y / n at the tty overrides the rules.

Triage lineMeaningAction
FAIL BISECT: BREAKS AT step K (<switch>)step 1 (everything off) was fine, step 0 broken, and step K is the first that switches a feature back on and brokethe switch of step K is the suspect (the steps are cumulative: everything before K is on too): a3 (2) = A3 (accel/rdna3-accel.cpp import rule, N27), flicker (3) / swap_torn (5) = the BAR reads of the diagnostics (cost per swap: swaps_hashed_h<N>; a budget of 3000 / 20000 per head is used up early, then the switch does nothing), swap_latch (4) = the wait under the family's lock (N28 open item 2), swap_mode (6) = the copy (N29), trace_scan (7) = more BAR reads, swap_stale (8) = the hold (N31). bisect-plugin (steps 0-4) does the same with rdna3mtl.conf (NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_SHARE, NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_PAGEOFF, NVMTL_TRACE) and the allow list's %apps line, restarting WindowServer at steps 1, 2 and 4; its autologin: line says whether the guest logs in by itself. The trace stream is pumped all through the run: stream_bytes / trace_events per step place a step in the decoded trace (tools/rdna3_trace.py), the steps' wall clock is in at:. The swap A/B is not part of this step (the triage does not expect it).

Step bisect-agdc (the same bisect on the agdc profile: WindowServer on the GPU)

The plain bisect boots the default profile (AGDC idle, WindowServer on the CPU), so its result does not hold for the real desktop; card run 2026-10-17f (rdna3vm step play, swap mode 0) is broken again. rdna3vm step bisect-agdc ([TESTING-VM](TESTING-VM.html), "Display bisect on the agdc profile") has the agdc step's boot-args plus rdna3bisect=4 and 13 steps of 30 s that are NOT cumulative (each sets its whole combination from the boot's values; since the write generations, below): 0 the defaults (mode 1, swap_gen 1, flipbuf_present 0), 1 mode 0, 2 mode 1 + swap_gen 0, 3 mode 0 + swap_gen 0, 4 mode 1 + flipbuf_present 1, 5 mode 0 + flipbuf_present 1, 6 mode 2, 7 mode 1 + iop 0, 8 mode 1 + a3 0, 9 mode 1 + swap_latch 0, 10 mode 1 + swap_stale 0, 11 mode 0 + iop_flip toggled, 12 the defaults again. Same 39-bisect.txt / <report>-bisect.txt as bisect (variant: 4 (bisect-agdc)), plus at each step's start a -- before counter block, trace_events_start, stream_bytes_start, and one trace: events W..W (asked by sysctl debug.rdna3accel_trace_dump) line in the kernel log.

Card run 2026-10-18b: the bisect judgement

The owner's reading of the bisect-agdc run: steps 1, 3, 5, 11 (swap_mode 0, the flip) work but are slow, every other step (mode 1 and 2, the defaults included) is broken. The first judgement ("a pipe that stood still although it moved in another step is broken") contradicted that on 10 of 13 steps: pipe 1 (the idle second monitor) only "moves" in the broken copy steps (stale buffers alternate), so the good mode 0 steps looked "stood still"; and the flip of the picture counted as "bytes changed". tools/triage_bisect.py judge() now judges every step on its own, on pipe 0 (pipe 1 never decides):

  • R1 (the content signal): 4 % or more of pipe 0's pixels are exactly (170,170,170) in either look (that run: right steps at most 2.8 %, broken ones at least 4.9 %; mode 0's flip shows no placeholder).
  • R2: pipe 0's two looks are the same (PPM difference and both bytes changed under 0.3 %) while the step ran 60 or more head-0 swaps: the compositor swaps and the screen shows nothing new (step 6, mode 2).
  • R3 (counters only): half or more of the head-0 swaps copied an empty back (swaps_copyempty_h0).
  • RIGHT otherwise; right,slow when swaps_latchwait_h0 is half of swaps_h0 or more and swaps_latchus_h0 / swaps_latchwait_h0 is 10 ms or more. No PPMs and no R3: not judged, never right.
  • A lit pipe at most 3 % not black is broken too (BLACK_PCT); the tty answer overrides the rules; --owner / owner.txt compare (AGREES / DISAGREES, a FAIL).

On that run the tool gives right,slow 1, 3, 5, 11 and broken 0, 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12 (13 of 13 as the owner). The limits come from one run: if a later run's good step has a grey share near 4 % or a broken one has none, DISAGREES shows it and the constants (GREY_FRACTION, STILL_MIN_SWAPS, EMPTY_FRACTION) are the place to look. tests/test_triage_bisect.sh scenario agdcowner reproduces the run (13 steps, the real counter deltas and grey shares) with the boundaries (3.9 / 4.1 % grey, 59 / 60 swaps, no PPMs, pipe 1 flicker alone).

Card run 2026-10-17d: the bisect

  • Evidence (rdna3vm step bisect, the owner's report): step 5 (every switch since the display bug on, but debug.rdna3accel_swap_mode 0 - the flip - and trace_scan 0) was perfect on both monitors. Step 6 switched only swap_mode to 1 (copy into the accelerator's scanout buffers, the default since 2026-10-17b) and broke both monitors. Certain from the report: mode 1 is the display bug. Why it breaks is unknown (DRIVER-PLAN N34).
  • Done: mode 0 (the flip) is the default again (RD3_SWAPMODE_DEFAULT in common/rdna3_swap_copy.h, commit 8c8fcf7) and trace_scan is 0 by default (gTrScan in accel/rdna3-accel.cpp, commit e8d8fda; the sysctl turns it on). The owner confirms the picture is right on both monitors. Modes 1 and 2 stay for comparison: boot-arg rdna3swapmode=1 / 2 or sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_swap_mode=1. Superseded by the write generations (the last section of this file): mode 1 is the default again.
  • The tools follow: (1) the swap A/B is opt-in (boot-arg rdna3swapab=1); a normal step runs none and triage does not fail it; (2) the bisect's steps 6-11 are now: 6 swap_stale (= the defaults = step 0), 7 control mode 1, 8 control trace_scan, 9 control mode 1 + swap_landed, 10 control mode 2, 11 control a3ws, 12 the defaults; triage_bisect.py expects swap_mode 0 and trace_scan 0 at step 0; (3) the copy counters and H-STALE are not judged in mode 0: INFO swap mode 0 ... the copy counters below and H-STALE are not judged and INFO H-STALE: head N: M swaps, all mode 0 (flip): not judged; (4) H-COPY (in-place rewrites in mode 0) is a WARN now, not a FAIL: its old advice "the copy (mode 1) is the fix" is what the bisect disproved, judge the looks; (5) coverage Q2 (samples of the surfaces' other buffers) accepts none when the report's counters say trace_scan 0 (autoreport and collect.sh now dump debug.rdna3accel_trace_scan; a report without that counter still FAILs Q2 on a run with no samples).
  • Open: window dragging is not smooth and Activity Monitor shows GPU 0 % in mode 0 (DRIVER-PLAN N35). Note that rdna3vm args "rdna3bisect=3" --stay has no agdc profile (the AGDC is idle, WindowServer does no GPU work, the idle GFXCLK cap is never lifted): to drive the desktop by hand and still get a report use rdna3vm step play ([TESTING-VM](TESTING-VM.html), "Play").

Reports 2026-10-17d/f/g: the write generations and the flip-present hooks

  • Evidence (17d's trace, 17f/g's serial logs): on the agdc profile WindowServer composites on the CPU through the family's aperture into buf[1] of each display surface, every frame (17d: 0x88850000 441 page-ons, 0x89040000 none): page-on b1 about 13 ms after a swap, copy forward b0 -> b1 about 1.9 ms before the next, submitSwapCopy(b0, b1) and 0.36 ms later submitSwapCopy(b0, b2) of a b2 it never draws. Mode 0 (the flip) puts b1 on the panel and WindowServer writes the next frame into it (17f: "rewritten in place", "A LIVE WRITER IS PAINTING THE SCANNED-OUT SURFACE"). Copy forward bursts rotate the three buffers at about 60 Hz with no swap (17d: 688.51-688.72 s, 803.1-806.6 s): the family presents those frames by another path; a burst leaves an old frame in b2, which mode 0 flipped to and mode 1 copied over the panel.
  • Done: the write generations (common/rdna3_write_gen.h, tests/test_write_gen.c). A page-on or a copy forward into a buffer stamps it; a swap presents its back only when it was written after the head's frame, otherwise it holds (no flip, no copy: the panel keeps its frame). Copy forward copies from a front written after that frame. Swap mode 1 (the copy) is the default again: the only mode in which the panel never shows a buffer WindowServer writes. The flip-present hooks trace (and with debug.rdna3accel_flipbuf_present=1 present) the family's submitFlipBuffer / submitScanoutFlipBuffer, and trace pickPresentType, didSubmitSwap, isBackBufferReady, submitUpdate; all of them always run the family's own afterwards.
  • Switches: debug.rdna3accel_swap_gen / boot-arg rdna3swapgen (1, 0 = only counted: swaps_genhold_would_h<N>), debug.rdna3accel_flipbuf_present / rdna3flipbufpresent (0), debug.rdna3accel_swap_mode / rdna3swapmode (1, 0 = the flip of 17d..g). The bisect-agdc plan (13 steps) compares them.
  • Log lines: swap #N: head H: the back (a2) 0x... held: never written (no page-on, no copy forward into it) and never presented on this head (generation G, the head's G2) -- no flip, no copy, the panel keeps its frame; ... presented: written after the head's last frame (...) (the first 8 of each head, then every 4096th); swap #N: head H: the back ... was NOT written since this head presented it ... but its samples changed -- presented: a writer the accelerator does not see; write generations: head H reset (a display mode change); write generations: the table is full (32 buffers) ...; copy forward #N: head H: the front (phys ..., generation G) was written after the head's frame (generation G2) -- copied from it instead of ...; hook: IOAccelLegacyDisplayPipe::submitFlipBuffer(event ..., N, res ... (phys ...), res ... (phys ...)) on head H (...; family lock ...; flipbuf_present 0: traced) -- the family's own runs next (the first 16 of each hook); hook: IOAccelLegacySurface::isBackBufferReady(N) -> 0x1 (ready) (surface ..., head H; call K); flipbuf #N: head H: ...'s buffer (phys ...) NOT presented: ...; flipbuf: head H: ... NOT presented: the family's lock is held by another thread ....
  • ECC review round 1 (the guards and the counters that make a decision provable from the report):
    • A hold never freezes the panel: after 250 ms without a present on the head and 3 holds in a row the back presents (the age guard, swaps_gen_aged_h<N>); a back with no generation while a newer one was evicted from the full table presents (the eviction guard, swaps_gen_evictpresent_h<N>). The 17c/17d traces had no hold run that the age guard would have cut (the longest: 5 swaps).
    • Swap mode 1, before a hold: the surface's buffer written after the head's frame (the newest, with pixels) is presented instead (swaps_gen_landed_h<N>; the trace marks it as a landed buffer).
    • Every judged swap is counted: swaps_gen_judged_h<N> = swaps_genpresent
      • swaps_genhold + swaps_genhold_would + swaps_gen_landed (per head); the swaps the rule could not judge (no head known at the swap's entry, no VRAM address) are swaps_gennotjudged_h<N>. Head resets per head: swaps_gen_resets_h<N> (another surface taking the head over resets it only after the last one was quiet for 500 ms; surfaces taking turns keep the record).
    • Stamps: swaps_gen_apcached (the page-on's cached descriptor, apart from swaps_gen_aperture), swaps_gen_cfnostamp (a copy forward that failed or did not copy every rect keeps its target's generation). The head's frame takes the generation captured at the decision.
    • Hooks: nothing runs at interrupt level or with interrupts disabled but a count (hook_intr with hook_intr_<kind>, flipbuf_intoff, hook_intoff); a resource argument below the kernel's addresses is not cast (flipbuf_badptr); flipbuf_nores, flipbuf_busy_h<N>, flipbuf_unlocked_h<N> (per head now), flipbuf_emptyheld_h<N> (of flipbuf_held_h<N>, the buffer read empty).
    • tools/kext_check.sh compiles the accelerator to assembly and tools/check_accel_slots.py checks every rd3Base* call jumps through the measured slot (2512 2504 2672 2640 2648 2664; the lock overrides 2144 2152).
    • New log lines: flip-present hooks installed: RD3DisplayPipe submitFlipBuffer vtable+2496, ... (at the accelerator's start; the base calls' slots are these + 16); swap #N: head H: ... presented by a guard: the AGE GUARD ... / the EVICTION GUARD ...; swap #N: head H: the back ... would be held (...) -- the surface's buffer 0x... written after the head's frame (...) presented instead; swap #N: head H: the write generations did not judge this swap: ...; write generations: head H reset (another surface swaps on the head, the last one quiet for 500 ms); copy forward #N: the target 0x... keeps its write generation: ...; flipbuf #N: head H: ...'s buffer (phys ...) read empty -- held; flipbuf: head H: ... NOT presented: neither buffer is a VRAM resource of ours ...; hook: ... was called at INTERRUPT LEVEL / with INTERRUPTS DISABLED (the first of each kind, logged by the next call); the first 16 hook: lines say interrupts enabled.
    • triage_reports.py --all lists the first kernel-log lines of each kind (write generations:, hook:, flip-present hooks, flipbuf, held:, did not judge).

Card run 2026-10-18b: slow window movement in mode 0 (the same-buffer flip)

  • Found in the trace: in swap mode 0, 628 of 655 flips (96 %) went to the address the panel already scanned (WindowServer draws into that one buffer every frame, swaps_inplace_h0 284 of 291). Each still waited for the flip latch (median 14.8 ms, inside the swap, under the family's lock), and while it waited WindowServer made no submissions; it flips about 2.5 ms after a vblank, so frames came at every 3rd vblank (about 20 fps; mode 1 about 56).
  • Done (rd3_latch_same_shown in common/rdna3_flip_latch.h, tests/test_flip_latch.c): a mode 0 swap whose back is the buffer the head scans, its flip seen latched (or the HUBP's in-use address is that buffer with no flip pending), is not flipped again and not waited for; the write generations, copy forward's record and the trace are kept. A flip to it still pending, another buffer or nothing recorded: flipped and waited for as before. debug.rdna3accel_swap_samenoflip / boot-arg rdna3swapsamenoflip (default 1; 0 = the old behaviour).

DRIVER-PLAN N38: presents without blocking WindowServer (the present queue)

  • What changed (not yet on the card): a mode 0 swap no longer sleeps for its flip's latch. Each head has one flip in flight; a present while it is pending waits in one slot (a newer one replaces it: newest wins) and the head's worker programs it at the latch (common/rdna3_flip_queue.h, amdgpu's dm_pflip_high_irq model). The legacy swap's own event cannot be completed later (the family's), so the writers the driver sees - copy forward's target and the family's page-on - wait for the latch only when they target the buffer still leaving the panel. Flip-buffer presents (flipbuf_present 1) and IOP transactions (iop_flip 1) use the same queue; an IOP transaction completes once its flip latched, and its zero-copy flip needs the plugin's dirty mark of that VRAM. The core's GPU activity goes into the accelerator's PerformanceStatistics (Activity Monitor's GPU column).
  • Knobs (each logged at boot: swap async:, iop queue: ...; iop wrote:, perfstats:): debug.rdna3accel_swap_async / rdna3swapasync (1; 0 = the old latch wait), _iop_queue / rdna3iopqueue (1), _iop_wrote / rdna3iopwrote (1), _perfstats / rdna3perfstats (1). All four change at runtime: the A/B is sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_swap_async=0 and back.
  • What the card must show: window movement smooth in mode 0 (the 18b trace's 20 fps gone), swaps_pq_prog_h<N> + _queued close to the head's flipped swaps, swaps_pq_early_h<N> and _dropped 0, swaps_pq_wait_h<N> small (only when WindowServer reuses the buffer leaving the panel within a frame), the triage present queue lines and H-PRESENT without FAIL, Activity Monitor's GPU % above 0 while windows move (perf_writes > 0). With the N42 interrupts live, H-PRESENT adds N latch(es) ended by the pflip interrupt (avg X us after it) and the timeline's latched ... (by the pflip interrupt, X us after it): close to the latches seen; absent while the latches are only polled (rdna3vblirq=0, an older framebuffer, swaps_pq_irqdone_h<N> 0).

DRIVER-PLAN N41: VRAM parking and WindowServer's pending GPU writes

  • What changed (not yet on the card): the family's deallocPhysical no longer frees a surface's VRAM; it is parked and the next allocPhysical hands the same VRAM back, so the family, its dirty mark and every page-off stay on the allocation the Metal plugin draws into (traces 17d / 17c: imported surfaces moved to never-written VRAM 13 / 19 times: grey, stale or flashing windows, black captures). A dirty mark of a parked surface makes it live again first. free() gives back what is parked and what the family left live. WindowServer's plugin marks the surfaces a command buffer writes pending at commit and ready after the GPU; a CPU lock's page-off (a window capture) waits for them, bounded (100 ms).
  • Knobs (each logged at boot: vm sticky:, surface wait:): debug.rdna3accel_vm_sticky / rdna3vmsticky (1; 0 = every dealloc frees, the old behaviour), debug.rdna3accel_surface_wait / rdna3surfwait (1), _surface_wait_ms (100); plugin env NVMTL_SURFACE_WAIT=0. All change at runtime: the A/B is sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_vm_sticky=0 and back. With rdna3a3ws=0 (the default) the boot line says MOOT: WindowServer's surfaces stay in system memory and nothing is marked.
  • What the card must show: no grey / stale / flashing windows, captures not black; triage VRAM parking INFO with vm_parked close to vm_unparked, no H-VMMOVE; surface wait INFO with rdna3a3ws=1 (MOOT without it, see N41 open item 1). The N41 WARN / FAIL rows (and MOOT) are also in the SUMMARY. WindowServer's plugin lines are in 24-log-coredisplay.txt (surface wait (N41): decision, stats every 4096 command buffers).

Card runs 2026-10-17d / 18b: buffer texture pitch and the UCLK floor (DRIVER-PLAN N43)

  • Found in the plugin trace of both runs: alloc-fail vkCreateImage(buffer texture) rc=-1000158000 (VK_ERROR_INVALID_DRM_FORMAT_MODIFIER_PLANE_LAYOUT_EXT), about 32 a second from WindowServer. The caller (newTextureWithDescriptor: offset:bytesPerRow: on a buffer) builds a linear DRM-modifier image over the buffer with the app's bytesPerRow as the explicit row pitch. RADV takes a custom linear pitch only as a multiple of 256 B (ac_surface_override_offset_stride, ac_surface_get_pitch_align: 256 / bytes per element pixels on GFX9+; the GFX10.3+ image descriptor takes the pitch in 256 B units, a hardware rule). Every alias that worked had a pitch % 256 == 0, every failure had not. The plugin only checked % 32 (NVIDIA's unit from the NVK port). The private copy (task 19) then drew the texture, at the cost of a new image and a synchronous GPU upload per use.
  • Done (metal/plugin/nvmtl_linear_pitch.h, tests/test_linear_pitch.c): with the DRM-modifier path (g_linmod) the alias is refused before vkCreateImage when bytesPerRow is not a multiple of 256 B, so alloc-fail no longer fires for it; the caller makes the private copy as before. The IOSurface alias (item 7: ... stays private: bytesPerRow is not a multiple of 256 (RADV linear pitch)) uses the same rule. Counted and logged (first 8, then every 1024th): buffer texture copy (pitch): bytesPerRow N is not a multiple of 256 B (RADV linear custom pitch) - WxH VkFormat F, a private copy instead of an alias (K so far), and the buffer texture census line ends ..., K copy (pitch). rdna3_vktest checks the rule on the card (LINPITCH: line: an explicit linear row pitch of 64 B must give -1000158000, 256 B must succeed). Open (needs the card): park the copy (keep its image and refresh it) instead of a new image + upload per use; every copy still costs a GPU upload per command buffer that uses it.
SeenMeaningAction
triage INFO buffer texture copy (pitch): at least N buffer texture(s) over a bytesPerRow that is not a multiple of 256 B were not aliased ...the plugin counted them (the lower bound: the log has the first 8 and every 1024th, the census line the running count)none; the cost is the open item above. N in the thousands per minute with WindowServer slow: do the copy parking

UCLK at its idle floor: every report so far read UCLK 96 MHz (level 0 of pp_dpm_mclk: 96 / 456 / 772 / 1218 MHz, UCLK DPM on, Linux idles at 96 too) and umc activity 0-2 %, with no memory load ever measured. A report of a step with rdna3pm=2 and RADV now carries a section == rdna3 memory load (rdna3_vktest --membw, rdna3_pmctl info x3 during it): ten seconds of vkCmdFillBuffer + vkCmdCopyBuffer between two device-local buffers (1 GiB each, less when VRAM is tight), rdna3_pmctl info (pp_dpm_mclk / sclk / fclk and the activity line) three times during it and once 4 s after. About 25 s; every other step prints one == memory load: skipped (...) line.

Kexts kmutil refuses, thread call stops (macOS 26, card run 2026-10-25b, DRIVER-PLAN N47)

On macOS 26 the framebuffer, the accelerator and the AGDC go into the Auxiliary KC through kmutil, which binds every import only to a library the kext's OSBundleLibraries declares and never to com.apple.kpi.private; the core is injected by OpenCore, whose prelinker binds kernel symbols kmutil would refuse. CI's KPI guard step runs tools/ci/kmutil_kpi_guard.py on every build: kmutil libraries + nm -u on the runner (macOS 15), and every import against xnu-12377.101.15's (macOS 26.4) export lists in tools/ci/data/ (what the runner's kmutil cannot see); make test scans the kext sources against the same lists. The kexts stop their thread calls with common/rdna3_tcall.h (public KPIs; thread_call_cancel_wait is private on 26), which, like thread_call_cancel_wait, waits for the last run however long it takes and logs RDNA3-tcall: <call>: ... when a stop waits long.

SeenMeaningAction
only RDNA3Accel and RDNA3AGDC loaded; the install log or kmutil log says Failed to bind '_<symbol>' ... could not find a kext which exports this symbolthat kext imports a symbol its declared libraries do not export on this macOS (26: kpi.private only)python3 tools/ci/kmutil_kpi_guard.py /Library/Extensions/<kext>.kext on the VM (read-only; it runs kmutil libraries -p ... --undef-symbols): it names the symbol and the libraries; replace a private one with public KPIs, declare the library (com.apple.kpi.unsupported for kauth_cred_issuser); if the lists in tools/ci/data/ missed it, the VM's macOS is newer than them: regenerate (tools/ci/gen_xnu_kpi_lists.py --tag <xnu of uname -v>)

Activity Monitor's GPU numbers (card runs 2026-10-25c..e, DRIVER-PLAN N49)

Activity Monitor's GPU History reads the accelerator's PerformanceStatistics / Device Utilization %; its per-process % GPU / GPU Time columns sum the accumulatedGPUTime of the IOAccelSubmitter2 entries (contexts, command queues) under the accelerator per IOUserClientCreator pid. Since N49 the utilisation is the kernel rings' busy time (GFX, compute, SDMA; the SMU's GFX activity reads 100 % at idle, P2) and each process's GPU time is added to its first IOAccelSubmitter2. ioreg -l -r -c RDNA3Accelerator | grep -E 'Utilization|accumulatedGPUTime|IOUserClientCreator' shows both on the card.

The screen is slow although apps draw fast (DRIVER-PLAN N61)

Symptom: animations and scrolling are smooth in the app but jerky on the panel (card run 2026-10-28b: the frame-order page drew ~59 frames a second, the panel showed 4.4). Every frame WindowServer presents passes three stages: its composite into a private image (GPU), the copy-out of that image into the IOSurface (plugin), the display pipe's copy of the IOSurface into the scanout (kernel, rdna3acceliop=1, iop_flip 0). Read, in this order:

Row / lineMeansAction
frame order: ... displayed X fps of the page's Y fps (Z %)how many distinct frames reached the scanout against those the page drew, per burstthe number to raise; 100 % is the goal at 60 Hz

plugin commit: N70 G-SUB <n> empty LAST parts retired by the ahead pass (the pass went on to the next buffer) / ... <n> ahead pass(es) stopped at an empty LAST part (NVMTL_GSUB_LAST_EMPTY=0) / ... stopped at an empty part (NVMTL_EVFOLD=0) / start line commit: N70 G-SUB an empty LAST part is retired ... | stops the ahead pass ...N70: the G-SUB submit-ahead pass handles the empty last part an event split leaves in a command buffer. retired growing with the commits = working: the pass went on to the next buffer instead of stopping, so later buffers are submitted ahead (expect most submits through nvmtl_gsub_finish, less fence-wait time, fewer execution-thread CPU seconds). stopped at an empty LAST part = the switch is 0 (the old behaviour: every later buffer submit + wait in turn); stopped at an empty part (NVMTL_EVFOLD=0) = event folding is off, so nothing runs ahead of a signal. Neither stopped line should appear in a default runnone by default. A/B: NVMTL_GSUB_LAST_EMPTY=0 (plugin env) restores the old stop; if frames are stale or missing only with the default, the empty-last retire is the suspect (DRIVER-PLAN N70). fps unchanged is expected when WindowServer's upload caps the game

What a WindowServer frame costs (DRIVER-PLAN N64)

Card run 2026-10-29a: the scanout showed 11.3 fps of the 60 fps page, but WindowServer's plugin trace (tools/plugin_trace.py --rates on 31-plugin-trace/WindowServer-*.trace) shows 122 command buffers a second (two per frame: 61 fps) in the seconds before and after the frame-order bursts and 20-36 during them: the strip probe (rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip, one ~2 ms ioctl after another, each under the kernel's display lock) slowed what it measured. From N64 the probe sleeps after each round as long as the round took (# pacing: line in the burst file). Compare the trace's per-second cb_commit column with the burst times (# frameorder <burst> <time> in 41-ladder-frameorder-*.log; the trace's second 0 is its opened time, UTC) before blaming the driver for a low displayed rate.

Row / lineMeansAction

triage ws frame: WindowServer[pid]: ws frame: avg X ms (encode E, GPU G, fbfetch barriers B/frame, uploads U MB/frame) | N frame(s) ... in S active second(s) = R/s, busiest second P; ... (plugin vk: ws frame: SUMMARY frames= active_s= fps_peak= passes= draws= fbf_draws= bar_img= bar_mem= rec_us_avg= rec_us_max= gpu_n= gpu_us_avg= gpu_us_max= wait_us_avg= ups= up_kb= up_us_avg= membar=, metal/plugin/nvmtl_ws_frame.h)per process, newest SUMMARY: a "frame" is a command buffer that began a render pass (WindowServer commits ~2 per displayed frame). encode = the command buffer's begin to its submit (WindowServer's encoding + the plugin's recording + surface-in uploads done meanwhile), GPU = its own TOP/BOTTOM timestamps, busiest second = most frames in one second (122 = 61 fps of two)encode over 8 ms (WARN): the CPU side (the trace's encode-time split); GPU over 8 ms (WARN): the GPU side (the barriers below, the uploads); GPU UNKNOWN: no calibrated timestamps

Presents at 60 Hz but a dragged window steps unevenly (DRIVER-PLAN N68)

Symptom: bare metal 2026-10-31 (two heads, the Metal plugin, WindowServer presents l10r): iop_ok_h<N> rises at 60/s and the panel's CRC changes every refresh, yet a window dragged fast advances by steps like 10 1 8 10 4 8 11 0 21 9 44 per refresh (240 fps video), seen as ~30 fps. The display pipe copied every frame straight into the LIVE scanout at any scan position and the transaction completed at once. Read during a fast drag, before and after sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_iop_latch=1 (each with a 240 fps video); python3 tools/triage_present_cost.py sysctl.txt prints the rows from a stick report's sysctl.txt:

Row / lineMeansAction
triage IOP timing: head H: N transaction(s), avg A ms, longest L ms, S over 8 ms ..., N59 stale wait W ms a transaction (stale_wait_ms V); C copies placed in the scan: X in the SAME refresh as the one before, Y refresh(es) with NO copy, M direct copies ending mid-scan (torn); scan quarters q1/q2/q3/q4 blank B (debug.rdna3accel_iop_txn_{n,us,max_us,slow,stale_us}_h<N>, iop_vbl_{n,same,skip,mid,bin0..3,blank}_h<N>)K1: the transaction's time on WindowServer's thread from entry to exit; K2: the head's OTG frame / scanline when the copy ended. SAME = two copies in one refresh (one was never on the panel), NO copy = the panel repeated a frame; with both near 0 every refresh got exactly one new frameWARN uneven steps with the latch off and SAME + skip much smaller with it on = the cause and the fix; large stale wait = the N59 gate holds the thread (sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale_wait_ms=0 live, compare)
kernel iop txn: head H: a transaction held WindowServer's thread U us (over 8000 us; K such on this head): gates G us (of them the N59 stale wait S us, debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale_wait_ms V), copy C us, latch wait W us; <path>, surface 0x..one slow transaction split (first 8 per head, then every 256th)the biggest share is what to cut: S the stale gate, C the copy (N66), W the latched copy's wait for the flip in flight

The mailbox gives each head a third 8 MiB buffer: a frame goes into a buffer neither scanned, in flight nor queued, is queued behind the flip in flight (the newest replaces the queued one, which never reaches the panel) and the transaction completes at the copy. Fast producer: mbox_h<N> = latched frames, replaced_h<N> large, iop_latch_wait_h<N> ~0, swaps_pq_latched_h<N> <= 60/s per head, no new mid-scan copies (IOP timing)the game's fps back near the latch-off figure (250-400) with no torn drag: done. with two buffers / refused the third buffer WARN: the visible heap had no 8 MiB (debug.rdna3_vram; a 1 GiB BAR has room) - frames are paced as before; still waited for N latch(es): flips of unknown target (mode sets, the framebuffer's own) - the busy lines; A/B: sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_iop_latch_mbox=0 live = the two-buffer cap back at once, =1 gone; any torn or stale frame with it on: =0 and take a report
